# CLAUDE.md

This file provides guidance to Claude Code (claude.ai/code) when working with code in this repository.

## Project status

HexHero is an Unreal Engine 5.8 C++ project. It started from the top-down template, whose code and content have been removed and remain in Git history. The hex world map and its camera exist; all other game systems are still to come.

## Game concept

A prototype for exploring UE and the developer's own skills, so favour learning and working results over production-scale systems.

- Turn-based strategy in the spirit of Heroes 3, with the developer's own changes.
- The world is the hex planet; it is no longer only a prototype. It is up to about 256 x 256 hexes (frequency 80). Mountains and other blockers make it a maze rather than open fields, so players are kept apart and must find a way.
- Resources, seven of them (the developer's document "Ресурси та шахти", https://claude.ai/artifact/W3L2KWhi2acH45sYd83RX7, read with the Claude Docs tools, with the answers in it of 2026-11-11): gold, the basic wood and ore, and the rare mercury, sulfur, crystals and gems. Each has a mine, which yields it every day once captured: the gold mine 1000, the basic ones 2, the rare ones 1. A mine has usually no guard, and then a hero beside it takes it by going onto its cell, where it stands at a place the mine leaves free and guards the mine while it stays (2026-11-11: until there were battles nobody took a mine a hero stands on; since 2026-11-12 a hero of another player fights it there); nothing walks through that cell. A guard of creatures, once there are battles, must be beaten first. Any resource may lie as a pile, taken once by the hero whose way ends on it, not from beside, which is only for mines, as they cannot be walked through, and not by a hero that only runs over it on its way further (2026-11-11). A mine that is taken gives what it gives in a day at once, if nobody took that this week (2026-11-11). A mill has a resource of its own and gives 3 days of the income of its mine every week. A player starts with 2 days of a gold mine, 4 of the basic ones and 2 of the rare ones, for now. The developer places the mines in the palette or has them scattered over the planet (Generate of the Mines tab, 2026-11-11). A mine changes hands: any hero that visits it takes it for its player, and a player the game moves wants a mine it lost back. Later, with castles: the town hall gives gold, 500 at first, then 1000 and 4000 as the castle grows, and the market trades resources at a rate that improves with the number of markets (a formula is proposed in that document, not accepted yet). Abandoned mines and looted wagons give nothing.
- Castles and factions are described in the developer's document "Опис замків: Замок та Некрополіс" (https://claude.ai/artifact/Tca27YHCGjZjJPyic71iN6): two factions, each with a town, seven levels of creatures in a basic and an upgraded version and two classes of heroes. As that document says itself, it repeats two factions of Heroes III one to one, so before any of it is built the creatures, buildings and heroes need names and changes of the game's own; its open questions (the numbers of creatures, the buildings, the screen of a town) are not answered. Nothing of it is built.
- Heroes gain experience by winning battles and from chests, which offer a choice of gold or experience.
- A hero is a leader who walks the map, gathers resources, visits buildings and leads an army into battle, as the developer described on 2026-11-10. It does not fight itself, as it is no unit on the battlefield, but sets the strength of its army and casts spells. Its attributes: Attack and Defense, added to those of every unit of its army, Spell Power, the strength and length of its spells, and Knowledge, its mana (10 a point). A new level raises one of the four by one, by chances of the class of the hero (a warrior mostly Attack and Defense, a mage Spell Power and Knowledge), and offers a choice of two secondary skills, a new one or one a level higher (Basic, Advanced, Expert; at most 8 skills). It learns spells in the mage guilds of castles (levels 1 to 5), from scrolls and at shrines on the map.
- A hero has points of movement every day, spent by the ground it crosses (a road the cheapest, swamp and snow the dearest), which skills (Pathfinding, Logistics) and artifacts change. Some objects add to them for the next turn or the week. There are several heroes. The game is started with P, beside B for editing, and only with a hero on the planet; a hero is always chosen while it is played, as a player without a hero has lost. A left click chooses a hero, a left click on a cell shows its way there, a second click on that cell or Space sends it, and when its steps run out the next hero is chosen (the developer's rules of 2026-11-11, which replaced the right click that sent a hero).
- An army has 7 slots for stacks of creatures. Each castle (faction) has 7 levels of creatures, most with a basic and an upgraded version. A creature has Attack and Defense, to which those of the hero are added, a range of damage (a stack deals that of one creature times its count), health, Speed (the order of turns in a battle and how far it moves on the field), Morale (good: a chance of another turn in a round; bad: a chance to lose one; neutral creatures and the undead have none) and Luck (a chance of double damage).
- A battle is fought in rounds, in which the stacks act from the fastest to the slowest whoever owns them, the attacker first at equal speed. The developer will describe battles and castles further.
- The map contains castles, mines, chests, hostile units and heroes.
- A castle is either neutral or owned by the player who captured it. As the developer described castles on 2026-11-11: a castle is taken by a hero, has buildings that are built and upgraded, a dwelling that was bought offers its creatures to hire from the start of every week, creatures stack and cost their price to go into the army of a hero or the garrison of the castle, and what is built in a castle shows on it from outside, each building in its own way. A castle takes three hexes, is nearly round, and stands on the ground of its hexes, its wall and its buildings following that ground, with no foundation under it; its flags and the roofs of its hall are in the color of its player, its other roofs clay tiles in their own color, and its stone is the masonry of its faction, one of men and one of the undead. A hero that goes into a castle of its player guards it, a second one swaps places with it, and going in and out costs no movement. A castle is chosen by a click on it, with the hero that guards it, as the developer proposed on 2026-11-11 when telling the two apart by where a click landed was not clear: an order then takes the hero out and leaves it chosen alone, and two clicks, or the key that sends a hero while no way is shown, open the panel of the castle, as a hero going in does; a castle chosen has a ring around its walls, as a hero has, and the castles are listed with the heroes. The cursor on a castle outlines its three hexes as one. A hero sent out of its castle onto a hero beside it swaps places with one of its own, and with one of another player that is the attack on the castle, the hero staying in it. A castle opens the fog of war for its owner three times as far as a hero. The panel stands right of the middle of the screen with the castle left of it (they swapped sides on 2026-11-11), the camera coming close; anything done outside the panel closes it, and under it nothing is picked. A castle is mighty: its walls are about as high as a rider, its towers and gates higher, and the hero in it stands in an open yard in its middle, where it shows. It has three gates, and a hero goes in and out by the nearest, whose leaves swing open for it and shut behind it; a castle is alive, with flags that wave and fires that burn. A castle builds one building a turn, which a cheat lifts. A guarded castle is fought for in a battle since 2026-11-12; until then it was taken by weighing the armies, the stronger winning.
- Units are hostile to everyone, except that a unit a player placed on the map is friendly to that player, who can take it back into their army.
- Attacking a unit or hero opens a separate small world of about 15x11 hexes. It is not generated: it is a hex grid laid over an authored 3D environment, where decor can be added to style a map. There the attacking hero's units fight the units from the world map. Built on 2026-11-12, as the developer chose its first build ("everything at once"): see the battles below; the environment is for now a field the code lays out by the biome fought on, with obstacles and decor of the meshes of the planet.
- An underground: entrances on the planet join in networks underground, and a hero who enters goes to it through a fade to black. It is a maze of passages between the entrances, not an open map, at least two cells wide, and between them an abyss in a teal grey mist where nothing moves. It has no mountains but cave decor such as stalactites, and an underground mood. It is a second hex sphere of the same frequency as the planet, each of its cells under the surface cell of the same index (2026-11-08), so the grid and editor of the planet serve it. It is seen from outside as a globe for now; the view from inside, with the camera looking at its inner surface as chosen on 2026-11-06, comes later, its far side hidden by a stone vault above the camera (decided on 2026-11-09).
- Ice at sea is passable, as all water is: floes of many sizes, from big slabs to small shards, float on open water between them, drift slowly and grind together, denser in the cold. There are no impassable ice shelves (changed on 2026-11-06).
- Multiplayer is wanted, and at least one player against AI opponents (the developer, 2026-11-11: "як мінімум 1 гравець + AI").
- The fog of war, decided on 2026-11-11: each player sees only what its heroes explored, and what was explored stays seen, as in Heroes III; the unexplored lies under clouds on the surface and in the dark underground, its edge soft and not along the hexes, and underground only the passages open, with some margin behind their walls.
- The light, decided on 2026-11-10 for both layers: each is lit around every hero (the focus of the camera on a planet without heroes; one day as far as the hero can go in a turn, which `LightMoveShare` of `DA_HexHeroes` does, 0 for now, so each lights as the focus did), by what glows and at the entrances and exits. The underground is dark away from it, its maze dim but readable from any distance, from afar mostly what glows showing; the surface lies in the shade of clouds away from it, much lighter than the underground and never night, the sun following the camera. L forces an even light on both layers for editing, so the palette does not lift the dark by itself.

Performance target: the game must run on modest gaming PCs, ideally at 60 FPS with everything on screen (gameplay, resources, the trees and other objects that will fill the map). Before relying on a feature of high-end GPUs, check that it has a cheaper fallback or can be turned off, so the project does not end in a dead end. What relies on them now and needs that review: Lumen with hardware ray tracing (`r.RayTracing=True`), virtual shadow maps, Substrate, Nanite mountains, the heavy per-pixel water shaders, and terrain without LODs. The planet was measured on 2026-11-05 at 1080p on an RTX 4080 Super: about 3.2 to 4.4 ms of GPU, the most in shadow depths (about 0.6 to 0.9 ms), clouds and water (up to 0.7 ms) and TSR (0.55 ms, TAA 0.07); plain shadow maps, no Lumen or ray tracing and TAA together saved about 1.3 ms. On 2026-11-07, with the sky light, the haze and the forests, at 1280x720: 3.86 ms of GPU over a dense forest with virtual shadow maps and 3.76 with cascaded ones (`r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 0`, which draws 8.2 million triangles against 3.5 and softer shadows), 4.15 and 3.98 at mid distance, so plain shadow maps work as the cheaper fallback; there is no menu of quality yet.

Not decided yet, so ask before building on any of these:
- the view from inside the underground, which comes after the view from outside that is built now: on 2026-11-09 the developer decided that it hides its far side with a stone vault, a smaller sphere above the camera that serves as the ceiling of the cave and can carry stalactites, with the camera always below it; it is not built yet, and how the camera and the light work inside and when the vault comes are still open; its size (1:1, the frequency of the planet), its voids (an abyss in a teal grey mist) and passages at least two cells wide are decided;
- how multiplayer works (hot seat, network); AI opponents are wanted, and a first one only explores (see the heroes), how they really play is open;
- how battles play out beyond their first build of 2026-11-12 (see the battles): the siege beyond its first build (the keep, a castle without a fort, whether the moat harms), the numbers of the walls, towers and catapult, which are Claude's, creatures of two hexes, the surrender of a hero for gold, artifacts, and whether the numbers, abilities and spells, which are Claude's, stay;
- the bonuses to movement (roads, skills, artifacts, objects of the map) and how a hero crosses the sea; the rules of movement the developer agreed to on 2026-11-10 are built, and the players move in turns since 2026-11-11 (see the heroes below);
- the chests (gold or experience, without numbers yet), and of castles what the first build left out: the mage guild, the market, hiring heroes, the abilities of the creatures, which wait for battles, and whether the numbers, which are Claude's, stay.

## Environment

- Engine: launcher-style build of UE 5.8.4 at `D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_5.8`, linked through the GUID in `EngineAssociation` in `HexHero.uproject`.
- Compiler: Visual Studio 2026 with MSVC 14.51. UBT warns that this is newer than the preferred 14.50; the warning is harmless so far, but check it first if compile errors look strange.
- Shell: Windows PowerShell 5.1.

## Commands

Build the editor target:

```powershell
& 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_5.8\Engine\Build\BatchFiles\Build.bat' HexHeroEditor Win64 Development "-project=D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_Projects\HexHero\HexHero.uproject" -WaitMutex
```

Close the editor or use Live Coding when C++ changes while the editor is open, because an external build cannot replace the DLLs of a running editor.

Open the project:

```powershell
& 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_5.8\Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe' 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_Projects\HexHero\HexHero.uproject'
```

Run the automation tests. The filter can be narrowed down to one test, for example `HexHero.HexMap.Mesh.Seamless`:

```powershell
& 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_5.8\Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor-Cmd.exe' 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_Projects\HexHero\HexHero.uproject' -ExecCmds='Automation RunTests HexHero.HexMap;Quit' -TestExit='Automation Test Queue Empty' -unattended -nullrhi -nosplash -nop4 -stdout
```

Run the game in its own window on any map file:

```powershell
& 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_5.8\Engine\Binaries\Win64\UnrealEditor.exe' 'D:\Work\lsiSerg\UE_Projects\HexHero\HexHero.uproject' '/Game/HexMap/Lvl_HexWorld?Map=World256' -game -windowed -ResX=1280 -ResY=720 -ExecCmds="stat fps"
```

The project has no lint configuration. `LogPython` errors from `EditorToolset` and `NiagaraToolsets` appear in `-game` runs and do not come from project code.

Logs are written to `Saved/Logs/`. `.sln` and `.slnx` files are ignored by Git; regenerate them from the context menu of `HexHero.uproject`.

## Architecture

There is one runtime module, `HexHero`, in `Source/HexHero`.

### Hex world map

The code lives in folders of `Source/HexHero`, and the level and materials in `Content/HexMap`. `Lvl_HexWorld` is the default map.

- `Core`: game mode and project settings.
- `Grid`: hex coordinates and layout, shared by any hex grid.
- `Map`: world map actor, mesh building, map loading and the map data assets.
- `Camera`: camera pawn and its data asset.
- `Player`: player controller.
- `UI`: Slate widgets, the cursor of the game and their data asset (`UHexUiConfig`).
- `Tests`: automation tests.
- `Planet`: prototype of the world map as a hex planet, apart from everything above.
- `Hero`: the heroes of the planet, their config, their ways and their turns, the sites, the castles, the neutral stacks and the fog of war.
- `Battle`: the battles, their rules apart from any UObject (`FHexBattle`), what starts and ends them (`UHexBattles`), their field (`AHexBattleArena`) and the figures of the creatures.

A data asset class stays next to the code that uses it. Every meaningful value that can be tuned for the result, also in the Custom HLSL of materials, belongs in a data asset, as far as it costs no performance; obvious math constants stay in code.

### Planet prototype

The world map is meant to become a planet, so this prototype tries the sphere before anything else moves to it. `Lvl_HexPlanet` in `Content/HexPlanet` uses `AHexPlanetGameMode` as its override; open it with `/Game/HexPlanet/Lvl_HexPlanet?Planet=<folder>` in place of the map in the run command, or with the buttons of the planets in `SHexMapMenu`, and `MapMenuAction` there shows the map menu over the planet (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::OpenMapMenu`), so no map loads until one is chosen, which opens it in `Lvl_HexWorld`, or a planet, which opens `Lvl_HexPlanet` again with it. Nothing cooks it yet. `Tools/create_planet_assets.py` creates that level, `M_HexPlanet`, `M_HexPlanetWater`, `M_HexPlanetWaterGrid`, `M_HexPlanetWireframe` and the materials of the underground, so edit the script and not them; with the argument `configs` (`-script="<file> configs"`) it only makes the data assets that are missing, and with `underground` only the materials of the underground, of the cave objects (`M_HexPlanetCave`) and of the motes (`M_HexPlanetMote`), leaving the rest as it is, and `underground markers` those of the markers of entrances too, which otherwise are not saved again with the same graphs, as that only churns their binary assets; an existing `DA_HexUnderground` takes the values of `UNDERGROUND_MIGRATIONS` there only while it still holds their old values, so tuning by hand stays.

- A planet is a folder in `Content/Data/Planets`, read at runtime as the maps in `Content/Data/Maps` (`HexPlanetFile`): `planet.json` (`FHexPlanetManifest`: version, name, description, `frequency`, `seed`, `sortOrder`, `generation`, and `underground`, the generation of the underground) and an optional `levels.txt`, one letter per cell in the order of `FHexSphereGrid` (D deep sea, S shallows, P plains, H hills, M mountains) in lines of 80, so an edit changes only the lines of its cells, and an optional `biomes.txt` in the same layout, the `PlanetLetter` of the biome of each cell (`HexPlanetFile::EncodeBiomes`); without it, or when it is not valid, `HexPlanetGenerator::GenerateLevels` generates them from the seed and `generation` (`FHexPlanetGeneration`), and a saved planet that lacked either file writes what was generated at once (`AHexPlanet::WriteGeneratedFiles`), so a planet never changes when the generation does later (a file that is there but not valid is left alone), and a pentagon that the file makes a mountain becomes hills. Its `start` is `Random`, `Water` (deep sea) or `Land` (plains); a random planet ranks the cells by a noise of levels (`LevelNoiseScale` of `DA_HexPlanet` divided by `continentSize`, `coastDetail` octaves) and splits them by shares, highest first, so a share holds on any planet: `landShare` of the cells is land and `shallowsShare` of the sea shallows, `mountainShare` of the land is mountains, the land furthest from the coast (`RangeMinLand`) ranked by where a second noise nears zero and how far inland it lies, and `hillShare` of the land is hills, the highest of the rest. Values of scale and speed are tuned on a planet of `ReferenceFrequency` of `DA_HexPlanet` (80, Terra), and on another planet scale by its frequency over that one (`UHexPlanetConfig::GetSizeRatio`), so they keep their size in cells: the ranges (`RangeNoiseScale`, `RangeWobbleScale`), so a smaller planet has fewer ranges as wide, and the clouds, whose `CloudScale` `AHexPlanet::SetCloudParameters` multiplies and whose `CloudSpeed`, a turn, it divides by the ratio, so a smaller planet has fewer clouds of the same size moving as fast over the ground. The defaults of `FHexPlanetGeneration` give `Terra` exactly as the old thresholds did. "New hex planet..." in `SHexMapMenu` shows `SHexNewPlanetPanel` (size, start, seed, shares, continent size, coast detail), whose Create opens the planet without writing anything (`HexPlanetFile::OpenNewPlanet` keeps it in memory and opens the level with `?NewPlanet`), so trying planets leaves no files. Such a planet has no folder (`AHexPlanet::IsSaved`), the overlay warns "Planet not saved", and `SaveViewAction` asks for its name in an `SHexDialog` (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::OpenSavePlanetDialog`) before it saves the view: the folder is the name with anything but letters of any alphabet, digits, - and _ made _ (`SanitizeFolderName`), a folder that is there already needs a second press of Overwrite, and `AHexPlanet::SaveAs` writes `planet.json`, `levels.txt`, `biomes.txt` and the files of the underground (see below). Leaving a planet from the menu for a map, another planet or a new one while it is new or has painted cells not saved (`AHexPlanet::HasUnsavedChanges`) asks first (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::LeavePlanet`): save, which leaves once saved, leave without saving, or stay; the console commands `hex.LoadPlanet` and `hex.LoadMap` do not ask. The main page of the menu has Save planet (`OnSave`, `CanSave`), enabled only while there is something to save, which saves as the save key does (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::SavePlanet`) and closes the menu only for the dialog of the name of a new planet. `CopyCameraAction` on such a planet asks first (`OpenCopyCameraDialog`): save, copy only `hex.PlanetSetCamera`, or cancel. A saved planet saves only the view, as before. A dialog gives the keyboard back to the game when it closes (`SetAllUserFocusToGameViewport`), as a text box committed with Enter would otherwise keep it. New planets go to `Content/Data/Planets` in builds with the editor, so they can be committed, and to `Saved/Planets` in a packaged game, whose Content is read only; `FindPlanets` lists both. Cells have the same size on every planet (`HexRadius`), so the frequency sets the radius: 20 is about 63 x 63 hexes, 32 about 100 x 100, 48 about 150 x 150, 64 about 200 x 200, and 80 about 256 x 256. `AHexPlanet` opens the planet of the `?Planet=` option of the URL (`HexPlanetFile::OpenPlanet`), else the one saved with the view, else the lowest `sortOrder`. `Terra` is the planet of the prototype and `Islet` a small one with more sea, both generated. Folder names must have no spaces, as `hex.LoadPlanet` takes one as an argument.
- Every value of the planet that can be tuned is in a data asset in `Content/HexPlanet`, which `create_planet_assets.py` creates only when missing, so tune them by hand: `DA_HexPlanet` (`UHexPlanetConfig`: size of the cells, generation of levels and ranges, sea, water cap, colors, outlines, and the values of `M_HexPlanet` and `M_HexPlanetWireframe`), `DA_HexPlanetWater` (`UHexPlanetWaterConfig`: every value of `WATER_CODE` and `WATER_OFFSET_CODE`, a wave as (frequency, speed, slope, height)), `DA_HexPlanetClouds` and `DA_HexPlanetCamera` (`UHexPlanetCameraConfig`, read by `AHexPlanetCameraPawn::Settings`). A missing one falls back to the defaults of its class with a warning. `HexMaterialConfig::Apply` gives a material every float, int (as a float), color, `FVector` and `FVector4` of a config as parameters of the same names, once when it is made, and the script names the ones each material takes (`add_config_parameters`, defaults from the class), so a new value needs only the property and the parameter. A parameter for an `FVector4` goes through an append node, as a vector parameter gives a pin only its RGB.
- `FHexSphereGrid` is a Goldberg polyhedron: each face of an icosahedron is split into `Frequency` steps, so it has 10 * Frequency^2 + 2 cells, and the first 12 are the pentagons. Seen from outside, neighbors and corners go counterclockwise, corner K lies between neighbor K and K + 1, and the side towards neighbor K runs from corner K - 1 to corner K; three cells share each corner. The points of a face blend its vertices by sines, which keeps the largest hex within 1.2 of the smallest, where a straight projection gives 1.9. Relaxing the lattice by springs or by moving to the middle of the neighbors made it worse. `FindCell` walks to nearer neighbors.
- `HexPlanetSurface` builds the surface: one `UHexPlanetMeshComponent` per chunk (`SurfaceChunks`, under the empty root `Surface`) with two sections, so what lies behind the planet or off the screen is culled and a rebuild recreates the render state of its chunks alone. That component keeps a section on the GPU alone (`PrepareSection` packs it on any thread, `SetSection` sends it and the CPU frees it there): a position, packed tangents and four UVs of half floats, 36 bytes a vertex (the walls of the underground have a fifth, 40), no vertex colors, indices of 16 bits where they fit, and ray tracing geometry built to be traced fast and compacted. Its render data outlives the scene proxies, so a change of material or visibility uploads nothing; it draws as a static mesh, with cached mesh draw commands, and gives ray tracing its sections as a dynamic mesh does, referencing a geometry the manager evicted so it comes back. `UProceduralMeshComponent`, used before, held each section three times (152 bytes a vertex in the component, a copy in its scene proxy, one on the GPU) and built its ray tracing geometry fast and uncompacted, 54 bytes a triangle where it is now 26. The build hands its sections over in floats (`FSection`), as the GPU takes them, and finds them in doubles. A chunk (`FHexSphereCell::Chunk`) is a triangle of a face of the icosahedron split into about `ChunkSteps` of `DA_HexPlanet` lattice steps along a side (`FHexSphereGrid::Build`, the `Grid.Chunks` test); 27 gives Terra 180 chunks of about 360 cells, which drew as few triangles as 320 chunks with a third fewer draw calls, and from close to the planet about 1.6 million triangles of 6 where the 20 faces drew 5.5. Each chunk also has a coarse mesh (`CoarseChunks`, `HexPlanetSurface::FBuild::CoarseSections`), a fan of 12 triangles a cell from its center, corners and side middles at the heights of the nearest vertices of its piece there (`FCoarsePiece`), placed as the piece, so 0.8 million triangles for the whole of Terra: beyond `CoarseDistance` of `DA_HexPlanet` from the camera it is drawn in place of the pieces. `AHexPlanet::UpdateChunkLod` shows each chunk by the distance of its bounds every tick (`ChunkLodStates`): the pieces where all of it is nearer than `CoarseBand` short of `CoarseDistance`, the coarse mesh where all of it is further than `CoarseBand` past it, and both where it reaches into that band, with the masked `LodNearMaterial` and `LodFarMaterial`, copies of `M_HexPlanet_Cut` with `LodSide` 1 and 2, whose mask (`SURFACE_CUT_CODE`) keeps complementary pixels by a noise of the pixel that changes every frame against how far each point lies across the band from `LodCamera`, the camera, so the temporal filter dissolves one into the other without a pop and every pixel is drawn once; only the chunks in the band pay for the masked material, which on Terra at mid distance cost about 0.2 ms of GPU. `hex.PlanetCoarseDistance` and `hex.PlanetCoarseBand` move the band while the game runs, for trying it closer, and 0 goes back to the values of the asset. The whole planet in view then draws about 1.6 million triangles where the pieces drew 9, and its GPU time on the machine it was made on fell from 4.5 to 3.2 ms at 1080p. So only the chunks near the camera keep their pieces (`ChunkPieces`): one whose nearest point comes within `PieceStreamAhead` of `DA_HexPlanet` past the band has them built in the background from what the surface shown was built from (`StartStream`, a build of the pieces alone with the heights of its corners and relief given, `FBuildOptions`, 6 to 35 ms a chunk on Terra, `PieceStreamChunks` at a time), one further than `PieceStreamKeep` past that frees them, and a chunk without them draws its coarse mesh; a rebuild of levels gives pieces only to the chunks that want them and drops what a stream built of a chunk it made anew (`ChunkGenerations`). At the start the build of every chunk stays for ten frames (`StartupSurface`), until the camera has its place, the chunks it wants take their pieces from it, and then the allocator is asked to give back what it can (`FMemory::Trim`). `UpdateChunkLod` measures from the view target, where the camera is in this frame, as the camera manager tells where it was in the frame before. A chunk without pieces draws its coarse mesh, which next to the pieces of a neighbor leaves gaps where the two do not meet, so a jump of the camera (`hex.PlanetSetCamera`) waits until every chunk that draws pieces from where it goes has them (`AHexPlanet::ArePiecesReadyAt`, `SetPiecePrefetch`, which builds those first, all at once, and keeps them; at most `JumpWaitMax` of `DA_HexPlanetCamera`): on Terra 61 chunks in about 130 ms in the background, while the old view goes on, and at the start at once from the build of the start. A chunk the camera still comes near without its pieces, nearer than the band, gets them built at once on the game thread (`BuildPiecesNow`, logged), which a fast orbit close up did not need. A jump is a cut (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::SetPlacement`): the lazy sun stands where it should over the new place at once while it follows the camera, rather than gliding there over the dark side, and the camera manager drops what the renderer kept of the frames before (`SetGameCameraCutThisFrame`). `hex.PlanetStreamPieces 0` keeps the pieces of every chunk. `hex.Later <Seconds> <Command>` runs a console command later, through the player as the console does, so `-ExecCmds` can test a jump while the game runs, `HighResShot 1` included. Whatever stands on the ground of a cell reads its triangles from the build being shown, else builds them again on its own (`AHexPlanet::GetCellTriangles`, `HexPlanetSurface::BuildCellTriangles`, which the `Surface.Rebuild` and `Rivers` tests compare with a full build, as they do a build of the pieces alone), the objects and the bridges in parallel. `hex.PlanetMemory [Delay] [quit]` logs the triangles by level, what the surface holds on the GPU, the memory of the process and of the RHI and the ray tracing geometry of the chunks (`D3D12.DumpRayTracingGeometries`), with the times of five seconds of frames, Delay seconds after the planet is built; the time of the render thread is n/a while Slate draws a second window, such as the toast of `HighResShot`, for some seconds after it, as the engine then keeps only the slice between the presents of the two windows (`PresentWindow_RenderThread`), and `hex.PlanetRenderTimes [Frames]` logs the times the engine gives each frame with the windows shown. On Terra on 2026-11-08 at 1280x720, close to a range / from orbit, all this took the process from 9.7 / 10.3 GB committed and 6.3 / 6.6 GB in use to 8.7 / 8.4 and 5.4 / 5.4, its peak from 12.9 to 9.5 GB committed, the ray tracing geometry of the surface close up from 208 to 123 MB, its build from 486 to 308 ms and showing it from 571 to 220 ms, at the same frame times; about 2.5 GB of what the editor binary holds is memory its allocator, mimalloc, keeps for reuse (`FMallocUnused` with `-LLMCSV`), which a packaged game, with the binned allocator, should not keep. The debug views keep the switch: `hex.Wireframe` draws a copy of each chunk and of its coarse mesh, shown with them (`WireChunks`, `WireCoarseChunks`, made the first time, under the empty `Wireframe`), so in the band both show, and a coarse mesh counts as terrain for `hex.WireframeTarget`; as the surface keeps its meshes on the GPU alone, the copies come from a build of the surface shown when that view is turned on (`BuildWireSections`) and from each build shown after (`SetWireSection`). The `Surface.Rebuild` test compares the coarse sections too. It reads no UObject, so it runs on any thread, and builds the chunks in parallel (`ParallelFor`); the kit is read once (`LoadKit`, `SurfaceKit`). `AHexPlanet::SetCellLevels` changes the levels at once and rebuilds what they reach in `UE::Tasks`: `FindAffectedChunks` takes the cells whose level or massif step changed (`FindMassifSteps` over the whole planet), their neighbors, whose meshes change, and the neighbors of those, whose normals on the shared sides change, and rebuilds every chunk holding any of them, both sections. The normals on the sides towards a chunk that is not rebuilt sum those of its cells next to it, which are built again only for that (the halo), so no seam shows; the `Surface.Rebuild` test compares a partial rebuild with a full one. `MeanPeak` stays at the value of the first build, so the grid fade does not jump. The sea field and the water grid are built again in the same task when a cell crossed the coast or moved between shallows and deep sea, and `Tick` shows the result (`FinishRebuild`), starts the next rebuild if levels changed meanwhile, as calls during a rebuild are merged, and broadcasts `OnLevelsRebuilt`; `GetShownLevels` gives the levels of the surface shown. The sea field is a new texture each time with a new name (`MakeUniqueObjectName`), as one made under the name of a texture still there replaces that object in place and the water turns light turquoise. `hex.PlanetSetLevel <Cell> <Level> [Rings] [ToCell]` sets a cell and the cells up to `Rings` around it, or a line of cells along the arc to `ToCell` and `Rings` around it, for trying long chains (`hex.PlanetFocus` logs the latitude and longitude of its cell, for `hex.PlanetSetCamera`), and the log gives each rebuild's chunks and times: on Terra a stroke of a few cells rebuilds two or three chunks in about 6 ms in the background, plus about 70 ms for the sea field and water grid when the coast changes, and shows them in about 30 ms with the sea, and the first build of all chunks takes about 160 ms after about 850 ms of reading the kit.
- The levels and biomes are painted with a brush (`UHexPlanetPainter`, owned by `AHexPlanetPlayerController`): B (`PaintModeAction`) turns it on with its palette `SHexPlanetPalette` at the left, whose buttons never take the keyboard, 1 to 5 (`PaintLevelAction`, one action whose keys give their number through a scalar modifier) choose deep sea to mountains and turn it on, the biome buttons of the palette paint biomes instead, at once and only where the level of a cell allows them, X (`BrushSizeAction`) makes the brush one to three rings of cells, in a cycle (the size buttons of the palette are lit only while no tool or one that uses it is chosen, `UHexPlanetPainter::UsesBrushSize`, as Entrance, Carve, River and Bridge take no size and outline one cell, `GetOutlineSize`), and Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Y (`UndoAction`, `RedoAction`) undo and redo whole strokes, up to `PaintUndoSteps`. Keys of symbols such as [ and ] are not used, as Unreal names them by the character of the layout, so on another layout they are other keys. While it is on, the left button paints instead of dragging the planet (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::OnDragStarted`), and a stroke walks the arc between the cells of two frames, so a fast mouse leaves no gaps; a pentagon painted a mountain becomes hills. The cells of a stroke show at once as flat tiles of the color of their new level in a mesh on the planet (`PaintPreview`, `PaintLift`, `PaintSeaLift` over the sea, and `PaintMountainTop` as the top of a mountain, so a tile covers one), and on release the stroke goes to `SetCellLevels`; a tile goes once `GetShownLevels` has its level. Painting marks the levels as edited (`MarkLevelsEdited`), the overlay warns "Levels not saved", and undoing or redoing back to the step of the last save makes the planet saved again: each step has an `Id`, a layer's top is the `Id` of its top step or `BaseId`, the last step the cap of `PaintUndoSteps` dropped, and the save keeps the tops (`SavedTop`, `OnSaved`); every change a step makes passes `bByStep` (`MarkLevelsEdited`, `SetReliefOverrides`, `OnRiversChanged`, `UHexPlanetRivers::SetEdges`, `SetEntrances`), and any other change, such as `hex.PlanetSetLevel`, sets `bEditedOutsideSteps`, which only a save clears (`UHexPlanetPainter::UpdateSavedState`, `AHexPlanet::ClearEditedBySteps`), and `SaveViewAction` writes `levels.txt` and `biomes.txt` of a saved planet (`AHexPlanet::SaveLevels`), while `SaveAs` writes them for a new planet that was painted. A stroke of levels keeps in its step of Undo the biomes it changed. `create_input_assets.py` adds the lines of these actions to an existing `DA_HexKeyLegend` that lacks them (`ADDED_LEGEND_ACTIONS`, also the line of `ForcedLightAction`), gives the line of an action another took the place of to the new one where it stands (`REPLACED_ACTIONS`, `IA_HexSunFollow` by `IA_HexForcedLight`) and deletes the old asset.
- Each cell is a pair of a level and a biome, as on the flat map: the biomes with a `PlanetLetter` (`AHexPlanet::BiomeConfigs`, by letter: W water, S shoals, I ice, G grass, D sand, R rock, N snow, M swamp, T tundra, P steppe, from `create_biome_assets.py`; there is no forest biome, as forests are objects (`UHexPlanetObjects`, below) that may stand on any biome, even snow, and the letter F of older planets became G), and a cell holds an index into them (`CellBiomes`). Without `biomes.txt`, or when it is not valid, `HexPlanetGenerator::GenerateBiomes` places them by a climate, with all its values in `Biomes` of `DA_HexPlanet`: a temperature, 1 at the equator and 0 at the poles, colder by `HeightCooling` a level and moved by noise (`ClimateNoiseScale`, on the unit sphere), and a moisture of land, 1 at the coast falling over `MoistureReach` cells, drier in belts around `DryBeltTemperature`. The sea freezes below `IceTemperature`, and up to `ShoreIceRings` cells of sea from snow land freeze too as shore ice (`HexPlanetGenerator::AddShoreIce`), so a snowy coast never meets open water; `hex.PlanetShoreIce [Rings] [save]` does the same on a planet that has its biomes, and froze 9 cells of Islet and 2 of Terra; land is snow, tundra, desert, steppe, swamp (only plains) or grass by thresholds, and dry mountains of grass or steppe are rock. `Temperature` and `Moisture` of `FHexPlanetGeneration` (and the new planet panel) move them for a planet. A biome its level does not allow is replaced by the one most neighbors have that it allows, else the default of the level (`FindFittingBiome`), also when a level is painted. `M_HexPlanet` reads them from `CellBiomeTexture`, two pixels per cell at twice the column of `CellMarks`: the terrain and rock layers of the biome, in blue which of the cell and its neighbors are mountains and in alpha which are under the sea, and the terrain layers of the six neighbors, two to a byte (`WriteBiomePixels`); a mountain draws the rock layer of its biome as its terrain, down to its foot and the water, so a change of biomes uploads that texture and rebuilds no mesh; only ice that freezes or thaws rebuilds the sea field, in the rebuild task of levels. `BIOME_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py` samples the layers of `TA_Biome_Color` and `TA_Biome_Normal` from the three planes of the world (`BiomeTileSize`, `TriplanarSharpness`, by the normal of the vertex, skipping planes of little weight), blends a cell into the neighbors across every side whose blend reaches a pixel, up to three other layers, half and half on the side and none `BiomeBlendWidth` inside, the line moved by a noise of two sizes (`BiomeBlendNoise`, `BiomeBlendDetail`) both cells see with opposite signs by the order of their layers, so they agree, and into the neighbor of a mountain that is not one by `MountainBlendShift`, and of land into its neighbor under the sea by `CoastBlendShift`, so where the winding coast lifts part of a cell of the sea above the water it draws the land, and a snowy coast shows no sand of the seabed, never past the apothem: of each pair of opposite sides one is at least that far, so no blend stops on a straight line where another side would take over, which taking only the nearest side and the other side at its corner did; the alpha of the layers and a noise of each layer (`BiomeEdgeNoise`) then decide which covers the other (`BiomeHeightBlend`, `BiomeBlendDepth`), so edges run in patches. The grass layer (`GrassTintLayer`) dries a little towards `GrassDryColor`, keeping its texture by its alpha: more the higher a hill (`GrassDryStart` to `GrassDryEnd`, `GrassHillDry`), in streaks of a bent noise on flat hill tops (`GrassStreak*`) and in rare patches anywhere (`GrassPatch*`), so wide grass is not one tone. Over that come the beach and the rock, each a property of the terrain layer (`BeachLayers0`..`2`, `RockLayers0`..`2`, which `AHexPlanet::SetBiomeLayers` takes from the first biome with that layer, `PlanetBeachLayer` and `MountainRockLayer`, and a rock of a mountain for itself), so they blend between cells as the layers do (`BlendMapped`): the beach below `BeachHeight` and on the slopes of the coast up to `ShoreSlopeTop`, in place of the rock there, sand for grass, swamp, tundra and steppe, snow for snow, the rock for a mountain, which from `SeabedHeight`, at the water, down over `SeabedFade` turns into the seabed of the layer, the heights of both deciding where each lies (`SeabedHeightBlend`, `SeabedBlendDepth`) and the upper part dusted with the beach (`SeabedDusting`), so a snowy shore goes through snowy sand into sand under the water (`SeabedLayers0`..`2`, from `PlanetSeabedLayer` of the biomes, else the beach), sand for snow, so snow never lies under the sea and the bottom is sand under every coast; the ice biome has the terrain of sand too, so the bottom does not change where the sea freezes; the rock on slopes (`SlopeStart`, `SlopeEnd`, steep enough that the gentle sides of hills keep their biome), snowed over on snow ground but for its high edges (`SnowRockCover`, `SnowRockPeek`), so a steep hill of snow stays snowy, snow above `SnowHeight` on flat ground, and the seabed darkens to `SeabedDarkening` at `DeepDepth`; the normal is in world space (`OutNormal`). Each layer is drawn at its own `TileSize`, `Tint`, `NormalStrength`, `HeightContrast` and `HeightOffset` of `DA_HexTerrainLayers`, which `AHexPlanet` gives the surface materials as `LayerParams` (`UHexTerrainLayersConfig::CreateParamsTexture`, a column per layer; its black default keeps `BiomeTileSize`), and a rock layer with a `ScreeLayer` or `GravelLayer` there is drawn as three (`FBiomeLayers::Sample`): its cliff where the ground is steeper than `RockCliffFlatStart` to `RockCliffFlatEnd`, its gravel where it is flatter than `RockGravelFlatStart` to `RockGravelFlatEnd`, the scree between them and in gullies (`RockGullyScree`), the flatness moved by a noise (`RockSetNoise`, `RockSetNoiseScale`) and the height of each deciding where they meet (`RockSetHeightBlend`, `RockSetBlendDepth`), so a mountain drawn as the rock of its biome has cliffs, scree and gravel at its foot without another table. Its GPU cost is not measured yet. The layers Tundra and Steppe were added to `Tools/HexTextures`. On Terra at 2880 x 1620 on the machine it was made on it cost about 0.2 ms of GPU (4.23 to 4.41 ms from far, 3.97 to 4.18 close; base pass +0.13, water +0.03). `hex.PlanetSetBiome <Cell> <Letter> [Rings]` sets biomes from the console.
- The relief of a mountain or hill can be set by hand (`HexPlanetSurface::FReliefOverride`, `AHexPlanet::ReliefOverrides`), stored in `relief.json` of the planet (`FHexReliefFile`, a sparse list of cells, `HexPlanetFile::LoadRelief`, `SaveRelief`, written with the levels and deleted when empty): the version of its piece and which of its allowed turns it takes, whether it is mirrored, each -1 to leave it to the hash, and a factor on its height. Only what keeps the sides joined is offered: a version is one of the pieces with the same sides (`FBuild::CellVersions`), a turn one that gives the same set of neighbors (`CellTurnChoices`, so a cell whose neighbors are not symmetric has one), a mirrored piece is looked up by the mirrored set of neighbors and always fits, and the factor blends across the sides as the generated one does. The palette has a section Relief of mountains and hills, whose buttons (Version, Turn, Mirror, Height, Auto; no keys, as symbol keys depend on the layout) choose an `EHexReliefTool` of `UHexPlanetPainter`: a click changes each cell of the brush once per stroke, at once, as a step of Undo (`FHexPaintStep::OldRelief`, `NewRelief`), and skips a cell the tool cannot change; Ctrl+click goes the other way (the version and turn before, lower), as the tooltips of the buttons say. Height multiplies the factor by `PaintReliefStep` of `DA_HexPlanet`. One tool of the brush is active at a time (`EHexPaintTool`): a level, a biome, a tool of relief, a kind of object (the section Objects of the palette, with the density of a cover, Sparse to Dense), the Eraser, which takes the cover, the decor and what was placed by hand off the cells, or River and Bridge (see the rivers), and choosing one clears the others and their highlight in the palette; Move the planet (no tool) at the top puts the tool down, so the left button drags the planet while the palette stays (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::IsBrushArmed`). Objects go on the cells at once, a kind only where its level allows it, as a step of Undo (`FHexPaintStep::OldObjects`, `NewObjects`). By hand (the toggle On cells / By hand of the section Objects of either layer, `UHexPlanetPainter::SetPlacingByHand`, `EHexPaintTool::Placed`) a kind chosen places one object at the point clicked, a mesh of the variant of the biome there, else any mesh of the kind (`MakePlaced`, its turn, scale and mesh from a hash of where it stands), as many as a cell takes, and Ctrl+click takes away the one nearest. The kinds of By hand are those of the layer and after them, under a heading of their own on the palette, the covers and decor of the other layer (`UHexPlanetObjects::FindPlaceableSets`, as the developer asked on 2026-11-10, so a dead tree stands underground and bones on the surface): these a hand alone places, so choosing one turns By hand on and turning it off goes back to a kind of the layer (`UHexPlanetPainter::IsObjectSetForeign`), and they take indices after those of the layer, so the generation and `objects.json` count the sets of the layer as before; a mass of the underground that fills its cell (`UHexPlanetObjectSet::IsCellMass`, a kind of the underground that blocks) is never placed by hand nor offered to the surface. The tools of `EHexPlacedTool` act on the object nearest the click within `PaintObjectPickRadius` of a cell radius on its cell or a neighbor (`UHexPlanetObjects::FindObjectNear`), placed by hand or of the cover or decor of a cell: touching one of those turns its cell into objects placed by hand where they stood (`UnpackCell`, through `PlaceInCell`, without their tilt), so it can be moved, turned or taken away alone and the rest of the cell stays as it was, which the developer chose on 2026-11-10 over keeping only By hand. Move drags it while the button is held, onto another cell too, shown apart from the chunks (`SetDragPreview`, an instance of its own), so no chunk is placed again every frame, Turn turns it by `PaintObjectTurn` degrees, Size scales it by `PaintObjectScale`, Version gives it the next mesh of its kind, Erase (the Eraser while By hand is on) takes away the one nearest every frame the button is held, and Ctrl goes the other way, each a step of Undo, a stroke of Move one step. An object placed by hand may reach over into a neighbor, but `UHexPlanetObjects::CanPlace` keeps it on land or a passage whatever its kind allows (`IsPlacedLevel`), on the ground of its cell, off an entrance, out of the sea and of a river and, underground or for a kind that clips its footprint, off the void and out of the walls, refusing a click with a message on the screen; it blocks nothing, so a single tree of a forest stands alone. It is drawn where it stands on the ground (`PlacePlaced`), after every cover and decor of the chunk, so the random values per instance of what the generation placed (`PerInstanceRandom`, by the order of the instances) do not change as objects are placed by hand, left out while its cell is no longer land or a passage, its ground no longer holds it or the maze opened a void beside it (logged), glows and gives spores as a decor of its kind, counts for the canopy seen from far away where its cell has no cover (`GetCellCanopy`), keeps puddles off its cell, and survives Generate again and New layout on a passage; the Eraser on cells takes it with the rest. `hex.PlanetPaint byhand <on|off>`, `placed <Place|Move|Turn|Size|Version|Erase>`, `place <@Latitude:Longitude or Cell> [ctrl]` (a cell for its middle) and `drag <From> <To>` drive them from the console. The palette stands at the top left corner, so it stays put as a tab or a tool changes its height, and scrolls when it is taller than the screen. The middle button drags the planet in any mode, so it moves while the left button paints, and a release within `ClickTolerance` pixels of `DA_HexPlanetCamera` centers the cell under the mouse (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::OnMiddleStarted`, `OnMiddleCompleted`). `SHexHoverInfo` shows the version and turn as one of how many, that the neighbors fix the turn, and what was set by hand. `AHexPlanet::SetReliefOverrides` rebuilds the cells whose overrides changed and their neighbors (`HexPlanetSurface::FindChangedOverrides`, the `ChangedCells` of `FindAffectedChunks`), which the `Surface.Rebuild` test checks, and `hex.PlanetSetRelief <Cell> <Version> [Turn] [Mirror] [Scale]` does the same from the console.
- Forests, bushes, flowers, stones and decor stand on the cells as objects (`UHexPlanetObjects`, a component of `AHexPlanet`), of kinds that are assets for an artist, `UHexPlanetObjectSet` in `PlanetObjectsFolder` of `HexMapSettings` (`/Game/HexPlanet/Objects/Sets`, found through the asset registry and in `DirectoriesToAlwaysCook`): its `Id` (the name in files), whether it is a `Cover`, which fills a cell at a density of 1 to 3 (`CountByDensity`), or a `Decor`, a few objects near the middle (`Reach`), whether it blocks movement (shown at the cursor; nothing moves yet), the levels it may stand on, and its variants by biome (`FHexObjectVariant`: `PlanetLetter`s, meshes, `Snow`, `ScaleFactor`, `CountFactor`, `GenerateShare`; a variant with no letters serves every biome without one), so trees follow their biome: snowed-over spruces on snow, spruces and birches in the tundra, dead trees and willows in swamps, acacias on dry land. The covers are Forest, Bushes (thickets of a few shrubs), Flowers (patches of a meadow in flower, as the developer asked for bigger glades on 2026-11-10, when the flowers were tufts a few units wide), TallGrass (green on grass and swamp, dry on steppe and sand), Wheat (a field, as dense at every density), Stones and Rocks. Each cell holds one cover and one decor, and besides them as many objects placed by hand as it takes (`FHexPlacedObject`, in `FHexCellObjects::Placed`, as the developer asked on 2026-11-10), in `objects.json` of the planet, a line per cell with only what it has (`HexPlanetFile::SaveObjects`; the objects placed by hand on the line of their cell as a list `placed` of `{"id", "x", "y", "yaw", "scale", "mesh"}`: its kind, of either layer, where it stands from the middle of the cell in radii of a cell along the axes of `UHexPlanetObjects::GetCellAxes` to a thousandth, its turn in degrees, its scale before the `ScaleFactor` of the variant its mesh belongs to and which mesh of the kind it is, counted over all its variants in order (`AllSlots`)), so Git merges it; a planet without it generates them from the shares of the variants (`UHexPlanetObjects::GenerateSurfaceObjects`), a cover in patches of a noise as wide as `GeneratePatchCells` of its kind in cells (Forest 7, TallGrass 6, Flowers 5, Bushes 4, Wheat 2.5, `ObjectNoiseScale` of `DA_HexPlanet` for a kind without it), dense in the middle of a patch by `GenerateDensityShares`, keeping the pentagons free, and writes it at once, even empty, as with the levels, and Generate again of the Objects tab (`UHexPlanetPainter::RegenerateObjects`, `hex.PlanetPaint regenerate objects`) gives the land those covers and decor again in place of what was painted on cells, as one step of Undo, what was placed by hand staying, so a planet saved before the kinds changed can take them (Terra was generated again on 2026-11-10, Small not, as it may hold painting); a save drops what the level of its cell does not allow, which is kept until then but not drawn, and what stands on the cell of an entrance the same save keeps (`AHexPlanet::GetKeptEntranceCells`, `UHexPlanetObjects::MakeSavedLines`, the `Objects.SaveLines` test), while an entrance that cannot be used, which the save leaves out of `entrances.json`, keeps the objects of its cell; generated objects are written before the entrances are known, so with all of them. The objects are instances, a `UHierarchicalInstancedStaticMeshComponent` per chunk and mesh (`ApplyChunk`), standing on the real ground: `PlaceInCell` throws them at random in the cell (`FRandomStream` of the cell and the `Id` as text, as the hash of an `FName` changes between runs) and finds the triangle of the surface under each (`AHexPlanet::GetCellTriangles`, which may build the ground of a cell again, so `PlaceChunk` gets it in parallel by cell, in buckets), skipping ground steeper than `MinUp` or within `MinHeight` of the sea, `Spacing` apart by size, leaning with the ground by `SlopeAlign`, sunk by `Sink`, now and then a giant (`GiantChance`); chunks are placed in parallel, and a rebuild of the surface, a change of biomes or of objects places the chunks of its cells again. The first custom data of an instance is the rock layer of the biome of its cell (`M_HexPlanetBoulder`), the second the snow of its variant. Each component is culled whole past `CullDistance` of its set plus its bounds (`SetCullDistance`), as a cull distance of the instances alone left every chunk in view drawing (1,688 draws over open sea, 545 after). `hex.PlanetObjects 0` hides them. Terra has about 451,000 of them on 17,000 cells, placed in 21 ms and shown in 220 ms. Their shadows are kept in the cache of the virtual shadow maps though the leaves sway (`ShadowCacheInvalidationBehavior` Rigid on their components; `hex.PlanetObjectShadowCache 0`, read when a chunk makes its components, for the old way): redrawn every frame for a sway nobody sees in a shadow, they were most of what a forest cost. On the machine it was made on at 1280x720, measured with `hex.PlanetObjects` 1 and 0 once the frame time had settled, about 75 s after the start (it falls by about 0.4 ms in the first minute, whatever is shown, which made earlier figures of the same run look like costs): over a dense forest at 600 the objects cost 0.36 ms of GPU (3.61 / 3.25 ms; 0.65 without the cache) and about 1.5 ms of the render thread (5.2 / 3.7, 921 draws against 470), at 1300 0.45 ms (3.94 / 3.49) and 0.9 ms, and over a meadow in flower 0.06 ms (3.26 / 3.20) and 1.5 ms. The trees of balls of before, opaque, cost 0.13 ms of GPU at the place where the cards cost 0.53 ms without the cache (3.39 / 3.26 against 3.80 / 3.28, the same cover), so the cards with their alpha cost about 0.2 ms more than the balls with the cache; on a modest GPU that is the first thing to look at, with the render thread. With the shadows cached, the sway costs only the motion vectors of the trees: 0.08 ms over that forest (3.63 / 3.55 ms with `r.Velocity.EnableVertexDeformation` 1 and 0), so swaying only the trees near the camera (`WorldPositionOffsetDisableDistance`, as the floes have) would save half of that at most and was left out on 2026-11-10; it belongs with the quality presets. Shadows draw the LOD the view draws. Coarser LODs for shadows alone (`r.Shadow.LODDistanceFactor 3`) save nothing with virtual shadow maps, also while the camera glides over the forest (3.65 ms either way), as their cache holds, but with cascaded ones (`r.Shadow.Virtual.Enable 0`), which draw every caster every frame, they save 0.15 ms of the 0.49 ms the objects cost there (3.67 / 3.52 ms, 3.18 without objects), so that setting belongs in the preset that turns virtual shadow maps off; `r.ForceLODShadow` changed nothing for the instances. The meshes are generated, and since 2026-11-10, when the developer found the trees of balls too cartoony, a plant is wood and cards: `Tools/HexPlanetObjects/generate_planet_objects.py` (any Python 3) writes GLB files to `Saved/HexPlanetObjects` with vertex colors (`COLOR_0`, linear; its alpha is how much a vertex sways) and a UV. The wood, a trunk that forks into limbs and twigs, is solid, its UV outside the atlas (`SOLID_UV`), and a card is a sheet of two or four triangles that shows one tile of the atlas `T_HexFoliage`, a spray of leaves, a bough of needles, a tuft of grass or blossoms, cut by its alpha (`add_card`). A broadleaf crown (`leafy_tree`: broadleaf, birch, oak) is lobes at the ends of its limbs, each covered with cards where no other lobe hides it (`add_lobe_cards`), two of three lying on it as shingles and the third standing out as the end of a twig, with dark cards inside that close the view through it; the normals of the cards turn away from the middle of the crown, so it is lit as one round volume, and their colors darken low and inside it. A spruce is whorls of drooping boughs, a pine plates of needles on a few limbs, a willow curtains hanging from arching limbs, an acacia umbrellas of feathery sprays; bushes, dry bushes, tundra shrubs (mats of heather), reeds, flowers (grass, stems from the side and blossoms open to the sky, for a camera above), tall and dry grass and wheat are cards that stand (`add_standing_card`, normals up, so the sun lights them as the ground) or lie (`add_lying_card`); stones, rocks with split faces, logs and the stump are solid as before. `generate_foliage_atlas.py` (any Python 3, no packages) draws the atlas, 1024 with 24 tiles laid out in `foliage_tiles.py`: a tinted tile is nearly white, so the color of the vertices of a card is the color of its leaves, and a colored one (flowers, wheat, reeds, heather) holds its own colors under white cards. A mesh of cards is written three times, the whole one and `_LOD1` and `_LOD2` with fewer and larger cards (`LOD_STRIDE`, `LOD_GROWTH`), as reducing triangles tears crowns apart, with the screen sizes of its LODs in the manifest (`TREE_SCREENS`, `PLANT_SCREENS`); `blender_preview_objects.py`, run with Blender, renders chosen meshes with the atlas to a picture in a few seconds, for judging shapes without the engine. `import_planet_objects.py`, run with the commandlet after `create_planet_assets.py` (or its argument `foliage`, which imports the atlas and builds the material alone), imports them to `/Game/HexPlanet/Objects/Meshes` with `M_HexPlanetFoliage` or `M_HexPlanetBoulder`, gives a mesh of cards the meshes of its LODs (`UHexPlanetObjectSet::SetMeshLodsFrom`, which copies their mesh descriptions, the meshes themselves imported to a folder that is deleted again) and a solid one two LODs reduced to 35% and 12% of the triangles (`SetMeshLods`, as the editor subsystem that does it is missing in a commandlet), and creates the sets of `SETS` that are missing, which then belong to the artist: delete one to have it made again; a set that is there takes the values of `MIGRATIONS` and `SHARE_MIGRATIONS` only while it still holds their old values. `M_HexPlanetFoliage` (`FOLIAGE_CODE`, `FOLIAGE_MASK_CODE`, `FOLIAGE_OFFSET_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py`) is masked and two sided with LODs that dissolve into each other: it draws the vertex color times the tile of a card (`FoliageBrightness`; the vertex color alone where the UV lies outside the atlas), cut at `FoliageAlphaCut` of the alpha of the tile, lit by the normals of the vertices from both sides (a normal in world space, which a two sided material does not flip), lighter or darker and warmer or cooler by instance (`FoliageTint`, `FoliageHueShift`), the leaves broken into clumps by `HexNoise` (`FoliageNoise*`) and lit through away from the sun (`FoliageTranslucency`, the sun from `MPC_HexPlanet`), snow of the snow layer on what faces up (`FoliageSnow*`), and sways the leaves (`WindStrength`, `WindSpeed`); all are values of `DA_HexPlanet`. The atlas keeps the share its alpha covers in its mips (`do_scale_mips_for_alpha_coverage`), as plain mips thin crowns out from far away, and does not repeat. `build_parameter_collection` keeps the parameters of `MPC_HexPlanet` that are there, as made again they get new ids and the aurora materials that read them fail to compile (`CollectionParameter has invalid parameter None`).
- Each layer has its own kinds of objects (`UHexPlanetObjectSet::PlanetLayers`; `UHexPlanetObjects::FindLayerSets`, the sets of a layer by `SortOrder`, which `Init`, the palette and the generation count by alike, and `GetPaintableSets`, its covers and decor), so the surface never shows a cave kind nor the underground a forest. The cave kinds (sets `DA_HexObject_Cave*`, Underground only, level 0): covers `CaveMushrooms`, `CaveStalagmites`, `CaveRubble` and decor `CaveCrystals`, `CaveStalagmiteField` (a forest of spires that fills a cell), `CaveBones`, `CaveRuneStone`. None of these blocks movement, as most corridors are as wide as passages may be and a blocking cell would cut one; the maze keeps heroes apart. Impassable cells, as the developer asked on 2026-11-09, are masses that fill a cell and block it (`bBlocksMovement`, decor sorted after the other kinds, 240 to 250, so the indices of `objects.json` stay): `CaveStalagmiteMass` (a mass of dark stalagmites fused on a mound, up to about 160 high), `CaveCrystalMass` (a druse of giant glowing crystals, about 160) and `CaveRockfall` (a heap of angular blocks, about 80); the generation gives a kind that blocks only empty cells where blocking leaves no passage one cell wide (`IsNeck`) and the passages around it meet within a few rings without it, checked again as it takes each (`FObjectKind::bBlocks`, `CanBlock` in `GenerateObjects`), so they stand in halls and corridors three or more wide, and the `Underground.Objects` test checks that the necks, the pieces, the joined networks and the cells out of reach stay the same with every cell that blocks made void. Their meshes reach at most 74 from the middle (`MASS_REACH` of `generate_cave_objects.py`) and may lean on the foot of a wall, as `PlaceInCell` keeps no wall clearance for a kind that blocks. Terra has 45 of them (18, 8 and 19), Islet 10, Small 3; `hex.PlanetUnderground all objects` added them to the files of 2026-11-09 without changing a line of them. A planet without `underground/objects.json` generates them when the underground is first shown (`UHexPlanetObjects::Generate`, which underground calls `GenerateUndergroundObjects` and leaves the surface code as it was) and `WriteGeneratedFiles` writes it, unless the entrances were edited and not saved (`bEntrancesEdited`), as the objects keep off the exits in memory: then the next save writes both, and leaving without it generates them again from the saved entrances; `HexPlanetUnderground::GenerateObjects`, pure and deterministic, gives each kind `GenerateShare` of its candidate cells (passages its levels allow, no pentagon, no cover or decor yet, not within `ExitClearRings` of an exit, 2 for the glowing and tall kinds so the marker of an exit stays the brightest thing of its hall), ranked by a Perlin noise of patches `GeneratePatchCells` across (its lattice step is twice that in cells, as the noise rises and falls about once over a step; the offset from an `FRandomStream` of the seed and the group, as a hash added to the point overflows `PerlinNoise3D`), shared by the kinds of a `GenerateGroup`, so a crystal cavern, a fungus grove or a field of spires is one place, and writes the index of its set + 1 (`FObjectKind::SetIndex`); a cover gets the density 3, 2 or 1 by rank. It logs the cells of each kind and the middle cell of its largest patch, for `hex.PlanetFocus <Cell> 900` underground. A game run and `hex.PlanetUnderground <Folder> objects` write the same file byte for byte (checked by md5 on a copy of Islet); Terra has 1,797 cells with cave objects (11,290 instances), Islet 300, Small 117. The cave meshes stay inside their cell on the sides towards a void and towards a cleared cell (`bClipFootprint`: `PlaceInCell` rejects a point nearer such a side than how far the mesh reaches from its pivot, read from its vertices at `Init` as the import gives them CPU access, times its size plus `ObjectFootprintMargin`, `HexPlanetUnderground::IsFootprintClear`, the `Underground.Footprint` test), so nothing hangs over the abyss from a ledge; off for the surface kinds. A rebuild underground logs the instances of each kind and the cells where none fitted (`FPlaceStats` of `PlaceChunk`). They draw with `M_HexPlanetCave` (`CAVE_OBJECT_CODE` of `create_planet_assets.py`, lit, no texture): the vertex color, which the shader reads linear as the glTF import undoes the sRGB encoding of vertex colors (decoding it again made them black), lighter or darker by instance and broken by the value noise of `NOISE_CODE` (`CaveObject*` of `DA_HexUnderground`), and by the alpha of a vertex it glows in `GlowColor` times `GlowStrength` of its set, breathing by `GlowPulse` at `GlowPulseSpeed` (a set that glows gets its own material instance, `GetMaterial(Parent, Set)`, which `HexMaterialConfig::Apply` gives the values of the set too); no lights, Lumen carries the glow. `GlowPool` of a set lights the floor under it and `Spores` rise from it (see the floor and the air below). The meshes come from `Tools/HexPlanetObjects/generate_cave_objects.py` (any Python 3; it imports the helpers of `generate_planet_objects.py`, whose `main` runs only as a script, so the surface meshes are not written again) to `Saved/HexCaveObjects`, and `import_cave_objects.py`, run with the commandlet after `create_planet_assets.py underground`, imports them to `/Game/HexPlanet/Objects/Cave` with CPU access and two LODs (`LODS` of the script, `default`, or `small`, which keeps more of the triangles and switches later, for the meshes of many small parts, as the generator marks them in the `lods` of its manifest) and creates the sets of `SETS` and `RIM_SETS` that are missing, which then belong to the artist; `reset=<Id>,<Id>` after the file name sets every value of those sets again from the script, for a set nobody tuned by hand (it alone imports no mesh). A cover takes its density by its rank in the cells it gets (`GenerateDensityShares`, 0.4 dense and 0.75 dense or medium, read to a millionth so the files of before come out byte-identical), lights `GlowPoolByDensity` of its `GlowPool` by density, and a rim that hangs needs a face steeper than `RimHangMaxUp`. The caps of mushrooms glow most at their rims and on spots and least on their crowns (vertex alpha), at `GlowStrength` 0.7, so their domes read and not flat discs, and the crystals are teal, two clusters a cell of many sizes off its middle (`Reach` 0.8), at 0.9. On the machine it was made on at 1280x720, with the review fixes of 2026-11-09, covers and decor cost about 0.12 ms of GPU at Terra exit 112 at 1500 (3.12 / 3.00 ms with `hex.PlanetObjects` 1, 0) and 0.18 ms of the render thread; the whole frame drew 786 meshes there (`ProfileGPU`, 250 of them shadow depths since the rim spires cast none). New placement takes its up from `AHexPlanet::GetUpAt` (away from the center today), so a view from inside the underground that turns the layer turns what stands on it too.
- The chasms have rims: kinds of `EHexObjectLayer::Rim`, which no file stores and nothing paints, generates, lists at the cursor, blocks or tints the canopy with (`Load` takes an Id of one in `objects.json` as unknown). The sets, Underground only, sort after every cover and decor (310 to 340), so the indices of `objects.json` stay: `CaveRim` (low broken rocks, 2 to 4 a side, no shadow), `CaveRimSpires` (sharp spires, 1 to 2 a side, no shadow since 2026-11-09, which took about 55 shadow draws off the render thread), `CaveStalactites` (`bRimHang`, 0 to 2 clusters a side down the cliff) and `CaveRimRunes` (a block of worked stone with a hexagon and runes carved into its face over the chasm and its top, on 5% of the sides, glowing faintly as the rune stones, `ExitClearRings` 2). `UHexPlanetObjects::PlaceRims`, called by `PlaceChunk`, places them with every chunk placed, so an edit of the maze, Carve, Generate again or Undo moves them once its rebuild is shown: `HexPlanetUnderground::FindRimSides` lists the sides (cell, K) of the cells of the chunk whose neighbor K lies `RimMinDrop` levels lower in the levels shown, the passage first, neither cell cleared nor within the `ExitClearRings` of the kind of an exit (`GetRingDistances`, kept in `ClearRings`), each side once (the `Underground.Rims` test); `RimSideShare` of them take the kind by a hash of cell, side and Id, and each gets `RimCountMin` to the first of `CountByDensity`, spread along it by a stream of the same hash but for `RimCornerGap` of it at each corner (8%), so they stand where they stood. One that stands lies on the lip, up to `RimInset` of the apothem in, so near the side it overhangs, X along the side within `RimTurn`, -Y over the void, leaning by `SlopeAlign` and `Tilt`, and keeps its footprint off a side towards a cleared cell; one that hangs finds the face of the cliff of the void `RimHangOut` past the side, takes its plane up to the height of the lip, found on the passage, and hangs along -Z down the face in front of it (+Y its normal), skipped where no face is found or it is not steep (2% of them on Terra). `SetClearCells` places again the chunks of every cell within `RimClearRings` + 1 of an entrance that came or went, though they store nothing. They draw with `M_HexPlanetCave` by vertex colors, grey-brown rock as the stalagmites, as `M_HexPlanetBoulder` draws the basalt of the cliff without the tint of its layer (`DA_HexTerrainLayers`) and they came out nearly black against the lit lip; the first custom data is still the rock layer of the void. Their meshes are two a kind in `generate_cave_objects.py` (`RimRock`, `RimSpire`, `Stalactites`, `RuneBlock`), as each mesh is a component and draws in every chunk with passages over voids (four, three and three of them drew about 300 more mesh draws on Terra, two about 200); `import_cave_objects.py` makes the `RIM_SETS` that are missing, and words after the file name (`-script="<file> RimRock RimSpire"`) import only the meshes whose names hold one, so those imported before stay. Terra has 40,621 of them on 7,368 sides, Islet 6,510 on 1,182, placed with the cave objects in about 3 ms more. `hex.PlanetRims 0` hides them (`UHexPlanetObjects::SetShown`, with `hex.PlanetObjects`), `hex.PlanetRimSides <Cell> [Rings]` logs which sides of the cells around a cell take each kind and why one takes none, and every rebuild underground logs the rims by kind and the hangs that found no cliff. At 1280x720 on the machine it was made on they cost about 0.07 ms of GPU at Terra exit 112 at 1500 (2.97 / 2.90 ms with `hex.PlanetRims` 1 and 0), 0.06 ms at 4000 and nothing measurable at Islet 1333 at 1400, and about 0.12 ms of the render thread for about 200 more mesh draws (597 against 400 at 1500). The lips carry walls, as the developer wanted on 2026-11-09 (a game seen from above without a ceiling, the abyss behind them, the walls in the style of stalactites, as high as a mountain of the surface): with `bWalls` (on in `DA_HexUnderground` by a migration) `AHexPlanet::UpdateWalls`, from `ApplySurface`, builds `HexPlanetWalls` (pure, the `Underground.Walls` test) from the levels shown. Its line follows the sides of the passages towards a void, keeping the tips of the hexes, and cuts across each corner of a void between two passages from `WallCornerCut` of each of its sides (0 follows the sides, 1 runs from tip to tip; half a side at most where the side ends at another such corner), as if the floor went on into that imaginary hex, so the walls still show the hexes they stand on, as the developer asked; every corner is rounded within `WallCornerRound`, and the line is sampled every `WallStep`. Where a wall runs straight over a row of hexes its protrusions, the cut corners of the voids, and its hollows, the tips of the hexes between them, alternate every cell, which the developer found showed a pattern on 2026-11-10, so each corner takes a cut of its own within `WallCornerCutVariation` of `WallCornerCut` by a hash of the corner, never past half a side unless `WallCornerCut` is, and `WallBridgeShare` of the hollows between two cut corners carry a canopy by a hash of the tip: the top of the inner face reaches from `WallBridgeFillMin` to all of the way across the hollow towards the chord between the protrusions on both sides (`FSample::TopPosition` and `Bridge`, the samples from where the curve of the corner before begins to where that of the one after ends taking the point of the chord as far along it as they lie along the line, so the rows keep their order), its underside going back to the face from under the overhang down to `WallBridgeUnder` of the height (`FLocal::FromTop`), while the foot stays on the sides of the hex, so seen from above the top of a wall runs on over two or three cells in places. The wall stands on the void side of the line, `WallHeight` (170) high, higher or lower by a noise, and `WallThickness` (75) thick at the floor: its inner face rises from a rounded foot of `WallFillet` (45, five rows) that reaches `WallFootReach` (14) onto the floor and, where the line cut a corner, from a flat floor out to where the floor of the passage begins (`FSample::Gap`, found against the polygons of the cells, `FindGap`), leans back by `WallLean` and bulges out by `WallOverhang` at its top, from which stalactites hang: ribs of a cellular pattern of the place (`WallRibSize`, so an edit leaves the stalactites of the walls it does not reach as they were) that stand out up to `WallRibDepth`, narrow towards tips between `WallRibTipMin` and `WallRibTipMax` of the height, `WallColumnShare` of them reaching the floor as columns, with flutes `WallFluteDepth` deep scooped down the face between them, as with a spoon; its crest rises and falls by `WallCrestJag` over each stalactite, dips by `WallCrestNotch` between them and has sharp knobs (`WallCrestNoise`), and the heights of all rows follow one curve from the height of the wall at the floor to its crest at the top (`GetRowHeight`), so the rows keep their order while the crest stays above 0.7 of the height. The walls differ along a passage by styles (`WallStyles`, `FHexWallStyle`: factors on the height, thickness, lean, overhang, bulges, crest and stalactites, and how much the stone is flowstone and how much it shows its joints), each holding in regions of a noise of the place about `WallStyleScale` across and blending where they meet (`WallStyleSharpness`); the defaults are a curtain of stalactites, smooth flowstone in long drapes, broken rock with a ragged crest and organ pipes, a row of columns (`MakeDefaultWallStyles`), and the third UV carries the flowstone and the joints of a vertex to the material. Within them the lip changes in runs of a few cells along the walls by a noise of the place `WallLipScale` across (`FParams::LipScale`, as the developer asked on 2026-11-10, when a low wall seen from above waved alike all along a passage): plain below `WallLipPlain`, its stalactites, flutes and overhang nearly gone, bold above `WallLipBold`, its stalactites `WallLipBoldDepth` times as deep and jutting out `WallLipBoldOverhang` as a ledge even on a parapet, and broken between them, `WallLipBrokenShare` of its stalactites gone by a hash of each; the width of the stalactites and the thickness of the wall change by up to `WallRibWidthVariation` and `WallThicknessVariation` in runs of their own, narrower stalactites shallower and rising less on the crest, so the faces keep their slopes. The outer face goes down `WallOuterDepth` into the chasm from the line smoothed over `WallOuterSmooth` each way, and the top of the crest lies between its inner shoulder, from the line, and its outer shoulder, from the smoothed line, as an offset of a line between the two folded where they part at a corner; inside a turn each row reaches at most about the radius of the sharpest turn within two samples of the line it stands from (`FSample::InnerReach` and the rest), so the wall does not fold (287 of 952,224 triangles in the test still face in, mostly at knobs of the crest, 253 of 937,488 before the canopies and the jitter of the heights and 63 before the runs of the lip). Each chunk has two `UHexPlanetMeshComponent`s, not in ray tracing and culled past `WallCullDistance` (20,000, so the globe from orbit shows the walls as rims along the maze): a coarse mesh (`WallCoarseChunks`: every third sample and 14 of the 25 rows of a sample, `CoarseStride` and `CoarseRows` of `HexPlanetWalls.cpp`, about a fifth of the triangles), built for every chunk, and the whole one (`WallChunks`) only near the camera, as the pieces of the surface: `AHexPlanet::UpdateWallLod` shows each chunk every tick by the distance of its bounds from the view target, the whole walls where all of it is nearer than `WallLodBand` short of `WallLodDistance`, the coarse ones where all of it is further than `WallLodBand` past it, and both where it reaches into that band (`WallLodStates`), with `WallNearMaterial` and `WallFarMaterial`, copies of the material with `WallLodSide` 1 and 2 whose masks (in `WALL_FADE_CODE`) keep complementary pixels by a noise that changes every frame, so one dissolves into the other. The shadow pass draws both whole (`ShadowReplace`), so in the band only the whole walls cast shadows (`SetCastShadow` of the coarse ones): until 2026-11-10 the coarse walls, a little in front of the whole ones in places, shaded them in stripes and the floor at their foot, a shadow that vanished as soon as a turn of the camera took the chunk out of the band (Small, 3.3152 13.7266 at headings -68.2 and -65.2). `hex.PlanetWallLod` 1, 2 and 3 draw every chunk whole, in the band or coarse, and `log LogHexHero Verbose` logs each change of a chunk. A chunk whose nearest point comes within `WallStreamAhead` past the band has its whole walls built in the background (`UE::Tasks`, `WallStream`, the 8 nearest at a time, shown by `FinishWallStream`, which drops what an older build of the walls made, `WallGeneration`), one further than `WallStreamKeep` past that frees them, a chunk that still lacks them draws its coarse walls, the tasks share the walls they build from (`TSharedRef<FWalls>`), and a hidden layer frees all of them (`DropWallWholes`). An edit builds again only the chunks whose hash of samples changed (`HashChunk`, which takes the samples from three before to four after each, with their pools and reaches), the coarse mesh always and the whole one only near the camera.
