A castle that is chosen has a ring on the ground around its walls in `RingColor` of `DA_HexHeroes`, as a hero has (the color of its owner until 2026-11-12), the hero in it none of its own (`UpdateRing`, `BuildRing`, which lays a band of a width around any point: `RingRadius`, `RingWidth` and `RingSegments` of `DA_HexCastles`). A hero sent from the castle it guards onto the cell of a hero beside that castle (`FindSallyTarget`, `Sally`, from `Send`) does not walk there, as the developer decided on 2026-11-11: one of its own player swaps places with it, for no points, as when that one is sent into the castle, and one of another player attacks the castle, the hero staying in it, for the points of the step out: the army of that one is weighed against the castle and its guard as in any attack on a castle (`UHexCastles::Visit`), so a stronger one takes it, which is Claude's reading of "це атака замку" until there are battles; the way shown then is a mark on that cell, and the panel says what a second click does. A left click on a hero of its own chooses that hero, so the swap is ordered with the right button. The castle chosen is named over itself with the hero in it (`SHexHeroPanel::OnPaint`), and the panel of the heroes lists the castles of the person under its heroes (`OnCastleChosen`, `ShowCastle`: a click chooses one and shows it, a click on the one chosen opens it). `SHexCastlePanel` opens on two clicks on a castle, on the key that sends a hero while a castle is chosen (`WalkHero`), and when a hero goes into a castle; then the camera comes to `PanelCameraDistance` of the castle, looking at the ground so far beside it that the castle shows at `CastleScreenX` (0.3) of the width of the screen (`OnCastleOpened`, `AHexPlanetCameraPawn::FocusOn`), and the panel stands with its middle at `PanelCenterX` (0.66), the castle left and the panel right since the developer had them swap sides on 2026-11-11. Anything done with the mouse outside it closes it (a press of any button, in `PlayerTick`), as do the keys of the heroes and the turn, and under it nothing of the planet is picked (`IsUnderCursor`, the cell under the cursor is none): it lists the buildings with what the next level of each costs, why it cannot be built and a button that builds it, the creatures offered with their numbers and buttons that hire one or all, into the hero beside the castle or the garrison, and the stacks of the garrison and of that hero with buttons that move them across; it is made again when a castle, an army or what the player has changed (`GetRevision`). A castle is nearly round and has no ground of its own, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 (it was a trefoil on a rise before): it stands on the ground of its cells and follows it, every piece of it stood on the ground where it lies (`UHexCastles::PlaceOn`, `MakeGround`, `FindGroundRadius`, which reads the triangles of each cell once), upright and reaching under its foot (`SKIRT` of the generator). Its wall is `WallSegments` (36) stretches of a mesh along a circle of `WallRadius` (1.35) radii of a cell around the corner its cells share, counted from `GateAngle` so every gate has the same gap, but for those within `GateHalfWidth` (12) degrees of a gate; it has `NumGates` (3) gates evenly around it from `GateAngle`, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 for ways in ("може 3 достатньо?"), `TowerCounts` towers evenly between them and the flag of its owner over its walls (`FlagMesh`, `FlagPlace`); `WallMeshes`, `GateMeshes` and `TowerMeshes` are by the level of the fort: a fence before one is built, a palisade, a stone wall with six towers, a higher one with slimmer and higher towers, their crowns standing out on corbels, at nine places, of which the three in the gates are left out. Those towers, the towers of its gates, the turrets of the great hall and the pillars of the last dwelling had pointed roofs until the developer found a castle had too many roofs on 2026-11-11 ("башенки апгрейдів - зробити меншими/тоншими чи зміненою геометрією - забагато криш"); the houses, the gatehouses, one tower of a hall and the spire of the cloister keep theirs. Each building that is built is an instance of its mesh at its place (`Place`, `Yaw` and `Scale` of `FHexBuilding`, in radii of a cell from that corner, and `HeightScale`, 1, which would stretch its masonry): the hall against the wall between two gates, facing the middle, and the dwellings against the wall in the three stretches between the gates, each as far out as its depth lets it (`MakeHall`, `MakeDwelling` of `HexCastleConfig.cpp`), so the middle stays an open yard, to which every gate leads straight and where the hero that guards the castle stands and shows (the hall stood in the middle at first on 2026-11-11 and hid it, then so near it that it reached onto the place of the hero, which the developer found looked stuck on: "якщо майданчик по центру замка - значить будівлі мають бути по краям"), on a paved round with stone posts that marks its place, as the developer asked the same day (`GuardMesh`, `SM_PlanetCastleYard`, at `GuardPlace`, the hero `GuardLift` above the ground on it); the mesh of the highest level built that names one, so each level of the hall is a larger hall, each dwelling a shape of its own, and a dwelling that was upgraded has a pole with a pennant beside it (`UpgradeMesh`). A hero goes in and out through the gate nearest it (`OpenGate`, from `Visit` and from `UHexHeroes::StartNextStep`; while any hero walks through a gate the game moves no hero on and ends no turn, `UpdateAi`, as until 2026-11-12 it went on `AiThinkTime` later, sent the hero on or gave the turn back, and the developer saw an enemy go into castles at once), as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 ("ворота відкрились, герой зайшов, ворота закрились"): the ninth custom value of the instance of that gate is when it opened (`FHexCastle::GateTimes`, the seconds of the world), by which the material swings its two leaves into the yard by `GateSwing` degrees over `GateRiseTime`, holds them for `GateHoldTime` and shuts them, and the hero, setting out `GateWalkDelay` of `GateRiseTime` later, walks from where it stood through the gate to the middle of the yard over `GateWalkTime` (`FHexHero::bGliding`, `GlideStart`, `GlidePoints`, placed every frame by `PlaceHero`), the one it swaps with walking out the same way, and a hero that leaves waits for the leaves and takes its first step from the yard through the gate (`GateStepFrom`); only where the person sees it, a hero out of sight being in at once. The straight way from a cell to a gate may brush a tower. The way shown into a castle ends on the place of its hero in the yard, through the gate nearest, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 (`FindGate`, in `UpdatePath`; its mark lay on the middle of the cell of the castle that was clicked before); a left click on a castle of the person still chooses it, so a hero is sent into it with the right button. It is drawn with `M_HexPlanetCastle` (`Material`; the graph of the sites, `build_site_graph` with `castle`): the flag and the pennants in the color of its owner, the flag flashing when the castle is taken, and of the roofs (the vertex alpha of about 0.5) only those of the hall, as the developer found too much of the color of the player on a castle on 2026-11-11, when every roof took it ("давай кріші в якийсь колір реальний, і тальки флаг, та може ратуша чи башенки на стінах - приймають колір гравця"): the other roofs keep the color of their clay, `RoofPlainTone` times as light as the scan and tinted by the style of the faction (`RoofHumanTint`, `RoofUndeadTint`, that of the undead dark), which a roof is told by the V of its UV, from `PLAIN_ROOF_V` (2) up, and under it for a roof of the owner (`own` of `add_roof`, `add_cone`, `add_house` and `add_tower` of the generator); the roofs as tiles, as they asked later that day in place of a plain color ("текстура черепиці, мох чи щось таке"): `T_CastleRoof` by the UV of the meshes, along the eaves and up the slope in tiles of `ROOF_TILE` units, the color of the owner as light as the scan of the tiles is (`RoofTone`, with `RoofTextureShare` of the colors of the scan, 0, and `RoofRelief` of its normal); they are clay tiles in channels, as the slates in courses of a first try looked like brick to the developer, who did not like the moss that try had either, so there is none, and its stone (the alpha of about 0.1) as masonry by the UV of its meshes, in tiles of `TILE` units, of the style of its faction (`FHexFaction::WallStyle`, the eighth custom value of an instance): that of men, courses of warm limestone blocks, for the Order of Dawn, and that of the undead, dark slates tinted darker and greener, for the Realm of Decay, each times its tint (`WallHumanTint`, `WallUndeadTint`), `WallTone` light and with `WallRelief` of their normal. The developer found the castles one tone all over, without shade, and the masonry too much of a pattern on 2026-11-11 ("в кутах темніше, на відкритих ділянках світліше - зараз занадто монотонне"), so the stone is shaded by the material and by the meshes (`Look|Shade` of `DA_HexCastles`): the scan is drawn `WallContrast` of the way from its own mean, the same texture at a coarse mip (`CASTLE_MEAN_MIP`), in patches of tone of the place `WallPatchSize` across (`WallPatchTone`, warmer or cooler by `WallWarmColor` and `WallCoolColor`), each piece lighter or darker by `WallPieceTone`; it is darker by `WallFootShade` at the ground, up to `WallFootHeight` of its mesh, and lighter by `WallTopLight` towards `WallTopHeight`, by where a pixel lies in its mesh (the position of the vertices before any offset, through a vertex interpolator); and a stretch of the wall is darker by `WallCornerShade` where it meets a tower or a gate, as a corner is: `UHexCastles` gives each stretch how near its two ends are to one as the tenth and eleventh custom values of its instance (`GetCorner`: `WallCornerInset` degrees around a tower, or the edge of a gate, fading over `WallCornerReach` degrees), which the material blends along the stretch (`WallCornerSpan`). The meshes bake the rest into the tone of their vertices: a tower is darker where the wall runs into it, up to the height of the wall (`JUNCTION_SHADE`, the `junction` of `stone_cylinder`), plinths are darker (`PLINTH_TONE`) and merlons, crowns and string courses lighter (`TRIM_TONE`), each stretch of the wall has a plinth and a buttress, a tower a plinth that spreads and a course two thirds up, a house a plinth and eaves that reach `EAVES` out over its long walls, and a roof is darker at its eaves than at its ridge. Where stone meets stone in a corner dirt has gathered, as the developer asked the same day for the merlons of the towers and other corners ("наче грязь скопилась від дощу за роки"): the foot of every merlon, the top of a wall under its merlons and of a tower under its crown, the top of a buttress, the walls of a house under its eaves and the gatehouse where it meets its towers are `GRIME_SHADE` as light and their own tone again `GRIME_BAND` further (`foot` and `top` of `stone_box`, which builds such a box in bands, `banded_box`, and `grime` of `stone_cylinder`), and the material draws streaks the rain left down the stone, a noise of the mesh stretched along its height (`WallStreakShade`, `WallStreakWidth`, `WallStreakLength`). The fires, embers and lit windows of the undead burn in `UndeadGlowColor`, a pale green. The cost of all this is not measured. The masonry is photo-scanned, as the developer asked and allowed to download on 2026-11-11, as long as it is light ("текстури мають бути не важкі"): two CC0 sets of Poly Haven at 1K, `medieval_blocks_05` and `castle_wall_slates`, and for the roofs the clay tiles `clay_roof_tiles_02` (`Tools/HexPlanetCastles/castle_texture_sources.json`), which `fetch_castle_textures.ps1` downloads to `Saved/TextureSources/castle` (not committed) and `import_planet_castles.py textures` imports as `T_CastleWall_Human`, `T_CastleWall_Undead`, `T_CastleRoof` and their normals, about 5.5 MB of assets together; without the downloads it imports the masonry `generate_castle_textures.py` generates (run with Blender for its numpy). A castle is alive, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11, by what the material moves, which the meshes mark with a V of 4 or more in the UV of a vertex (`SITE_OFFSET_CODE`): the leaves of the gates (plain vertices, U how far from the hinge along Y, signed), the cloth of the flags and of the pennants on the towers and halls (U how far from the pole, V 7 and a hundredth of how long it is), which is laid along a wind that blows east over the ground and veers by `FlagVeer` degrees, so the flags of a castle look one way, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 (each flew the way its mesh was turned before), and waves by `FlagWave` at `FlagWaveSpeed`, and the flames of the braziers beside every gate and before the halls (what glows by itself, U how far up), which dance by `FlameDance`; what glows by itself in a castle, its fires and its lit windows and slits, is `FireGlow` bright and flickers by `FireFlicker`, and embers in `EmberColor` rise from the fires, sparkles as those of the piles. The meshes are placeholders that both factions share, from `Tools/HexPlanetCastles/generate_planet_castles.py` (`Saved/HexPlanetCastles`), ordinary static meshes that an artist can rework or replace, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11: each is named in `DA_HexCastles` (`WallMeshes`, `GateMeshes`, `TowerMeshes`, `GuardMesh`, `FlagMesh`, `UpgradeMesh` and the `Mesh` of each level of a building), so a mesh under another name stays when the import runs again, which writes over the generated ones; one that is to be drawn by `M_HexPlanetCastle` keeps its conventions (the alpha of a vertex 0.1 for stone, 0.3 for what glows, 0.5 for roofs, 1 for the flag, the tone of a vertex as its baked shade, the UV in tiles of `TILE` units, a V of 4 or more for what moves), and `Material` of the config is one material for all of a castle: a hero, 2.5 times its mesh, is about 125 units high, and since the developer found the castle low on 2026-11-11 ("висота стін замала, замок має відчуватись потужніше, героя у ньому має бути видно - але десь такої ж висоти") the walls are about as high as a rider (a fence 24, a palisade about 50, stone 69 and 85 with their merlons), the towers 96 and about 130 with their crowns, the gatehouses 104 and 124 (the roof over the way of the higher one lay across the wall until 2026-11-11, as the length of a roof runs along its own X, which its turn lays along the wall), and the halls and dwellings are stretched `HEIGHT` (1.8) times above the ground after they are modelled (`stretched`, their masonry keeping the size of its blocks). In a castle of the highest fort seen from low and close, as when its panel opens, the towers and roofs hide much of the hero in its yard. `import_planet_castles.py` runs in three steps: with the argument `textures` it imports the masonry, then `create_planet_assets.py heroes` builds the material, which needs the module built, as it reads the defaults of `UHexCastleConfig`, then without an argument it imports the meshes. Small has a castle of the Order of Dawn for Red, one of the Realm of Decay for Blue and one of nobody. Their GPU cost is not measured. Not built: the mage guild, the market, hiring heroes, abilities of creatures, splitting a stack, castles the generation places underground, and saving what a castle holds.
- Battles (`Source/HexHero/Battle`), built on 2026-11-12 when the developer said "починаєм робити битви" and sent the line-ups of the Castle and the Necropolis of Heroes III as references. Their answers, all built: a first build with everything at once (the field, the turns by speed, walking, melee with a strike back, waiting, defending, shooting, obstacles, morale, luck, the spells of the hero, the abilities of the creatures); the roles of the levels of those line-ups with our own names (those of `DA_HexCastles`) and numbers, as a one-to-one copy of Heroes III is not wanted; CC0 models; a world of its own; a starter set of spells for every hero until the mage guild; hero against hero, against a castle and against neutral stacks of the map (below); the loser leaves the map; the person fights by hand, with Auto, Quick and Retreat. Every value is in `DA_HexBattle` (`UHexBattleConfig`, `Content/HexPlanet/Battle`, which `Tools/HexBattle/create_battle_assets.py` creates when it is missing, so tune it by hand); the numbers, the formulas, which creature has which ability and the spells are Claude's.
  - The rules are `FHexBattle` (`HexBattle.h`), pure, deterministic from the seed of a battle (the `HexHero.Battle.Queue`, `Damage`, `Abilities`, `Spells` and `Quick` tests): a field of `Columns` x `Rows` (15 x 11) pointy-top hexes (`FHexCoord`, odd-r offset), the attacker on the first column and the defender on the last, up to as many stacks a side as there are rows, then a second column, `ObstaclesMin` to `ObstaclesMax` obstacles between them that always leave a way from one edge to the other. Each round the stacks act from the fastest to the slowest (spells change the speed), the attacker first at equal speed, then by their places in the armies, and those that waited after all the others, the slowest first (`GetRoundOrder`, `GetQueue`). A stack walks up to its speed in hexes around obstacles and stacks, or flies over them onto a free hex (`FHexCreature::bFlies`), strikes a stack beside it from a hex it reaches, which strikes back once a round, shoots while it has shots and no enemy stands beside it (half the damage past `ShotFarHexes`, a shooter in melee `MeleePenaltyShare` of it), waits once a round, or defends (`DefendShare` more Defense until its next turn). The damage of a strike is a roll between the least and the most damage of a creature for each creature, ten rolls at most, scaled to their number, more by `AttackBonusPerPoint` for each point of Attack above the Defense of the one struck (up to `MaxAttackBonus`) and less by `DefenseCutPerPoint` for each below (down to `MaxDefenseCut`), the Attack and Defense of a hero added to those of all its stacks and the Defense of the defenders of a castle times the `DefenseFactor` of its fort (`bCastleDefenseFactor`), as long as there are no walls on the field. Morale: an army of one faction has `OneFactionMorale`, each faction more takes `MixedFactionMorale`, plus the morale of the creature (`FHexCreatureStats::Morale`), the undead none; good morale gives another turn after a turn by `MoraleChancePerPoint` a point, bad takes the turn by `BadMoraleChancePerPoint`; luck (`Luck`) doubles a strike by `LuckChancePerPoint`. The abilities are bits of `FHexCreatureStats::Abilities` (`EHexAbility`, in `HexCastleConfig.h`, with `Shots`, `Morale` and `Luck`, which `UHexCastleConfig::PostLoad` gives a config saved before them from the defaults by the Ids while they hold none): `DoubleStrike` (strikes and shoots twice), `RetaliateTwice`, `NoRetaliation`, `NoMeleePenalty`, `AreaShot` (the shot hits the enemies around too), `LifeDrain` (`LifeDrainShare` of the damage heals and raises its own), `Charge` (`ChargeBonusPerHex` more a hex run), `Breath` (the strike goes on into the stack behind), `Undead`, `Regenerate` (its wounded creature heals at the start of its turn), `ManaDrain` (`ManaDrainPerTurn` from the other hero each turn). By role (`With` in `HexCastleConfig.cpp`): the Order of Dawn has a spearman whose upgraded version strikes back twice, a shooter whose upgraded one shoots twice, a flyer that strikes back twice, a swordsman whose upgraded one strikes twice, a mage that shoots and strikes in melee as hard, a rider who charges and a winged guard of good morale, whose upgraded one strikes back twice; the Realm of Decay is all undead: a skeleton, a slow tough zombie, a flying spirit that regenerates and whose upgraded one drains mana, a flying blood drinker nothing strikes back at, whose upgraded one drains life, a mage whose shot hits an area, a dark rider who charges and has luck upgraded, and a bone dragon whose breath goes on. Shooters have 12 shots, 24 upgraded. The hero of a side casts one spell a round for its mana (`Mana`, `Knowledge` times 10, kept after the battle), at the power `Power` + `PowerPerSpellPower` times its Spell Power, an effect for `BaseRounds` + `RoundsPerSpellPower` times it: the starter set every hero knows (`Spells`, `MakeDefaultSpells`): Bolt at an enemy, Blast over a hex and those around it, friend and foe, Mend of the wounded of a stack of its own, Haste, Slow, Blessing (the most damage), Weakness (less Attack) and Ward (less damage taken). A side whose stacks are all dead, or that retreats, loses; after `MaxRounds` the defender keeps the field. The game fights a side by `PlayAi`: its hero casts the spell worth most of the power of the stacks it kills or changes, when that is worth `AiSpellWorth` of its mana; a shooter shoots what it kills most of, a stack strikes what it kills most of minus half of what is struck back, from the nearest hex, and else walks towards the nearest enemy; `PlayToEnd` fights a whole battle so. `FHexBattleEvent`s tell what happened in order, for the field to play (`TakeEvents`).
  - `UHexBattles` (`HexBattles.h`), an object of `UHexHeroes` (`GetBattles`), starts a battle (`Begin` with an `FHexBattleRequest`) where a hero visits a cell (`UHexHeroes::VisitSite`): a neutral stack, a hero of another player out of a castle (also one that guards a mine; `IsFoeAt`, which `FindWay` makes a visit, from the cell beside it), or a castle of another player or of nobody that a garrison or the hero in it defends (its guard's army and the garrison on one side, `GuardSlots`); a castle with nothing in it is taken as before; a castle with a fort is fought for behind its wall (below), and a hero sent from the castle it guards onto a hero of another player beside it is that hero attacking the castle (`Sally`). A battle in which a person fights is shown on its field; the others are fought at once (`PlayToEnd`, logged), also where the person sees them, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 when the game fought neutral stacks in front of them. The result is applied in the next update of the heroes (`ApplyPending`, from `UHexHeroes::Update`, which waits while a battle is shown, so the game, the AI and the walking stand still): what is left of each stack goes back to its army or garrison, the hero that wins gains `ExperiencePerHealth` experience a point of health of what it killed (nothing levels up yet) and keeps the mana it has left, and who lost or retreated leaves the map with its army (`RemoveHero`); a castle that falls is taken by the hero that won (`UHexCastles::Visit`), its garrison gone, and a neutral stack that loses is gone, one that wins keeps what is left. A hero the game moves attacks a hero of another player or a neutral stack whose army its own outweighs `AiAttackMargin` (1.4) times by power, within `AiAttackReachDays` of movement (`MoveAiToSite`), and castles as before.
  - The field, `AHexBattleArena` (`HexBattleArena.h`), is spawned by `AHexPlanetPlayerController` the first time a battle is shown (`OnBattleShown`, `OnBattleClosed`), at `ArenaOrigin` far below the planet: both layers hide with their sky lights and air (`AHexPlanet::SetBattleHidden`, which also stops their streaming for the frame), the camera of the planet waits (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::SetBattleShown`) and the view goes to the camera of the field, which takes the post process of the camera of the planet (`CameraDistance`, `CameraPitch`, `CameraLookShift`, `CameraFieldOfView`; `hex.Battle camera <Distance> <Pitch>` tries others), the sun of the level is borrowed and given back (`SunShare`, `SunColor` of the ground), and the widgets of the planet leave the screen for `SHexBattlePanel`. Its ground is a plane with `M_HexBattleGround` (the terrain layer of the biome fought on from `TA_Biome_Color`, darker past the field and underground), the lines of the hexes and the marks of where the stack whose turn it is reaches, the enemies it can strike or shoot, the hex under the cursor, the hex it strikes from and the area of a spell are procedural meshes with `M_HexBattleMark` (`GridColor`, `ReachColor`, `HoverColor`, `TargetColor`, `ActiveColor`), one translucent section drawn in the order of its triangles; since 2026-11-12, as the developer asked for it as on the planet, the hexes of the field the stack does not reach lie under `ReachOutsideColor`, a see-through black, and the edge of those it reaches has a line `ReachLineWidth` wide in `ReachLineColor` with a light `ReachGlowWidth` wide inside it that fades inwards (`ReachGlowColor`), and the way to the hex under the cursor, or to the hex it strikes from, shows as a way does on the planet, a line through a dot on each hex to a ring on the last, straight for a flyer (`FHexBattle::FindPath`, `PathColor`, `PathLineWidth`, `PathDotRadius`, `PathEndRadius`), the obstacles and what stands around the field are meshes of the planet by the biome (`Grounds`, `FHexBattleGround`, an open field of rocks and trees, the underground of stalagmites and crystals), and a sphere with `M_HexBattleSky` and a sky light of its own make its sky. It plays the events one after the other (`WalkSpeed`, `FlySpeed`, `StrikeTime`, `ShotSpeed`, `DeathTime`, `TurnPause`, the game's turns `AutoSpeed` times as fast while it fights for the person): a figure walks along its way or flies in an arc, turns to and strikes what it strikes, a shot flies, numbers of the damage, the healing, morale and luck float up, the counts over the figures change as it plays (`FHexBattleEvent::CountAfter`), a spell lights its hex, and a stack that is all dead plays its death and lies there as a corpse in its last pose (`HexUnitFigure::Play` with `bAtEnd`, a flyer on the ground), as the developer asked on 2026-11-12, as magic is to raise them one day; a creature without a death of its own goes. The way of a flyer to the hex under the cursor is the arc it flies, as dots in the air, and a ring where it lands. The person's orders: a left click on a hex the stack reaches walks there, on an enemy shoots it or strikes it from the hex beside it nearest the cursor, and with a spell chosen casts it there, the right button keeping the spell, and the person moves the camera of the field, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 (`AHexBattleArena::MoveCamera`, `ZoomCamera`, `DragCamera`, from `AHexPlanetCameraPawn::UpdateBattleCamera`, whose input the field takes while it is shown): the wheel brings it from `CameraMinDistance` to `CameraMaxDistance`, lower towards `CameraClosePitch` near the field, Q and E and the right button held turn it around the point it looks at, and the keys, the edges of the screen and the left or middle button held move that point, at most `CameraScrollReach` hexes from the middle, all eased by `CameraSmoothing`; Wait (Z), Defend (Space), Spell (the starter spells with their mana), Auto (the game fights for the person, and again the person), Quick (the rest at once, the field showing the end) and Retreat, and OK on how it ended (the losses of both sides and the experience) closes the field (`UHexBattles::Close`). The panel shows the sides with the mana of their heroes and the round, at the bottom in the middle whose turn it is and what a click where the cursor is would do (`GetTurnText`, `GetActionText`), as the developer asked on 2026-11-12: a strike or a shot with the damage and the kills it would deal, whether it is far (half the damage) or a shooter in melee, and whether the stack struck dies or strikes back with what it would deal and kill then (`FHexBattle::PreviewStrike`, `FHexStrikePreview::bStrikesBack`, the `HexHero.Battle.Damage` test), a spell with its damage, kills or healing (`PreviewSpell`), a walk or flight with its hexes, or why not; the stacks in the order they act and the log of the last events; and beside the cursor what the stack under it is, in one panel (`SHexBattlePanel::RebuildTooltip`, made again when the stack changes, on the other side of the cursor where it would leave the screen): its number and name in the color of its side, its stats as icons with numbers (Attack, Defense, damage, health of the top creature, Speed, shots, morale, luck; `StatIcons` of `DA_HexUi`, `StatIconSize`), what it can and the spells on it. `hex.Battle <state|move|strike|shoot|wait|defend|cast|hover|camera|turn|auto|quick|retreat|close>` gives the orders from the console, by hexes as column and row (`turn <Degrees> <Notches>` turns and zooms the camera).
  - The figures (`HexUnitFigure`) are skeletal meshes of CC0 models of Quaternius (`Tools/HexBattle/unit_model_sources.json`, which `fetch_unit_models.ps1` downloads to `Saved/ModelSources/units`, about 3.4 MB, not committed; the knight is that of the heroes), built by `Tools/HexBattle/blender_build_units.py` (run with Blender; its argument `preview` renders a frame of each animation to `Saved/BattleUnits/preview`): each `UNITS` entry is a model, the colors of its materials or of its texture at the UV of a corner baked into the colors of its corners, the alpha 1 on what takes the color of its side, colors of its own for some materials (`recolor`), what it carries made and bound to a bone (a spear, a sword, a shield, a bow, a staff, wings), its animations kept under the names the battles read (Idle, Move, Attack, Shoot, Hit, Death), and its height at rest without what it carries measured into `units.json`, as the skeletons of these models are scaled a hundred times and their bounds in the engine say nothing of their size. The Order of Dawn: the knight with a spear and a shield (Sentinel), with a sword and a shield (Oathsworn), with a sword and wings (Sky warden), the hooded adventurer with a bow (Huntsman) and in a robe with a staff (Pilgrim), a small dragon in gold (Glowwing) and the rider of the heroes (Dawn rider); the Realm of Decay: the skeleton (Bone warrior, and darker as the Ash mage), the zombie (Decayed), the ghost (Shade), the adventurer pale and in red with wings (Blood drinker), the dragon in bone (Bone serpent) and the rider dark (Decay rider). `Tools/HexBattle/import_battle_units.py`, run with the commandlet after `create_battle_assets.py` (which also builds `M_HexBattleUnit`), imports them to `/Game/HexPlanet/Battle/Units` (with their animations about 41 MB of assets, the most of the project after the textures), draws them with `M_HexBattleUnit` (the colors of the vertices times `Tint`, the color of the side by their alpha, `SideColorShare`, glowing a little) and gives `DA_HexBattle` the look of each creature that has none (`UnitLooks`, `FHexUnitLook`: the mesh, its height on the field, `MeshHeight` and `MeshFoot` from `units.json`, how high a flyer floats, its turn, -90 for every figure, as all come in facing their -Y as the rider does, which the developer found side on at 0 on 2026-11-12, the tints of the basic and the upgraded version, the animations; `reset` sets them again) and the grounds their meshes while they have none. Every figure is `UnitScale` (1.8) times the height of its look (the Bone warrior and the Ash mage, whose skeleton stands with its foot at its origin in the engine though Blender measured it 163 below, take a foot of 0, `FOOT_OVERRIDES` of `import_battle_units.py`, as they floated until 2026-11-12, and the Blood drinker no longer floats); the figures play the idle of their models, a calm breath, which a run measured on the field and on the map; a creature without a look is a cylinder in the color of its side. The figures of these models are in another style than the rider, and some roles are stand-ins (a small dragon for the flyer of the Order, the adventurer with wings for the blood drinker), as the packs of Quaternius that hold more of them could not be downloaded (their Google Drive gave Quota exceeded); an artist can replace any of them in `UnitLooks`.
  - The cursors of the game (`EHexCursor`, `SHexGameCursor`), as the developer asked on 2026-11-12: Slate draws one at the mouse over every widget (ZOrder 1000) while the cursor of the system is hidden (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::UpdateGameCursor`, `CurrentMouseCursor` None), and draws none where a widget asks for a cursor of its own, as a text box does, nor for a cursor without a picture, when the system cursor shows. On the field of a battle it shows what a click does (`AHexBattleArena::GetCursor`): walk, fly, shoot, shoot from afar at half the damage, strike, a sword turned to point on the screen from the hex the stack strikes from to the one it strikes, cast, or nothing; on the planet while the game is played what a click or sending the hero chosen does (`UHexHeroes::GetOrderAt`, `EOrder`): choose a hero or castle of the person, walk there today or on a later day (by what the way costs against the points left), no way, attack (a neutral stack, a hero of another player, a castle that is defended, a sally), go into a castle or take one nobody defends, visit a mine or mill or take a pile, go through the entrance whose mouth is under the cursor, or swap with a hero of its own; the default arrow over the panels, the menu and the cheat sheet. Every cursor and icon is animated, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12: its picture is a strip of `Frames` frames played at `FramesPerSecond` (`UHexUiConfig::MakeFrameBrushes`, `GetFrame`), a horse that gallops for walking in place of the footprints, which seemed to point a way, swords that clash, a waving flag, a swirl that turns, a heart that beats and so on. Every picture is in `DA_HexUi` (`UHexUiConfig`, `Content/HexPlanet/UI`, `Cursors`: a texture, its hot spot and its size), drawn by `Tools/HexUi/generate_ui_icons.py` (the Python of Blender, for numpy; shapes as signed distances, drawn at twice their size) to `Saved/HexUi` and imported by `import_ui_icons.py` with the commandlet (`reset` sets the lists again), about 0.4 MB, placeholders an artist replaces in the asset. `hex.Cursor` logs the cursor chosen and that of the system, as `hex.Mouse` moves the mouse of the game but not the cursor Slate draws at, and `Shot showui` leaves it out.
  - Cheats for trying the game, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 (`HexCheats`, `Source/HexHero/Hero`): `hex.Cheat reveal [Player]` explores both layers, the passages underground, for the person who plays (`UHexHeroes::RevealAll`), and `hex.Cheat enemies [on|off]` marks every neutral stack and hero of another player on the layer shown with a line up from its cell and its name and number over it (`SHexEdgeMarkers`, `EnemyMarkHeight`, `NeutralMarkColor`), under the fog of war too but not behind the curve of the planet. `hex.Cheat passive [on|off]`, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13, makes the side of the game in the battle the person fights now attack nobody: every turn of its stacks and towers is a defence and its hero casts nothing (`FHexBattle::SetPassiveSide`, read by `PlayAi`, so Quick keeps it too), for that battle alone, as `Start` clears it; its stacks still strike back by the rules (the `HexHero.Battle.Passive` test). `hex.Cheat help`, `hex.Cheat` alone and `hex.Cheat.Help` log every cheat and command for trying the game (`HexCheats::GetLines`), and Ctrl+H (`IA_HexCheatSheet`, a line of the legend under Menus) or `hex.CheatSheet` shows the same over the screen in a see-through black (`SHexCheatSheet`, `CheatSheetOpacity`). `hex.Hero` takes a cell as `near:<Steps>[:<Side>]` from the hero chosen too, as `hex.Neutral` does.
  - A castle with a fort is fought for behind its wall, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 (`FHexBattleSiege` of `FHexBattleSetup`, from `UHexBattles::Begin`, `FHexBattle::PlaceSiege`, the `HexHero.Battle.Siege` test): the wall stands `SiegeWallFromEnd` (4) columns before the last across every row, its gate in the middle row and its towers on `TowerRows`, as many as `TowersByFort` gives the fort, symmetric since the developer asked on 2026-11-13 (none in the base fort, two at the ends of the wall in a citadel, and two more beside the gate in a castle; one and three before), each section, the gate and a tower as strong as `WallHealthByFort`, `GateHealthByFort` and `TowerHealthByFort` say, so a castle built up has stronger walls (`FHexBattleWall`, `EHexWallPart`). Since 2026-11-13, as the developer asked ("стіна замка - потрібна пряма, вода перед нею"), the wall runs straight across the field (`AHexBattleArena::GetWallLinePoint`: through the middle of what the hexes of its column share, as odd rows shift half a hex, and `WallEndReach` of a row past the first and the last) and a moat lies before it (`FHexBattleSiege::bMoat`, `bSiegeMoat` of `DA_HexBattle`, `FHexBattle::GetMoat`, `IsMoat`): the hexes of the column before the wall, a line no walker crosses in one turn, but for the hex before the gate, the only one from which the gate is reached, where a bridge of planks goes over it (`GetBridge`, `BridgeWidth`); a step of a walker into the moat costs `MoatStepCost` (2) of its speed, half its speed in the water, as the developer asked the same day ("штраф на мув 50%"; it ended the walk at first, which was Claude's, after Heroes III) (`FindReach`, by fronts of equal cost), a flyer flies over it, and it harms nobody. Its water is part of the ground of the biome, as the developer asked the same day (a dark strip along the whole wall looked cut off, and then the water was not under the wall, its waves did not read as water and it had no foam): it lies from under the front wall to its face (`FSiegeShape::WallFace`, by the thickness of the mesh of the wall) out to the far sides of the hexes of the moat, which zigzag by half a hex from row to row, each corner rounded by a share of its own up to `MoatCornerCut` of a hex and the line drawn towards a straight one by chance, so it shows no pattern, winding by `MoatShoreNoise` of a hex, and `MoatEndReach` of a row past the corners (`MOAT_SHORE_CODE` of `create_battle_assets.py`, which `M_HexBattleGround` and `M_HexBattleWater` share, from `MoatRows` and `MoatWall` of `SetMoatParameters`; it takes the rows of the field along X and the way in along Y, as the field lies); the ground draws its bed (`MoatBedColor`) and wet banks (`MoatBankColor`, `MoatBankWidth`), its ends round off over `MoatEndRound` of a hex, raggedly and each its own way, and over it lies dark see-through water (`M_HexBattleWater`, translucent and unlit, lighting itself by the sun of the field, `SunDir`, the sky, `MoatSkyColor` and `MoatSkyReflection`, and a glint, `MoatGlint`, `MoatLight` bright, as a lit translucent surface took the light of the coarse volume of the translucency of Lumen; on a lattice `MoatLift` above the ground, which must stay above `MoatWaveHeight`, as the troughs of its waves under the ground drew round holes in a lattice in it, the circles the developer saw on 2026-11-13; clearer at the shore and up to `MoatOpacity` in the middle, its ripples two layers of `T_HexNoise` drifting), with foam in a line along the shore and a thinner one where it meets the wall, as wide as a second noise that drifts says (`MoatFoamColor`, `MoatFoamWidth`, `MoatWallFoamWidth`, broken by `T_HexNoise`; a sine along the wall drew a thick wave that ran along it until 2026-11-13) and, on a band of the mesh up the face of the wall (its second UV 2 and more), water that washes up the wall `MoatSplashHeight` and falls back (`MakeMoatWater`); `create_battle_assets.py water ground` builds the two alone, and the hint at the bottom says that a step into it costs double. A standing part is no way for anyone but the gate, which opens for the defenders, whose units go out and in through it, and stays open for good once a unit is killed in it or it falls (`IsPassable`, `IsGateJammed`); a part that falls is rubble anyone walks over. Each tower holds `TowerWeeksByFort` weeks of the shooters of the lowest level of the faction that shoot, part of the castle (`FHexBattleUnit::bTower`): they never move, shoot whoever stands beside their wall too, are no target of strikes, shots or spells, and die with their tower. A stack of the attacker that fights in melee strikes a part beside a hex it reaches, `WallStrikeShare` of what it would deal a creature, and nothing strikes back (`CanStrikeWall`, `StrikeWall`). The catapult, which a hero buys in a castle for `CatapultCost` of `DA_HexCastles` (`UHexCastles::BuyCatapult`, War machines in the panel of a castle, `FHexHero::bCatapult`, `hex.Cheat catapult [Hero]`), stands in a corner behind the attacker, `CatapultSpeed` fast in the order of turns, and shoots at a part of the wall anywhere (`CanFireCatapult`, `FireCatapult`), hitting with `CatapultHitChance` for `CatapultDamageMin` to `CatapultDamageMax`; the person aims it by a click, the game shoots at the gate first, then the towers, then the sections nearest the middle, and its stacks at the wall strike the gate first (`ChooseCatapultTarget`, `ChooseWallStrike`). It has `CatapultHealth`, anyone may strike or shoot it, and one destroyed is lost to its hero. The machines count for neither side's victory nor go back into an army. On the field the wall is the meshes of the castle by its fort (`WallMeshes`, `GateMeshes`, `TowerMeshes` of `DA_HexCastles`, in its material and masonry, `SiegeWallHeight` high) along that straight line, the gate, since the developer found it too narrow and of stone on 2026-11-13 and then asked for it and the bridge more real, of the shape of an arch and Gothic: an opening `GateOpening` hex radii wide (2, the longest diagonal of a hex) between two towers `GateTowerScale` of a tower, under a pointed arch in a wall of the castle (a static mesh the field builds, `FPropMesh::Build`, drawn as an instance of the material of the castle with its custom values, its stone marked as the meshes of the castle mark it and its UV in tiles of `CastleTile`, as thick and high as the wall itself, `ArchThickShare` and `ArchTopShare` of the bounds of the mesh of a wall, or `GateArchCrown` above the point of the arch where that rises higher, as a gatehouse, with merlons, its stone of the tone of the meshes of the castle (`CastleStoneTone`, STONE of the generator; it was twice as light until the developer saw it), as the developer found it of another stone and thicker than the walls when the props drew it; its sides circles `GateArchRadius` of half the opening springing `GateSpringShare` of the wall up), with two leaves of photo-scanned timber whose tops follow the arch, bound with iron (`GateWoodTexture`, `GateWoodColor`, `GateIronColor`, `GateLeafThickness`), that swing `GateSwing` degrees into the yard while a defender goes through or stands in it (`UpdateGate`, `GateSwingSpeed`), seen from above where the gatehouse of the castle hid them, rubble where a part fell, the shooters on top of their towers, `TowerFigureScale` of their size and `TowerStandShare` of the way up the mesh of the tower (`TowerFigureLift` until 2026-11-13, when the developer found them too large and hanging in the air), a tower at an end of the wall in its corner (`GetWallPartPoint`). The castle is closed, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13, so it reads as a castle and not as a wall standing in a field: from the corners of the front wall, `WallEndReach` of a row past its ends and outside the field, its walls turn back past the last column, `BackWallReach` hexes beyond its middles, where a palace closes it, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13 for something like a guarded palace and for side walls that go on, so the yard reads as the front of a castle: the side walls go `SideWallBeyond` hexes on past it, and at the back of the yard the hall of the castle in the mesh of its level built stands on `PalaceSteps` steps `PalaceStepRise` high of the masonry of the castle (`StepsColor`) in an opening of the back wall, the dwellings built beside it behind that wall, outwards by turns (`HallScale`, `HallYaw`), and from a citadel on towers at the corners of the palace and a keep behind the hall, a tower `KeepScale` times as large; none of them is part of the rules, and no decor stands in the castle. The sun of the field stands at `SunPitch` and `SunYaw` in a siege too, as the developer asked when a sun turned for the siege darkened the front of the wall, and a fill light without shadows from the other side, `FillShare` as bright (`FillPitch`, `FillColor`, the sun keeping the forward shading of the translucent water by its `ForwardShadingPriority`), lightens what lies in a shadow, as behind the wall, the catapult the mesh an artist gives (`CatapultMesh`) or a frame, an arm and a bucket of basic shapes; the health of the gate, the towers and the parts struck shows over them, a stone flies in a high arc, the cursor and the hint at the bottom say what striking or firing at a part does, and the panel at the cursor what a part is. Not built: harm from the moat, the keep, walls that harm what stands on them when they fall, and a castle without a fort fought for in the open as before.
  - The panel of a creature (`HexUnitTooltip`, `FHexUnitTooltipData`) is the same in a battle, beside the cursor, and in a castle, as the tooltip of a creature offered to hire, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12: its stats as icons with their names and numbers in two columns, whether it flies (`IconFlies`) and whether it shoots with how many shots or fights in melee, what it can, the spells on it and, in a castle, its price and how many come a week. The unit under the cursor on the field has a thin outline along its silhouette (`UpdateOutline`, `M_HexBattleOutline`: a copy of its figure that follows its pose, pushed out along its normals by `OutlineWidth` and `OutlineWidthPerDistance` a unit of distance from the camera, only its back faces drawn, `OutlineFriendColor` or `OutlineEnemyColor`), which costs one more draw of that figure; not measured.
  - What of a siege is no mesh of the castle draws with `M_HexBattleProp` (`PropMaterial`, built by `create_battle_assets.py prop`), masked, so the leaves of the gate dissolve in front of the hex under the cursor as the wall does (`SiegeFade`, `AHexBattleArena::FadeMaterials`, which takes the gate alone, as the developer wanted the bridge solid): a color and a normal texture with a tint, by the first UV of a mesh the field builds in world units or projected from the three planes of the world for a cube, the normal out in world space; the timber of the gate and the bridge is photo-scanned CC0 of Poly Haven at 1K, `dark_wooden_planks` and `weathered_planks` (`Tools/HexBattle/battle_texture_sources.json`, downloaded by `fetch_battle_textures.ps1` to `Saved/TextureSources/battle`, not committed, and imported by `create_battle_assets.py textures` as `T_BattleGateWood`, `T_BattleBridgeWood` and their normals), and the stone the masonry of the castles (`StoneTextures`, `StoneNormals` by style). The bridge is a deck of planks rising `BridgeRise` in its middle with posts and rails. The towers on the field are `TowerWidthScale` (1.8) times as wide as their meshes, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13. The field is small and closed, as they asked the same day for a field bounded by rocks and a dense forest with the fog of war beyond it on to the horizon: `ForestCount` trees of the decor of the ground in clumps (`ForestClumping`) from `ForestStart` hexes past the box of the field and the castle (`AHexBattleArena::GetFieldBox`), `RockCount` of `BorderRocks` (the boulders of the kit) `RockSize` hexes large on `BorderReach` hexes, all of them instances with the rock layer of the biome as their first custom value, as on the planet (the obstacles drew the default layer, grass, before), and the fog of war a plane at `FogHeight` with `M_HexBattleFog` (`FogMaterial`, unlit and see-through, from `FogStart` to `FogEnd` past the box in `FogColor` with wisps of `FogWispColor` drifting at `FogSpeed`, `FogUndergroundShade` as light underground), the sky taking its color, so the field stands as an island in it; the ground reaches out under it.
  - On the field a tower is struck as a part of the wall though its shooters stand in its hex, and where the ground under the cursor lies off the field the ray of the cursor picks a part of the wall it meets (`FWallPick`, `WallPicks`, upright cylinders around the parts as they stand, the towers at the ends of the wall in their corners, the arch of the gate and the towers beside it as the parts of the wall they stand over), as the developer could not strike the towers at the ends or the wall beside the bridge on 2026-11-13; on the field the hex of the ground stays and the wall in front of it dissolves, as a pick there kept the wall whole under the cursor and the hexes behind it out of reach. A click of the right button on the field gives the order the left one would, or takes back a spell chosen (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::OrderGame`). The forest goes `ForestBeyond` hexes on past the rocks under the fog (`ForestCount` 620), and on a ground whose letter is in `RockyGroundLetters` (D, sand) it is of rocks and stones (`RockyDecor`), as a desert has no wood. The fog is alive as the fog of war of the map: `FogLayers` banks `FogLayerStep` of `FogHeight` apart, each `FogLayerFade` as dense as the one under it, churning in three octaves of `T_HexNoise` `FogScale` across, with wisps (`FogWisp`) and glows that swell and fade (`FogPulse`, `FogPulseRate`, `FogPulseColor`).
  - The field around a battle is not flat, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13 for hills, plains and a ground that is not one flat texture: the ground is flat over the field and the castle with `HillFlatMargin` hexes around them and rises over `HillRamp` hexes into hills up to `HillHeight`, a noise `HillScale` across (`AHexBattleArena::GetGroundHeight`, a lattice of 120 steps, the decor standing on it), and `M_HexBattleGround` draws patches of the rock layer of the biome (`UHexBattles::GetRockLayer`, `GroundRockShare`, more on the hills) and the relief of the layers by their normals (`TA_Biome_Normal`, `GroundRelief`). The debug views of the maps work on the field (`UpdateDebugView`): `hex.Wireframe` 1 (F) draws the edges of every mesh over the render and 2 the triangles alone, by the show flag `MeshEdges` and the wireframe view mode of the engine, `hex.Water` (V) hides the water of the moat and G the lines of the hexes; the scope and target of the wireframe do not apply there. The keys that move the camera take it over from the edge of the screen, on the planet and on the field, which scrolls again once the mouse has moved `EdgeScrollHoldDistance` (`HoldEdgeScroll`), the lowest of all input, as the developer asked on 2026-11-13. When the one that strikes or shoots or the one struck lies off the screen or within `CameraFrameMargin` of an edge, the camera comes to their middle and far enough that they take `CameraFrameRoom` of the view (`AHexBattleArena::FrameCamera`, from `BeginEvent`), as they asked the same day. In a siege the wall, its towers and the palace dissolve within `SiegeFadeRadius` of the line from the camera to the point under the cursor where they stand in front of it, keeping `SiegeFadeKeep` of their pixels, as the walls of the underground do (`SiegeFade` of `M_HexPlanetCastle`, `CASTLE_FADE_CODE` of `create_planet_assets.py`, which the battle sets on its own instance and the planet leaves off; the shadow keeps them whole).
  - Not built: creatures of two hexes, the surrender of a hero, levels from the experience, the mage guild and spells of its own for each hero, the abilities of Heroes III not in the list, a field authored by hand for each biome, keys of their own for the orders, and a save of a battle. Not measured: the GPU cost of the field and its figures.
- Neutral stacks stand on the planet (`UHexNeutrals` in `Source/HexHero/Hero`, an object of `UHexHeroes`, `GetNeutrals`), as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 for neutral creatures that guard mines and passes: a stack (`FHexNeutral`: a creature of a faction, upgraded or not, and a count) fills its cell, so no hero walks through it (`GetCellCost`, `CanStand`), and a hero sent onto it fights it from the cell beside it (see the battles). `neutrals.json` of a planet (`FHexNeutralLine`: id, faction, creature, upgraded, count, layer, cell; a line a stack, written with the heroes, deleted when there are none) holds them; the Neutrals tab of the palette on either layer (`EHexPaintTab::Neutrals`, `CaveNeutrals`, `EHexPaintTool::Neutral`, `UHexPlanetPainter::PaintNeutral`) places one of the faction, creature, upgrade and count chosen with a click and takes one away with Ctrl+click, each a step of Undo (`FHexPaintStep::OldNeutrals`), and Generate (`GenerateNeutrals`, one step of Undo, another layout each press) scatters them over both layers by `NeutralGeneration` of `DA_HexBattle` (`FHexNeutralGeneration`, numbers of Claude's): beside `MineShare` of the mines and mills, on the cell before each that is nearest where the heroes start, and in the narrow places of the ways, an open cell whose open neighbors lie in two runs, `PassesPerThousand` to a thousand cells a hero walks, `Spacing` cells apart, never within `StartClearSteps` of where a hero or a castle starts, stronger the further away (the tier, steps over `TierSteps` up to `MaxTier`, one more underground, is the least level of the creature, which may be one higher, and the count is `WeeksBase` + `WeeksPerTier` a tier of the weekly growth of its dwelling, `WeeksSpread` more or less, `UpgradedPerTier` a tier upgraded). Since 2026-11-12, when the developer found too few, stacks also stand on open ground anywhere, `OpenPerThousand` (10) to a thousand cells a hero walks, the underground has `UndergroundDensity` (3) times as many of both, `PassesPerThousand` is 8, `Spacing` 4 and `MineShare` 0.85, and each generation logs what each layer wanted and got. Small holds 41 of them, generated with the seed 2026 as enemies to fight (`hex.Neutral generate 2026`, saved): 32 on the surface and 9 underground, of levels 2 to 5. A stack takes a level of its tier or one higher, the tier rising every `TierSteps` (14) steps up to `MaxTier` (3), as a first try with a spread of two levels gave Small mostly creatures of levels 5 to 7. A stack is drawn as one to `NeutralMaxFigures` figures of its creature, more for more creatures, on the ground of its cell facing south (`HexUnitFigure`, `NeutralScale`), hidden under the fog of war, and the panel at the cursor names it as a hero sees it from afar, "A pack of bone warriors" (`DescribeCount`; the number itself while the game is not played). New game puts every stack back as `neutrals.json` has it. `hex.Neutral <list|add <Cell> <Faction> <Level 1-7> <Count> [up]|remove <Cell>|generate [Seed]|clear>` drives them from the console. Not built: a stack that attacks a hero passing beside it, one that joins a hero, and a stack of mixed creatures.
- The fog of war (`UHexShroud` in `Source/HexHero/Hero`, an object of `UHexHeroes`, `GetShroud`), as the developer decided on 2026-11-11: each player has a map of what it explored, on each layer, which its heroes open, and what was explored once stays seen for good, with whatever stands there, as in Heroes III (two states, no third of ground explored but out of sight). A hero explores the cells up to `SightRadius` (5) steps of neighbors around the cell it stands on (`HexShroud::FindWithin`, `ExploreAround`), underground only the passages among them, as nobody walks the voids: when the game starts (`StartPlay`, `ExploreAll`), with every step (`StartNextStep`), through an entrance (`Travel`) and when it joins a game in play; New game forgets it all (`Restart`). A castle explores for its owner too, `CastleSightFactor` (1.5, 8 steps) times as far, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 ("замок також розкриває туман ... в 3 рази більше ніж у героя"; 1.5 since 2026-11-12, as 15 steps opened 708 of the 4002 cells of Small at the start): when the game starts and when a castle is visited or taken (`ExploreCastles`). It is on while the game is played with `bShroud` of `DA_HexHeroes` (`IsShroudOn`), never in the editing of the planet, and `hex.PlanetShroud 0` lifts it; nothing of it is saved yet, as where the heroes walked to is not. The fog shown is that of the person whose turn it is, or was last (`GetShroudPlayer`, see the turns). The fog forbids no move, as the developer decided on 2026-11-11 (a first build had no way over unexplored cells, the rule of Heroes III): a hero a person moves may be sent over ground its player has not explored, where a step counts `UnknownCost`, that of open plains, whatever lies or stands there (`FindFieldFor` with `bThroughUnknown`, `GetWayStepCost`; a site or an entrance there is unknown, `IsHidden`, so no way is found through an exit its player has not seen; a void of the underground beside a passage it explored counts as seen, `IsSeenVoid`, as nobody explores the voids and a way would else be laid through the walls), so the way shown is the least it can cost: its last label reads "2+ days" and the panel "at least" its points. Such a way is checked where the hero reaches the first cell of it that lay under the fog (`FHexHero::CheckCell`, `FindCheckCell`, `CheckRoute` from `StartNextStep`), by which it has seen what lies beyond, as the developer asked on 2026-11-11 in place of a check before every step, which was built first that day: while the rest of its way is open and no other way to the cell it was sent to is cheaper by more than `RerouteStopPoints` (0), it walks on, to the next cell of it that is still under the fog, and else it stops for the person to decide, that cell kept so the new way shows and a click or Space sends it on; a way whose next cell turned out closed stops it there as any blocked way does. The heroes the game moves keep to what their player explored. The panel at the cursor says only "Unexplored" of a cell the fog hides. `AHexPlanet::UpdateShroud`, every frame a layer is shown, turns what the player explored into how much of each cell shows (`ShroudTarget`: 1 where explored, and underground `ShroudVoidMargin` (0.6) for a void beside a passage that is explored, so the walls along the passage and a strip of the chasm behind them show, as the developer asked for some margin from the walls), eases what is shown towards it over `ShroudRevealTime` (`ShroudShown`; at once for a layer just shown or another player) and writes it into `ShroudField` (`UploadShroudField`): a texture `ShroudFieldSize` (512) a side, a map of the sphere by the direction from the center folded out as an octahedron (`HexShroud::GetFieldUV` and `GetTexelDirection`, which `FieldUV` of `SHROUD_COMMON_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py` repeats, so change both together), each texel a blend of its three cells (`FHexSphereGrid::FindBlend`, found once, `ShroudTexels`, about 2 ms on Terra), so the edge of the fog does not follow the hexes; the whole texture goes up while cells open. `ApplyShroud` gives the materials the field, `Shroud` (0 without fog) and the `Shroud*` values of the config of the layer by name. On the surface it is a bank of cloud (`SHROUD_CODE`): what stands on ground that is not explored is hidden whatever its height, and a bank `ShroudTop` high lies over that ground, marched in `SHROUD_STEPS` (6) looks through the view, `ShroudDensity` thick, thinner at its top (`ShroudTaper`), darker at its foot (`ShroudBaseShade`) and at night (`ShroudNightShare`), in `ShroudColor`, a dark blue since the developer found it too grey on 2026-11-11, with patches of tone (`ShroudTone`), its edge moved by a noise of two sizes that drifts (`ShroudSoftness`, `ShroudEdgeNoise`, `ShroudNoiseSize`, `ShroudDrift`), and alive, as they asked the same day for something to move in it: its noise changes in place (`ShroudChurn`), lighter wisps wind through it in patches that wander (`ShroudWisp`, `ShroudWispSize`, `ShroudWispSpeed`, the ridges of a noise that another, flowing, bends), and here and there a glow swells and fades inside it (`ShroudPulse`, `ShroudPulseSize`, `ShroudPulseRate`, `ShroudPulseColor`, one to a place, each at its own time), all taken once a pixel where the view meets the bank; the haze lays it over the opaque scene after the shade of the hero, the water over itself, and the real clouds drift above it. Underground the fog of the layer lays `ShroudDarkColor` over what is not explored (`SHROUD_DARK_CODE`). What is drawn after them and adds light or lines of its own takes the field too and shows only well inside what is explored (`SHROUD_SHOWN_CODE`, `add_shroud_shown`): the grid lines of the sea, the shafts of daylight over the exits and the motes and spores (`UHexPlanetMotes::SetShroud`); the aurora over a pentagon that is not explored is hidden (the Niagara materials take no texture of a layer). The argument `shroud` of the script builds those materials alone (water, water grid, haze, underground fog, motes, markers). The `HexHero.Hero.Shroud` test checks the cells a hero sees, the maps of the players and that a texel of the field is looked up where its direction lies. On the machine it was made on at 1280x720 on Terra, after the frame time had settled, measured with `hex.PlanetShroud` 1 and 0 before the wisps and the glows, whose cost is not measured: about 0.04 ms of GPU close to a hero at 900 (3.79 / 3.75 ms), 0.11 ms at 4000, where most of the screen is fog (4.40 / 4.29), and nothing measurable underground (3.61 / 3.62); these were measured while what the fog hides was still drawn under it; the chunks it covers whole are not drawn since (see the turns). Not built: sight of mines, and a save of what was explored.
- Rivers run along the sides of the cells, from corner to corner (`UHexPlanetRivers`, a component of `AHexPlanet`, and `HexPlanetRivers`), so for movement a river lies between two cells: a move across it costs `RiverCrossCost` of `DA_HexPlanet` times more unless a bridge stands on that side (`GetCrossingFactor`, shown in `SHexHoverInfo`; nothing moves yet). A side may hold one only between plains and hills, so a side whose cell becomes sea or a mountain drops out until it is painted back, and a save leaves it out. `rivers.json` of a planet holds the sides, a line each with its two cells and whether a bridge stands there (`FHexRiverEdge`, `HexPlanetFile::SaveRivers`), so Git merges it; a planet without it generates them and writes it at once, even empty (`HexPlanetRivers::Generate`, `River*` and `BridgeSpacing` of `DA_HexPlanet`): each starts at a corner at the foot of mountains, else of hills, at least `RiverSourceSeaDistance` rings inland, and walks along sides between two cells of one level, never higher, to the corner a side takes nearest the nearest cell of the sea (carried out along the rings and checked against the neighbors, so the distance changes smoothly and a river runs straight towards the coast rather than wandering over rings of equal distance), up to `RiverFlatSteps` in a row that bring it little nearer; at each corner it turns left or right, and turning the same way at two corners in a row, which takes it off to the side, costs `RiverStraightness` against how much nearer a side takes it, with `RiverWander` of randomness (the `Rivers` test wants fewer than 40% of the sides turning it the same way at both ends; 20 of 92 there). It ends at a corner on the coast or another river, kept from running next to the rivers before it, and a bridge goes about every `BridgeSpacing` sides (Terra 566 sides with 56 bridges, Islet 38 with 3). Which way a side flows is not stored but found from the net (`BuildNet`): towards the nearest corner on the coast along the rivers, else towards the lowest corner of a net with none, as bits of each cell (the sides in the low byte, in the high byte those that flow from corner K - 1 to K). The water does not follow the sides but one smooth line, the same for every cell, so the water, its banks and its valley join across the sides without steps (`HexPlanetRivers::BuildCurve`, `FCurve`). The net becomes lines: at a corner of two rivers a line goes on through whichever way they flow, so a river stays one line where its flow parts or meets (a split corner is no spring), and at a corner of three the river flowing out and the largest coming in (most sides upstream) go on while the third ends there, moved onto the line of the others, the point of it nearest that corner, so the waters join; each line runs downstream where most of its sides flow, and `FlowSigns` says where the flow runs back. A line goes through the corner it starts at, the middles of its sides and the corner it ends at; how straight it runs, and so how far it leaves the sides of the cells, is `RiverCornerCut` (each corner between two sides pulled that share of the way onto the chord between their middles: 0 along the sides, 1, the default of the class, straight through their middles, where a zigzag of sides becomes a straight line a quarter of a cell from its corners; `DA_HexPlanet` has 0.6), `RiverRelax` (passes that straighten it further over several sides) and `RiverMaxOffset` (how far it may leave its sides at most, 0 for no limit, held before and after it is rounded off); the `Rivers` test measures them (about 4 along the sides, 22 through the middles, 8 with a limit of 8, 45 with six passes). Then it is rounded off `RiverSmoothing` times (Chaikin), wound by `RiverMeander` of a noise of the place (`RiverMeanderScale`), fading in over `RiverMeanderFade` of a cell from the ends of a line, and a river that ends on the coast goes on `RiverMouthLength` into the sea (`FCurve::Mouths`); a spring, a corner of one river away from the sea, narrows it by `Narrowing` over `RiverSourceTaper` of a cell. `hex.PlanetRiverShape [CornerCut] [Relax] [MaxOffset]` tries the shape while the game runs (`AHexPlanet::SetRiverShape`, which builds every chunk again, or the first build when it comes from `-ExecCmds`), and without arguments goes back to `DA_HexPlanet`. Each segment is listed for the cells within `GetReach` of it (`CellSegments`, two rings around the cell of its middle), so two cells that share a point find the same nearest point (`FindNearest`, which the `Rivers` test checks on every side). `HexPlanetSurface::Build` takes that line and lowers the ground into a valley along it (`RiverValleyDepth`, a flat bottom `RiverValleyBottom` wide each way from the line and slopes of `RiverValleySlope`), so a river lies a little below its cells, and the rebuild of changed levels rebuilds the cells whose bits changed and their rings (`FindChangedCells`). `M_HexPlanet` (`RIVER_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py`) finds the exact distance to the line for each pixel, as distances in the vertices, blended by triangles about 17 across on flat plains, missed a line that crossed one, and their sides, which do not fit between two lines or past the end of one, drew false water: `UHexPlanetRivers::UpdateTexture` lists for each sector of each cell (the triangle from its center to corners K - 1 and K, which the angle of the fourth UV gives) the segments of the line, every other point of it, within reach of anything of a river there (water, banks, the gap from the run-up, plus 25 for the pieces whose middle moves), and `CellRivers`, a pixel per sector at six times the column of `CellMarks`, gives the first of them in RGB and their number in alpha, while `RiverSegments`, a float texture 4096 wide, holds two texels a segment: its ends as directions from the center with the narrowing at each, and in the first whether it is frozen (16, either cell of its side has a biome of `RiverFrozenBiomes`) and flows from its first end to its second (32). The nearest gives the distance, the flow, which way is away from the river for its banks and which side of it a pixel lies on for its lanes; a change of biomes writes them again (`RefreshTexture`), and a new planet or more segments make the textures anew (`AHexPlanet::ApplyRiverTextures`). The water is `RiverWidth` wide and at least about a pixel, and lets its bed show through as the shallows of the sea do: the sand of `RiverBedLayer` (layer 1) tinted by `RiverBedTint`, under `RiverWaterEdge` to `RiverWaterMiddle` of the colors of `RiverShallowColor` to `RiverDeepColor`, darker under its banks (`RiverEdgeShade`), with faint ripples carried downstream (`RiverFlowSpeed`, `RiverRipple*`) and, so it shows that it flows, lines in lanes along the river that drift downstream, broken into dashes of a noise carried with the water in the same two phases (`RiverStreak*`); on steep ground denser and brighter lines are the foam of rapids (`RiverRapids*`). It is rough enough that the sun makes no glints on it (`RiverRoughness` 0.65). Its banks are wet earth (`RiverBank*`, the edge moved by `RiverEdgeNoise`) with a volume alike on both sides whatever the light, darker at the foot and lighter on the crest (`RiverBankShade`, `RiverBankHighlight`), and a normal bent a little across them (`RiverBankRelief` over `RiverLeveeWidth`, away from the nearest point of the line); a frozen river is still, cracked ice (`RiverIce*`). Below the sea it fades out over `RiverMouthDepth`, so at its mouth the water of the sea meets the bed alone, and within `RiverMouthCalm` of the coast, by the distance of the sea field, it grows as light as `RiverShallowColor` and its rapids calm down, so it joins the shallow water it meets; where a frozen river reaches the sea its ice goes on as `RiverMouthFloes` broken floes, smaller further out to `RiverMouthFloeReach`, the first on the edge of the water (`AHexPlanet::UpdateFloes`, before the pack, which keeps away from them). Where an open river reaches the sea its water pours out into it: `AHexPlanet::UpdateRiverMouths` gives `M_HexPlanetWater` the mouths nearest the point under the camera, up to 8 (`RiverMouth0..7` with alpha 1 when given, `RiverMouthDir0..7`, `RIVER_MOUTHS` of the script), and `PLUME_CODE` draws from each a fan `RiverPlumeWidth` wide at the mouth that spreads by `RiverPlumeSpread` out to `RiverPlumeLength`, starting `RiverPlumeBack` inland so it covers the edge of the water there: the foam of the shore and the breakers give way in it (`RiverPlumeCalm`), so no line of foam lies across a mouth, its water is silty (`RiverPlumeColor`, `RiverPlumeTint`) and rougher (`RiverPlumeChop`), lines in `RiverPlumeLanes` lanes stream out with the river and wander as they spread (`RiverPlumeLineWidth`, `RiverPlumeDashLength`, `RiverPlumeSpeed`, `RiverPlumeFoam`, `RiverPlumeWobble`), and froth churns where it meets the sea (`RiverPlumeFroth`); all of them are values of `DA_HexPlanetWater`. It reflects by `RiverRoughness` (the custom node gives the roughness), hides the grid line of its side, and comes after `CANOPY_CODE`, so a forest never covers it; objects keep `RiverWidth` / 2 + `RiverMeander` + `RiverBankWidth` from its line (`GetRiverDistance`), and boulders stay out of the valleys. The bridges are instances of `BridgeMesh` of `DA_HexPlanet` where the line passes nearest the middle of their sides, on the ground there, across the line and scaled to `BridgeLength` (`UpdateBridges`, drawn with `M_HexPlanetBoulder` in the rock layer `BridgeRockLayer`, culled past `BridgeCullDistance`): `SM_PlanetBridge` in `Content/HexPlanet/Bridges`, a stone arch with a humped deck and parapets that `generate_planet_kit.py` writes to `Saved/HexPlanetBridge` and `Tools/HexPlanetKit/import_planet_bridge.py` imports apart from the kit, whose import deletes its folder, and gives to `DA_HexPlanet` while it names no bridge. The palette has a section Rivers: River lays a river along the shortest way over sides from the corner the cursor stood nearest to the next (`UHexPlanetPainter::PaintRiver`, by the point of `AHexPlanet::FindCellAlongRay`), Bridge puts one on the side with a river nearest the cursor, Ctrl takes either away, each stroke a step of Undo (`FHexPaintStep::OldRivers`); the water and the valley show once the rebuild is shown, as both follow the line it builds. Their GPU cost is not measured yet.
- `AHexPlanet::BeginPlay` only reads the configs and the manifest and shows a loading screen over the viewport (`ShowLoadingScreen`, `LoadingBackgroundColor` and `LoadingTextColor` of `DA_HexPlanet`); the build of everything else (`BuildPlanet`, about 2 s on Islet, mostly the surface) runs in `Tick` three frames later, once the screen has been drawn, so pressing Play shows it at once instead of a frozen window. Until then `IsBuilt` is false, the radius 0 and the grid empty: the camera pawn and the controller wait for it, and the console commands of the planet (`hex.PlanetSetLevel` and the like) refuse to run, so in `-ExecCmds` they come too early.
- Light and air: the sun is the only light of the level, and `AHexPlanet` adds a sky light (`SkyLight`, `CreateSkyLight`) with a plain white cubemap made at runtime, the same from every direction, as up differs over the planet and a sky light lights by the directions of the world (`bLowerHemisphereIsBlack` off), in `SkyLightColor` and `SkyLightIntensity` of `DA_HexPlanet`, so shadows and the undersides of trees are not black; Lumen shades it by what covers each point. It also lights the night side a little. The air is a haze of its own, as the height fog of the engine is flat and its sky atmosphere needs a planet of at least 1 km of radius (Terra is about 115 m): `HAZE_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py` takes the length of a view through a shell `HazeHeight` thick, thinner by `exp(-height / HazeFalloff)`, in eight steps, clear within `HazeStart` of the camera and full `HazeStartFade` further, lit by the sun where each step lies (`HazeColor`, `HazeNightColor`, `HazeSunWrap`), up to `HazeMax`, all `Haze*` values of `DA_HexPlanetClouds`, which `SetCloudParameters` gives every material that needs them. `M_HexPlanetHaze`, a post process at `Scene Color Before DOF` in the unbound `HazeVolume` of `AHexPlanet`, lays it over the opaque scene and over the space behind the planet, where the views that pass the rim glow as its atmosphere, while the water and the clouds, translucent after the depth of field and so not in its input, lay it over themselves; UE 5.8 has no location before translucency any more. So the horizon of a close camera fades into blue and from orbit the planet has a rim of air. Forests show from far away, where their objects are culled: each kind of cover has `FarCover` and `FarColor` (`UHexPlanetObjectSet`, Forest 0.9 and Bushes 0.35, turned to white by the snow of a variant by `FarSnowShare`), `AHexPlanet::UpdateCanopy` writes `CellCanopy`, a pixel per cell (the root of the color and the share, a little less for a sparse cover, as more drew a mosaic of hexes) and in the alpha of the second pixel of the cell in `CellBiomes` which neighbors have a cover, whenever `UHexPlanetObjects::RebuildChunks` runs, and `CANOPY_CODE` tints the ground with it, all of it from `CanopyFadeEnd` and `CanopyNearShare` close by, where it darkens the floor of the forest, up to a side towards another cover and fading out before other sides along a ragged line (`Canopy*` of `DA_HexPlanet`). The sky light and the haze cost about 0.2 ms of GPU together on the machine they were made on. The land away from the hero lies in shade, as the developer decided on 2026-11-10 (`bHeroShade` of `DA_HexPlanet`, off underground): `AHexPlanet::UpdateHeroShade` gives the haze, the water and the clouds where the heroes of the layer stand (see the heroes; the focus of the camera on a planet without any, `FindViewFocus`), each with in its alpha how far its light reaches (`GetHeroLightReach`, `HeroLightReach` cells), and `HeroShade`, 0 under the forced light of L, and `HERO_SHADE_CODE` of `create_planet_assets.py`, which the haze lays over the opaque scene and the space past the planet and the water and the clouds over themselves at their own points, keeps day within `HeroShadeStart` of the reach of the nearest and from `HeroShadeEnd` of it `HeroShadeBrightness` of the light and `HeroShadeSaturation` of the colors, the shade of clouds and not the night, while what glows by itself, the auroras, keeps its light; around each entrance that can be used and each aurora the shade lifts by `EntranceLightLift` within `EntranceLightReach` cells and `AuroraLightLift` within `AuroraLightReach` (`LightField`, a cubemap by the direction from the center, `LightFieldSize` texels a side, built in about 2 ms on Terra whenever the markers are placed, `UpdateLightField`). Its GPU cost, a loop over the lights and a sample of the cubemap in three passes, is given with the heroes.
- `AHexPlanet` gives each cell a level from levels.txt or `HexPlanetGenerator`, and builds two mesh sections per face, the second with the mountains, and keeps where the vertices and triangles of each cell lie in them (`CellMeshes`). Frequency 80 is as many cells as 256 x 256. The first UV is 0 in the center of a cell and 1 at its sides, which `M_HexPlanet` turns into the grid line (`GridWidth`, never under about a pixel, `GridMinPixels`, nor over `GridMaxPixels` for the half each cell draws, so a slope facing the camera does not draw it twice as wide as flat ground seen at a slant), and its V is the height above the sea as a share of the mean peak of mountain cells, by which the line fades out from `GridFadeStart` to `GridFadeEnd`, so it does not show on mountains, and below the sea over `GridSeaFade`, as the lines of the cells of the sea lie on the water: `WaterGrid` is a strip inside the sides of each such cell at the height of the sea (`AHexPlanet::BuildWaterGrid`, as wide as the widest line, so change it with `GRID_LINE_CODE`), whose U runs as on the surface, and `M_HexPlanetWaterGrid` draws the same line there as translucent black (`WaterGridDarkness`), after the water and before the clouds by sort priority, and hides it with the water of the cells `hex.Water` hides; the second is the UV of the piece. The surface draws no vertex colors of its own, and the tiles of painting draw colors of their own (`PreviewTile`, `AHexPlanet::GetPreviewMaterial`, which has `CellMarks` and the colors of the outlines, and whose tiles carry the third and fourth UVs of their cells, so the edge of the brush shows on them); the surface draws the biomes, see below. Clouds are four octaves of 3D value noise of the direction from the center, computed in `CLOUD_CODE` without textures, so they never repeat, the large octave shifting the finer ones so their edges curl into puffs; they turn around the axis of the planet, and each octave moves along the radius and back at its own pace (`CloudMorph`, `CloudMorphSpeed`), which takes another slice of the noise, so they grow, part and dissolve in place. The two finest octaves fade out where a pixel covers more than `CloudDetailFade` of their cells, as from far away they only draw streaks. All their values are in `DA_HexPlanetClouds` (`UHexPlanetCloudConfig`, created by the script only when missing, so tune it by hand), and `AHexPlanet::SetCloudParameters` gives every float and color of it, with `PlanetRadius`, to each material that draws the clouds or their shadows as parameters of the same names, once when it is made, so a new value needs only the property and the parameter (`CLOUD_PARAMETERS`, `SHADOW_PARAMETERS`, `SHELL_PARAMETERS` in the script, whose defaults come from the class). `AHexPlanet` draws them as a sphere (`Clouds`, `CloudRings`) at `CloudHeight` above the sea with the unlit translucent `M_HexPlanetClouds`, sorted after the water, as both have their bounds around the center.
