They fill `CloudThickness` around their height: `SHELL_LIFT_CODE` puts the shell on the side of it towards the camera, and `CLOUDS_CODE` marches the view through it from there, forwards where it goes in and backwards from the far side of the shell, which past the rim of the planet is where a view comes out of its second pass, with more looks the longer the view is in it (`CloudVolumeStep`, up to `CloudVolumeSteps`, offset by frame for the temporal filter); a point needs more cloud at the top and the bottom (`CloudVolumeRound`), so big clouds fill it and small ones stay thin, its bottom is darker (`CloudVolumeBaseShade`), and the light it takes grows with the length (`CloudVolumeDensity`), so along the shell at the horizon they stand up as heaps. From above it takes about two looks, and at 1280x720 on the machine it was made on all of it cost about 0.2 ms of GPU over a single look. They also look puffy from above: the density is a height, and a second look `CloudLightStep` of a cloud towards the sun puts a side where it rises there in a cool grey shade (`CloudRelief`, `CloudShadeColor`), only where there is cloud. They thin out at night and where the shell is seen edge on, so the rim of the planet shows no stripes, have `CloudOpacity` from the farthest camera, thin to `CloudNearOpacity` as the distance from the camera to a cloud, plus `CloudHeight`, comes down from `CloudFarHeight` to `CloudFadeEnd`, so close above them the land shows through, and fade out from there to `CloudFadeStart`, so the clouds next to the camera are gone while those far off stay. The camera at its closest is at about the height of the clouds, where a flat shell only shows streaks, so `AHexPlanet` draws the shell twice: `Clouds` below the camera, and `SkyClouds`, whose material has `CloudSkyLayer` 1 and which `SHELL_LIFT_CODE` moves out to `CloudSkyHeight`, above the close camera, as a sky whose clouds show over the horizon, with bounds widened for it. As the camera comes down through `CloudSkySwitch` above the sea the one below fades out and the sky fades in over `CloudSkySwitchFade` on both sides, so there are always clouds, and `AHexPlanet::Tick` hides the shell that is faded out. The material is two sided, and the side away from the camera fades as the shell seen edge on does, so from above the far half is hidden and from under the sky its inside shows, which fades less edge on (`CloudEdgeOnFadeNear`). A cloud whose line from the camera dips below the sea is hidden, as the water writes no depth and the cloud, drawn after it, would show through it near the horizon. Their shadows (`CLOUD_SHADOW_CODE`, `CloudShadowStrength`) darken the base color of `M_HexPlanet`, so only the lit side, and the color of `M_HexPlanetWater` with its glints, by the cloud where the ray to the sun meets the shell, so they lie under their clouds; `AHexPlanet` gives all three `PlanetRadius`, `CloudHeight` and `SunDirection`.
- Relief comes from a kit of meshes, `DA_HexPlanetKit` (`UHexPlanetKitConfig`), one kit per level. A cell takes the piece of its level for the mask of neighbors on the same level, chosen and turned by `HexMeshBuilder::GetJoinedPiece`, as the mountains of the flat map, and a version and a symmetric turn by a hash of the cell. `HexPlanetKit::PlaceVertex` bends each of the six triangles of the regular hex onto the triangle from the center of the cell to two of its corners by the same weights, so direction D of a piece is neighbor D and its corner D is corner D - 1 of the cell. Two levels share the slope between them, so the side of a hex lies halfway down it and its grid line with it, and a coast runs near the sides: a corner lies at the mean top of its three cells, and a side at `GetSideHeight`, the mean of the tops of its two cells in its middle, bending linearly to its corners, which both cells find alike. Height 0 of a piece goes onto the top of the cell and its edge, `-FootDepth`, onto the line of the side, so the higher cell falls to it and the lower one rises, and heights between blend them over `FadeHeight` above the edge. Land bends its top onto the line near a side to the same level (`bTopFollowsCorners`), so with `FadeHeight` equal to `FootDepth` a side that a piece models anywhere between 0 and the edge lands on its neighbor, and a point on a side towards another level, or at a corner of such a side, goes onto the line whatever its height; the `Seams` test checks it. A piece must therefore lie at the edge on a side towards another level, steepest there so the two halves join smoothly, within the edge and 0 on other sides, and have a vertex in the middle of every side.
- Neighboring mountains and hills differ, and chains rise and fall, as the map is meant to be a maze of ranges: each kit has `Variety` values in `FHexPlanetKit`, which `import_planet_kit.py` sets from `VARIETY`. A cell may take its piece mirrored (`bMirror`, by a hash: sector D goes to direction 5 - D and its corners swap, looked up by the mirrored mask, `MirrorMask` in `HexPlanetSurface.cpp`, also in the coarse mesh). Each cell stretches its piece above `ScaleBase` by a factor (`HexPlanetSurface::FindRelief`, `CellRelief`, shown in `SHexHoverInfo`): from `HeightScaleMin` to `HeightScaleMax` by a noise of the place of about `HeightNoiseCells` cells, so a chain is low for a while, then high, plus `HeightJitter` by cell and, with `PeakChance` that grows where the noise is high, `PeakScale` more for a big peak. It depends on the place and the level of a cell alone, so painting changes it only with the level and `FindAffectedChunks` needs nothing more. `HexPlanetKit::BuildFrame` blends it as the massif, the mean of two cells in the middle of a side and of three at a corner, so a shared side gets the same factor from both cells; land fades it out where its top follows the side lines (`bTopFollowsCorners`), as it lies on them anyway. The center of each piece also moves by up to `CenterShift` of the radius in a direction of its own (`FCellFrame::CenterShift`, added by the weight of the center, which is 0 on the sides), so peaks stand off the middles and the cells of a chain do not line up. The inside of each cell, fading out before its sides (`Inside` in `PlaceVertex`), also takes a shape of its own (`Shape` of `FHexPlanetKit`): its height above `ScaleBase` as a share of `ShapeHeight` is raised to a power between `SharpnessMin` and `SharpnessMax` by a hash (`FCellFrame::Sharpness`), so one peak is a blunt dome and the next a needle, a noise of the place adds summits and notches (`CragHeight`, `CragScale`), and the inside turns around the center by up to `Twist` degrees (`FCellFrame::Twist`), so ridges do not all point alike. Neighboring peaks join into a chain: the ground rises by `RidgeLift` along the line from the center of a cell to the middle of each side towards the same level, from nothing at the center to all of it on the side, `RidgeWidth` of the side wide, so a saddle between two mountains lies high and a range reads as one chain with peaks on it rather than a row of teeth; a side of a narrow chain (a cell with at most two neighbors on its level) takes all of it, while inside a massif only `RidgeMassifShare` of the sides do, chosen by a hash of the pair of cells, and the rest `RidgeMassifLow` (`FCellFrame::RidgeWeights`), so a massif is no net of ridges. A chain one cell wide still looks massive (`Bulk` of `FHexPlanetKit`): the low ground of a piece, which the blend into the side line would flatten into a strip of low land along the sides, rises by `BulkLift` (times the root of the factor of the height), from nothing at the foot to all of it `BulkReach` of the way up to the peak, so the rock reaches out to the sides on a full base; it depends on the height on the piece alone, so a side shared with the same level gets it alike and the foot stays on the side lines. Where a noise along the ranges says so (`SplitNoiseCells`, `SplitThreshold`, `FCellFrame::Split`), the crest of a cell with neighbors on its level splits into two blades with a couloir between them along the lines from its center to the middles of those sides, `SplitDepth` deep and `SplitBlade` high at `SplitWidth` of a cell radius, only inside, so it joins again on the shared sides. They move no vertex in or out, so they cost no triangles and no measurable GPU. High ground breaks into sharp faces like split stone: a ridged noise of the place moves it by `JaggedHeight` (`JaggedScale`), by a share that grows from `FacetHeightStart` to `FacetHeightEnd` above the sea (`HexPlanetKit::GetFacetShare`), so small mountains stay smooth, and the same share goes into X of the second UV, by which `M_HexPlanet` shades that ground flat by triangle from `PosDdx` and `PosDdy`, each face leaning at random by regions of `FacetCell` (`FacetStrength`, `FacetTilt` of `DA_HexPlanet`); ray tracing has no such change across a pixel and keeps the vertex normal. The sides are rock down to the foot too (`Rock` of `FHexPlanetKit`): gullies cut down the fall line by `GullyHeight`, channels of a ridged noise of the angle around the center of the cell and, stretched, of the distance from it (`GullyCount`, `GullyLength`), most on the middle of the slopes and fading before the sides, so spurs stand between them; the share of a gully at a vertex goes into Y of the second UV, by which `M_HexPlanet` darkens it (`CreviceDarkening`) and lets snow reach `GullySnowReach` further down it on steeper ground (`GullySnowFlatness`), so snow lies in streaks; and ground with faces steps in ledges of `LedgeStep` (`LedgeStrength`, a function of the height and the place). On Terra the planet has about 10 million triangles, close to a range about 3.1 million drawn where 2.7 were, and on the machine it was made on that cost about 0.16 ms of GPU there, 0.03 ms for the whole planet, and about 0.8 GB more memory of the process. Finer ravines than a lattice step (about 8 units) would need a rock normal stretched along the slope in the material. Boulders lie at the foot of the mountains on the edge of ranges as instances apart from the surface (`FHexPlanetBoulders` in `Boulders` of `DA_HexPlanetKit`): `HexPlanetSurface::Build` puts up to `MaxPerSide` on each side of a cell of mountains towards land or shallows (`MinLevel`), along it and up to `Spread` into the neighbor, on the ground a flat tile of the neighbor would have there, sunk by `Sink` of their size, leaning up to `Tilt` (`FBuild::Boulders`, by chunk, so a rebuild replaces those of its chunks), and `AHexPlanet` draws them with a `UHierarchicalInstancedStaticMeshComponent` per mesh (`BoulderComponents`, `UpdateBoulders`, culled past `CullDistance`), with the rock layer of the biome of their mountain as the first custom data of an instance, which `M_HexPlanetBoulder` of `create_planet_assets.py` projects from the three planes of the world, lighter or darker by `BoulderTint`, and a change of biomes updates. The four rocks come from `generate_planet_kit.py` (`Boulder`, an icosphere cut by planes into broad faces, a normal per face) and `import_planet_kit.py` puts them and the material into the kit, so run `create_planet_assets.py` before it. Terra has about 13,000 of them, about 0.3 million triangles close to a range, and on the machine it was made on the shapes, faces and boulders cost about 0.03 ms of GPU for the whole planet and nothing measurable close to mountains. The snow line stays at `SnowHeight`, so only the high peaks get snow. A mountain never takes a factor below `HeightScaleFloor`, so a low one still reads as a mountain, and from `HighRockStart` to `HighRockEnd` above the sea `M_HexPlanet` covers the ground with the rock of the biome whatever its slope, so it is rock and not grass while hills stay below it. A lone cell (at most `LoneNeighbors` neighbors on its level) with no neighbor on its level takes at least `LoneHeightMin` and raises its height from the foot to the power `LoneSharpness` (`FCellFrame::LoneDome`), while a spur, the end of a chain with one such neighbor, skips both, as on the piece of a spur, whose peak stands off its center, they made a leaning tower, takes `SpurHeightScale` of its factor of the height (not below `HeightScaleFloor`), so a chain steps down at its end, and is never sharper than a cone; both stand on a dome over its whole cell of `LoneBase` of its peak (`FCellFrame::LoneBase`, on the height and not on the piece, as the low heights of a piece blend into the side line, and only inside, as such a cell may share a side with one neighbor) and moves its center only `LoneShift` of `CenterShift`, so it fills its cell instead of standing as a thin spike. A cell with no neighbors on its level also has `LoneSummitStyles` more versions besides its pieces (`FCellFrame::Summits`, the styles in `BuildCell`): two summits of unlike heights, three in an uneven cluster, and a main peak with a lower shoulder, turned by a hash, each a peak of `SummitHeight` and `SummitWidth` over its first piece lowered to `SummitBase` of its height above the side lines, only inside; the factor of the height counts by its root there, so a high cell does not make a tower. The hash and the Version tool choose among them and the pieces. `FBuild::UnmatchedRidgePoints` counts the points on sides between mountains (a kit without `bTopFollowsCorners`) that only one cell built, and the `Surface.Rebuild` test wants none, mirrored pieces included; the `Seams` test gives every cell a random factor and land a plateau. Hills have four styles of bumps, one per version (`HILL_STYLES`: knolls, a ridge, two big knolls, many small), kept gentle enough that the rock of slopes (`SlopeEnd` of `DA_HexPlanet`, 0.8) does not ring them like craters. They fade out before every side, so a group of hills drew a mound in each cell with flat ground along the sides; so hills with neighbors on their level rise together into an upland (`PlateauLift` and `PlateauFill` of `FHexPlanetKit`, 16 and 0.45 from `VARIETY` of `import_planet_kit.py`, in `HexPlanetKit::PlaceVertex`): the ground rises by `PlateauLift` times the factor of the height, on the sides between two such cells, at the corners of three and in the middle, by the share of the sides of a cell towards neighbors on its level (`FCellFrame::PlateauCenter`, `PlateauSides`, `PlateauCorners`: the mean of the cells of a side or corner that all share the plateau, else 0, and never a pentagon), blended as the massif and eased in from a side towards another level, so the inside of a big group rises by all of it, its edge by about half, a chain by a quarter and a pair hardly, and it ramps down to the side lines. The bumps take only part of the lift, so the ground between them comes up towards their tops: at a `PlateauFill` of 1 a bump twice as high as the lift keeps its top, and the ground never folds. On Terra it adds no triangles and no time to the build; the steepest edges of an upland show a little rock of slopes, and its highest knolls can reach `HighRockStart`. Below the top of the plains the ground on slopes moves up and down by a noise of the place (`HexPlanetKit::ApplyWobble`: `CoastWobble`, `CoastWobbleScale`, `CoastWobbleDetail` of `DA_HexPlanet`), most halfway down a slope and not at all on the tops, clamped to the slope, so the coast and the edge of the shelf wind across the cells; it is a pure function of the point, so cells that share a point agree, and the slope pieces of the deep sea, shallows and plains have 6 lattice steps instead of 4 for it. On the machine it was made on at 1080p this cost about 0.06 ms of GPU close to mountains (2.0 to 2.4 million triangles) and 0.1 ms over a coast (2.9 to 3.3 million), nothing for the whole planet (3.2 ms, coarse meshes), and about 0.4 GB more memory of the process.
- `Tools/HexPlanetKit/generate_planet_kit.py` writes the land pieces (deep sea, shallows, plains, hills with bumps) as GLB files, which Blender opens, with `kit.json` to `Saved/HexPlanetKit`, and `import_planet_kit.py`, run with the commandlet, imports them to `Content/HexPlanet/Kit` and writes `DA_HexPlanetKit`, so change the scripts and not the asset. Mountains are the meshes of the flat map, with the planet's own lighter massif, sampled as a height field on a lattice of 12 steps (`Subdivisions`, as their 24 steps came to 30 million triangles; 8 were too coarse for gullies), and what they model past their cell at notches is left out, as the neighbor rises to the side there. The planet reads the vertices of every piece, so they need CPU access, which the import and `assign_hex_mountains.py` give; run the import again after rebuilding the mountains. The planet has about 10 million triangles, so `r.RayTracing.ResidentGeometryMemoryPoolSizeInMB` in `DefaultEngine.ini` is 2048: at the default 400 the new ray tracing geometry of a section whose render state was recreated was evicted at once, and `UProceduralMeshComponent`, which the surface used then, hit an ensure ("geometry 'Surface' is evicted"); `UHexPlanetMeshComponent` references an evicted geometry, so it comes back.
- The 12 pentagons are never mountains (a mountain there becomes hills) and stay passable, a flat tile of their level that slopes to the side lines towards other levels, built with the same frame as any cell whose kit has no piece; each is meant for a gameplay element that gives bonuses, not decided yet, and a set piece of its own may come later, as long as it can be walked over. From each rises an aurora (`AHexPlanet::BuildAuroras`, `Auroras`): a `UNiagaraComponent` of `NS_HexPlanetAurora` at the height of the sea over the pentagon, with Z away from the center of the planet, drawn after the clouds and left out of ray tracing (`SetVisibleInRayTracing`), as light added to the scene needs none there and its ribbons, moved by their material, hung the GPU in a play session in the editor (`DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG`, a page fault in a ray tracing shader). The Niagara system is an asset for an artist to tune by hand, not made by a script (it was built once through the NiagaraToolsets of the editor's MCP server, which `HexHero.uproject` enables): `Curtain` and `CurtainOuter`, each a burst of particles on a ring that live for ever, drawn as a ribbon whose width stands up (`CustomSideVector`, facing Z), jittered out and in by a random velocity that drag stops, the outer wider, lower and bluer, and `RisingLight`, sprites aligned to their velocity that rise from a ring and sway in a curl noise. Its materials come from `create_planet_assets.py`: `M_HexPlanetAurora` for the ribbons (`AURORA_CODE`: rays of a value noise along the curtain that drift and run up, brightest at its lower edge, from `AuroraColorLow` to `AuroraColorHigh` as it rises, `AuroraFlipV` for which way V runs, broken into `AuroraDrapes` with dark gaps), whose world position offset (`AURORA_OFFSET_CODE`) folds the curtains in and out and sways them along themselves, more the higher up (`AuroraFold`, `AuroraFoldCount`, `AuroraFoldSpeed`, `AuroraSway`, out along the normal of the ribbon, as the object position a Niagara material reads is not that of its system and moved the curtains far off) and `M_HexPlanetAuroraSpark` for the sprites, both additive, unlit and two sided, with their values as material parameters, tinted by the particle color and its alpha, brighter at night, fading out next to the camera, a little brighter from far away (`AuroraFarBoost` from `AuroraFarStart` to `AuroraFarEnd` from the camera), so the 12 read as landmarks from orbit, and hidden where the line from the camera dips below the sea (`AURORA_COMMON_CODE`), as the water writes no depth and they would show through it. They read the sun and the radius of the planet from `MPC_HexPlanet`, which `AHexPlanet::Tick` sets, as Niagara draws with the materials themselves; `PlanetRadius` is 0 outside the game, which turns the test off, so the preview of Niagara shows them.
- `AHexPlanetCameraPawn` orbits it with the keys of `DA_HexInput`, and the sun follows the point it looks at unless `hex.PlanetSun` is 0 (`UpdateSun`): it shines from `SunAzimuth` of `DA_HexPlanetCamera`, degrees from the north of that point towards the right of a camera facing north, so turning the camera turns no shadow, at `SunCloseElevation` to `SunFarElevation` by the zoom, and it is lazy: it stands still, so shadows do not slide and virtual shadow maps keep their cache, until it lags `SunLagShare` of the distance of the camera (at most `SunLagMaxDegrees`) behind where it should stand and the camera has stood still for `SunSettleTime`, or lags `SunMaxLagFactor` times that while it moves, and then it glides there over `SunMoveTime` (each move is logged as Verbose of `LogHexHero`). `hex.PlanetSun 0`, from the console only since L became the forced light on 2026-11-10, keeps the sun where it was, so turning the planet shows its night side and the shadows, and the panel of settings shows "Sun: Fixed" (`AHexPlanetCameraPawn::GetSunStatus`). L (`ForcedLightAction`, `IA_HexForcedLight`, which took the place of `IA_HexSunFollow`) turns the forced light on and off (`hex.PlanetForcedLight`, `AHexPlanet::IsLightForced`, the panel "Light: Forced", `GetLightStatus`), which lights both layers evenly for editing: the shade of the surface away from the hero goes, and underground the light of the hero and the fog around it go while the sun takes `ForcedSunIntensity` of its `FHexLayerLighting` (1.5 lux, the placeholder before the dark), the sky light `ForcedSkyLightIntensity` (0.3) and the light the ground gives by itself `ForcedAmbient` (0.06) of `DA_HexUnderground`, each never below its own value, so 0 keeps it; the materials take the ambient through `ForcedLight` of `MPC_HexPlanet` (`force_ambient` of `create_planet_assets.py`, which `build_parameter_collection` adds to the collection without making the others again). `hex.PlanetFocus <Cell> [Distance]` glides to a cell, and at startup puts the camera there at once. `HighResShot` in `-ExecCmds` shoots on the first frames, before a glide ends.
- `AHexPlanetPlayerController` outlines the cell under the cursor in white (`OutlineColor`; while the game is played thinner and letting the ground through, as the developer found the white one too bright and thick there on 2026-11-11: `PlayOutlineColor`, `PlayMarkWidth`, `PlayMarkMinPixels` and `PlayMarkOpacity` of the config of the layer, which `AHexPlanet::SetHoveredCell` gives the materials in place of `MarkWidth`, `MarkMinPixels` and `MarkOpacity`, and on a castle the edge of its three cells as one, the group it is given; `hex.PlanetHoverCell <Cell>` stands in for the mouse, for screenshots, and the argument `marks` of `create_planet_assets.py` builds the materials that draw the outlines alone, the surfaces of both layers and the grid of the sea), which `AHexPlanet::FindCellAlongRay` finds by stepping a quarter of a cell along the ray from the sphere of the highest tops until it goes under the top of the cell there, the water for a cell of the sea, as its grid lines and outline lie on the water and a slanted ray to its bed reached cells further on, and halving the last step; it counts a point a hundredth of a unit above a top as under it, as the last step lies on the water and rounding alone lost the cell there for a frame, which made the panel at the cursor blink, and its neighbors in blue (`NeighborOutlineColor`) only while the debug wireframe draws them too (`AHexPlanet::SetHoveredCell`), whose own colors match (`WireHoverColor`, `WireNeighborColor`); while painting it outlines only the edge of the brush (`BrushOutlineColor`). An outline is no mesh of its own: it colors the grid lines on both sides of the edge of a set of cells, each half `MarkWidth` wide and at least `MarkMinPixels`, glowing by `MarkGlow`, so it is centered on the edge, lies on the real relief, and over the sea on the water; at a bend of the edge the half of a marked side goes on past its corner into the side next to it, as far as the band of that side, so the line joins with a sharp miter, as a disc around the corner left dots. `CellMarks` is a texture with a pixel per cell, 2048 to a row, whose red holds the sides to outline as bits in `MarkPrimaryColor` (`OutlineColor` or `BrushOutlineColor`, which `SetHoveredCell` gives the materials), and green those in `NeighborOutlineColor` (`AHexPlanet::SetCellMarks`, which uploads it when it changes), and every vertex of the surface and of `WaterGrid` has the pixel of its cell as its third UV (`HexPlanetSurface::GetCellUV`, the column and row apart, as UVs are half floats that hold whole numbers exactly only up to 2048) and where it lies in a regular cell whose side K faces K turns of a side as its fourth (`GetLocalUV`), so `MARK_CODE` in `create_planet_assets.py` finds the side nearest a pixel and the other side at its nearest corner by its angle, in ints, as fmod and exp2 of floats dropped some sides to the one before. Below the sea the surface hides it at once and `M_HexPlanetWaterGrid` draws it, so the strips of the sea are as wide as `MarkWidth` too, except in the cells whose water `hex.Water` hides, as their strip is hidden with it: `AHexPlanet::SetWaterHiddenCells` sets blue of `CellMarks` in them (every cell while all the water is hidden), and there the surface draws the grid lines and the outline on the seabed. A hole of more than one cell also gets its rim in alpha of `CellMarks`, bits of sides as in red, which only `M_HexPlanetWaterGrid` draws (`MARK_HOLE_BITS` in `MARK_CODE`), in `WaterHideOutlineColor`, so the water around the hole outlines it while the seabed keeps the outline of the cell alone. It shows the cell in `SHexHoverInfo`. Its `SHexDebugOverlay` shows the lines of `DA_HexKeyLegend` whose actions the pawn and the controller bind (`UHexKeyLegendConfig::GetBoundActions`, `GetSections` with `OnlyActions`), once the pawn has bound them, and so does the flat map (`AHexMapPlayerController::UpdateLegend`), so neither lists keys that do nothing there, such as U and L on the flat map; each line is logged as Verbose of `LogHexHero` (`LogSections`). The overlay has the same panels of settings as the flat map (`SHexDebugOverlay::AddViewStatus`, which both controllers use) and the red warning while the camera differs from the one in `LastView.json`, and `hex.HoverInfo` hides the panel at the cursor.
- `hex.CopyCamera` (Ctrl+C) on the planet copies `hex.LoadPlanet <Folder> | hex.PlanetSetCamera <Latitude> <Longitude> <Heading> <Distance>` (`FHexPlanetCameraPlacement`, heading clockwise from north), which brings the view back from any level and logs the cell under it; on the planet already open the camera jumps there once the pieces around that place are built (see the surface). `hex.LoadMap` also works from the planet, opening `Lvl_HexWorld` with that map. Ctrl+Shift and a number key (`SaveBookmarkAction`, `IA_HexSaveBookmark`) save the camera in a slot of the bookmarks of the planet or flat map open, and Ctrl and the key (`JumpBookmarkAction`) put it back there, 1 to 9 and 0, the tenth (`HexCameraBookmarks`, `Saved/CameraBookmarks.json`, a block per planet and per map, of the player and not in Git, as the developer chose on 2026-11-10): on the planet with its layer, as `hex.PlanetSetCamera` does, once the pieces around the place are built and through a trip to the other layer, on a planet not saved kept in memory until a save gives it a folder (`SaveView`), and on the flat map at once with its turn, zoom and mode; `hex.Bookmark <save|jump> <0-9>` does the same from the console.
- `LastView.json` (`FHexLastView`) holds a block per kind of view, so more can be added: `flatMap` (`FHexLastFlatMapView`, the map and its camera) and `planet` (`FHexLastPlanetView`, the folder of the planet and its camera, which counts as not saved on another planet), and `bPlanetOpen`, whether the planet was open last, written by `HexLastView::SetPlanetOpen` whenever either level opens. `HexLastView::Parse` also reads the flat layout of before the blocks. The planet camera is saved with `SaveViewAction` (Ctrl+S; Alt+S is Simulate of the editor, which a play session takes before the game). `Lvl_HexWorld` without `?Map=` then opens the planet instead (`HexLastView::ShouldOpenPlanet`, checked by `AHexWorldMap`, which skips loading a map, and by `AHexMapPlayerController`), and opening a map from the planet writes `bPlanetOpen` false first, or it would come straight back. Each camera keeps the other's fields when it saves.
- With Shift held the keys and the edges of the screen move the planet camera `ShiftMoveFactor` (2.5) of `DA_HexPlanetCamera` times as fast, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12. The planet camera moves with the inertia of `DA_HexCamera` (`KeyboardInertia`, `DragInertia`, `DragReleaseSmoothing`) as an angular velocity around the center (`AngularVelocity`). The mouse at the screen edge moves it as on the flat map (`AHexCameraPawn::GetEdgeScrollInput`, `hex.EdgeScroll`), and C or `hex.Camera` switches the same modes: top-down turns at most `MaxCameraTurn` from north and back to north on release (`UpdateTopDownHeading`), free stays turned.
- The debug views of the flat map work on the planet with the same keys and console variables, read through `AHexWorldMap::GetDebugViewSettings`. `hex.Wireframe` draws a copy of the sections of the surface with `M_HexPlanetWireframe` (`WireChunks`, `WireCoarseChunks`), and the cells around the cursor in their colors from vertex colors (`HoverWireframe`, from the triangles of those cells, `GetCellTriangles`, with the walls along them underground); only the triangles with every cell hides the chosen sections of the surface, and around the cursor the surface keeps its normal render and its masked copy `M_HexPlanet_Cut` hides only those cells (`SurfaceCutMaterial`, by the same nearest centers as the water), as on the flat map. `hex.WireframeTarget` counts the mountain sections as not terrain. The wireframe hides what lies past the horizon of the planet, as nothing covers it there. `hex.Water` 2 and 3 hide the water of cells by the nearest of 19 cell centers (`HideCell0`..`HideCell18`, `HideCount` of `M_HexPlanetWater`). G hides the grid lines through `GridOpacity` of a dynamic instance of `M_HexPlanet`.
- The seabed lies `ShallowDepth` or `DeepDepth` below the sea. Water is a cap of rings around the point under the camera, dense close to it and reaching a quarter of the way around, which `AHexPlanet::Tick` turns after the camera has moved. `M_HexPlanetWater` is a separate copy of the open sea of `WATER_CODE` (swell, ripples, colors, the lighter side of waves, glints), kept in `create_planet_assets.py`, so a change to the flat water does not reach it. Noise comes from three planes of the world position blended by the normal (`TRI_NOISE`), waves run along 3D directions with their slope along the surface, the sun comes from the light through `SunDirection`, and the water darkens on the night side. The camera of the planet looks from further than the flat one, so `WaveScale` stretches the swell, chop and ripples, also in `WATER_OFFSET_CODE`, and from a pixel size of `FarCalmStart` the ripples, slope and lighter side of waves fade, as dense waves only shimmer from far away. The cap turns with the camera, so its vertices slide over the waves, and as the surface between them is flat, a wave with few vertices on it pulses in height as the camera scrolls, faster the faster it moves, so the cap is dense (`WaterRings`, `WaterSegments`, `WaterInnerStep`) and `WATER_OFFSET_CODE` blurs the noise that bends the swell to that spacing and fades each wave out of the height as the step of its bent phase between points a spacing apart nears a wavelength (`VertexWaveFadeStart` to `VertexWaveFadeEnd`), and it stays in the normal of the pixels. The noise that bends the swell moves its phase so far that both shaders take it at one mip from any camera (`SwellBendMip`), as a mip chosen by the size of a pixel or the spacing made the waves run as the camera moved, and the patches of tone take theirs at `PatchMip`, as the fine detail of the noise speckled them. `HexPlanetSeaField` writes a cubemap with the depth of the seabed, in blue the ice, in alpha the signed distance to the coast, (distance + 50) / 450 so it goes 50 under the land, a soft minimum (`ShoreSoftness` of `DA_HexPlanet`) of the distances to the land cells within three rings, each a disc as wide as the middles of its sides (`FShoreParams`), which grows evenly inside a cell where a blend between cell centers is flat and further out follows the run of the coast rather than each cape, and in green how much its lines bend around land (their curvature times the distance to the nearest land center, 1 around a lone cape), computed exactly from the discs; near the real coast, which winds across the cells (land rises out of the shallows, the sea floods parts of land cells), the distance moves to be 0 just where the ground crosses the sea: `HexPlanetSurface::Build` gives the points where the edges of its triangles cross the sea by chunk (`FBuild::Coasts`, kept in `AHexPlanet::ChunkCoasts` and brought up to date by a rebuild in its task), each takes the value of the field of the cells there, and a texel takes off those of the points nearby, weighted towards the nearest over `ShoreCoastBlend`, fully within a third of `ShoreCoastFade` of them and fading out by it, so the breakers and their run-up meet the real shore (`AHexPlanet::GetFrozenCells`: frozen sea and land next to it), blended between cell centers by `FHexSphereGrid::FindBlend`, its faces in the GPU order of `GetTexelDirection`. A second cubemap, the shore field (`AHexPlanet::ShoreField`, the second half of the same pixels, so a rebuild carries both), cuts the coast into stretches, one land cell each unless `ShoreStretchJoin` joins two: red is the phase of the stretch of the nearest land cell, from a hash of its first cell, and green how far a texel lies from the end of that stretch / 256, from the bisector towards the nearest land cell of another stretch (`(r2^2 - r1^2) / (2 |c2 - c1|)`), so the breakers fade out there, before the phase blends across it between texels; blue is how far it lies along the coast from the middle of the stretch / 256 (across the mean direction from its cells to the sea), which caps the length of a breaker, and alpha how fast the distance to the coast grows (the length of the gradient of the soft minimum). The frozen sea (blue of the sea field, edge moved by noise) is open, passable water with floes on it: `WATER_CODE` makes it dark and cold (`IceWaterColor`, `IceWaterShare`), fading in over about a cell from the open sea (`IceWaterFade`, wider than the edge of the floes, `IceEdgeSoftness`, so the two waters do not meet on a line) with lower waves (`IceWaveShare`, also in `WATER_OFFSET_CODE`), slush that drifts with it (`IceSlush`), foam where it meets a floe and clearer water over what lies just under it, so the ledge of a floe shows, both by the scene depth behind the water (`IceFoamWidth`, `IceUnderClearness`, `IceClearDepth`), and from far away, where floes are culled, their color (`IceFar*`); all are `Ice*` of `DA_HexPlanetWater`. The floes are instances (`AHexPlanet::UpdateFloes`, `FloeComponents`, a `UHierarchicalInstancedStaticMeshComponent` per mesh of `Floes` of `DA_HexPlanetKit`): every cell of frozen sea (level below 0 and a frozen biome) gets a cover of floes, `FloeEdgeCover` next to open sea growing to `FloeInnerCover` `FloeDenseRings` cells in, moved by a noise (`FloeCoverNoise`), and the largest floe grows from `FloeEdgeSizeMax` to `FloeSizeMax` alike, so the edge of the pack is broken and loose and its inside holds large plates; `FloeTries` sizes, largest first, are thrown at random places in the cell, a floe keeping its center in its cell, `FloeShoreGap` of its radius from a side towards land and `FloeGap` from other floes, and those below `FloeShardSize` take the shard meshes. Each mesh has two components, as the unlit water takes no shadows and hides the deep seabed: floes over the shallows cast shadows (`bFloeShadows`), whose seabed shows them through the water, and floes over the deep sea cast none. They are placed at the start and whenever a rebuild changed the sea field, on the main thread (Terra: about 3,200 floes, 2,000 of them over the shallows, in 5 ms, about 0.23 million triangles; close over the pack at 1280x720 on the machine it was made on they cost about 0.27 ms of GPU with all shadows and within the noise, under 0.1 ms, with the split), and all their values are `Floe*` of `DA_HexPlanet`. `M_HexPlanetFloe` of `create_planet_assets.py` colors their top, sides and what lies under the water, and moves them in its world position offset (`FLOE_OFFSET_CODE`): each drifts, turns back and forth, bobs and rocks at its own pace, so neighbors drift together and apart, which stands in for grinding; past `FloeMotionDistance` they stand still and past `FloeCullDistance` they are not drawn. The slabs and shards come from `generate_planet_kit.py` (`Floe`: a broken, stretched outline, a top of leaning facets, a wide ledge under the water, 1.3 of the outline, and a bottom). The shore has foam (`Shore*`, `Surf*`, `Runup*` and `Roller*` of `DA_HexPlanetWater`). `WATER_CODE` finds how deep the water is over the ground behind it from the scene depth, turned into the depth along the normal of the planet, so it follows the real coast and not the cells, and lays a film of foam at the edge, laced (`ShoreLace`) and never narrower than a few pixels of the change of the depth (`ShoreFoamPixels`), thinner from far away (`ShoreFar*`). Breakers come one by one along the coast: the coast is cut into stretches, one land cell each unless `ShoreStretchJoin` of `DA_HexPlanet` joins two, each breaking at its own phase, which the shore field gives (`ShoreField`, see the sea field), and `SHORE_WAVE_CODE`, shared by the water and `M_HexPlanet`, reads the wave of the stretch in front of a point: every `SurfPeriod` seconds a stretch sends one with `SurfChance`, now and then a roller (`RollerChance`, by `SHORE_HASH` of the wave and the stretch). The wave forms `SurfReach` from land, about one and a half cells, along the distance to the coast in alpha of the sea field (signed, so it goes on under the land), divided by how fast that grows (alpha of the shore field), as close to the shore the land cells around pull the soft minimum different ways and it grows slowly there, so a wave rushed in and swelled at the end; it rolls in at an even speed over `SurfRunShare` of the period as a thin line (`SurfWidthStart` to `SurfWidthEnd` world units, divided out of the distance by how fast it grows on screen, `surfGrad`) that grows from nothing to full white with a short tail of streaks, then over the last `SurfShoreDistance`, two fifths of a cell, speeds up to `SurfShoreSpeed` of its speed by the coast line, as the white water of a broken wave rushes in (a slowdown there, which `SurfShoreSpeed` below 1 still gives, looked wrong; the speed changes evenly with the distance, so `SHORE_WAVE_CODE` places the crest in closed form, `stretchCrest`, and gives back when it reaches a distance, `SHORE_CREST_TIME`), and at full strength rolls on at that pace to the real edge of the water, wherever that lies against the coast line of the field, and on under the land, whose ground hides it. It fades out towards the ends of its stretch (`SurfBorderStart`, `SurfBorderEnd`) and is at most `SurfLength`, a cell, long, however far the wedge of sea in front of its stretch fans out, and at its ends it starts `SurfCurl` further out and catches up by the shore zone, so it bends into a C. Just when it meets the edge of the water its water runs up the beach: `RUNUP_CODE` in `M_HexPlanet` (so `AHexPlanet` gives the surface materials `DA_HexPlanetWater`, the sea field and the shore field too, and `RUNUP_APPLY_CODE` lays its foam and wet sand on after the rivers, which keep them off out to `RiverRunupGap` past their banks, so at a mouth the foam does not roll up a river) finds, for a point of the beach, where the edge of the water lies below it, from the distance to the coast there plus its height over the tangent of the slope of the ground (the normal of the vertex, smooth across triangles), and from when the crest reached that edge the water runs up there fast over `RunupRiseTime` to `RunupHeight` above the sea, `RollerScale` higher for a roller, then slowly back over `RunupFallTime`, a rim of foam at its edge, the foam it left sliding down after it in thinning streaks, and the sand it wet, once the water has slid back off it, darker until it dries (`WetDarkening`; darkened under the sheet of water it looked like a shadow under the foam); timed alike, its foam slides back into the water over the edge (`BackwashDepth`). So the foam is born just where and when the wave meets the shore, whichever side of the coast line of the field the real shore lies; a start at one time for the whole stretch, when the crest crossed that line, was early or late by up to a second or two.
 Earlier tries taught what to avoid: fronts by depth covered the shallow shelf in blotches, as it is not much deeper than they ran; the noise of the texture of water stays blotchy even at a coarse mip; a time shift that changed along the coast faster than the phase changes across it made waves run along the coast or out to sea; a lag of the ends that changed faster than the distance turned arcs across the coast; and with one slow shift the whole coast broke as one wide front, hence the stretches. The breakers fade out where the lines of the distance wrap around a cape (`SurfCapeStart`, `SurfCapeEnd` against the green of the sea field), as there they would run across the coast. The lagoon colors and the lower waves near land (`LagoonDistance*`) also use that distance. The same depth puts foam on the wide ledge of each floe (`IceFoam`, `IceFoamWidth`), laced by the slush, and the shores of the frozen sea get the film and fewer breakers. On the machine it was made on it cost nothing measurable at 1280x720 (over a coast 3.40 to 3.67 ms of GPU between runs, 3.56 with the stretches, over the pack 3.96), and the sea field with the shore field takes about 0.27 s to build on Terra, 0.24 without the real coast and 0.16 without the stretches. The cliffs of the flat water are not there.
- The underground is a second `AHexPlanet` of the same frequency, whose cell i lies under surface cell i, seen from outside as a globe (the view from inside is not built; it is to hide its far side under a stone vault above the camera, see Not decided yet, and what this run of 2026-11-09 added does not hang on the camera being outside: what stands on the layer takes its up from `AHexPlanet::GetUpAt`, the stalactites hang from the lips of the passages and the motes follow the camera). `EHexPlanetLayer` (Surface, Underground, in `HexPlanetLayers.h`) names the layers and `UHexPlanetLayers`, a world subsystem, holds both planets and the one presented (`Present`, which hides the other first, so two sky lights are never in the scene, and `OnPresented`); every lookup of the planet goes through it, and console commands act on the layer shown, but `hex.PlanetShoreIce` and `hex.PlanetRiverShape` on the surface. The surface spawns the underground in its `BeginPlay` (`SpawnUnderground`, deferred, with `PlanetLayer`, `PlanetConfig` = `UndergroundConfig` (`DA_HexUnderground`) and `Material` = `UndergroundMaterial` (`M_HexUnderground`); `-ini:Engine:[ConsoleVariables]:hex.PlanetSpawnUnderground=0` leaves it out), which takes the folder and manifest of the surface, reads or generates its data in the background once the surface is built (`StartUndergroundData`, `FinishUndergroundData`: its own grid and `HexPlanetUnderground::Load`, 15 to 18 ms on Terra), and builds its meshes only when it is first shown, behind the black of a trip (`SetPresented`, about 100 ms and 2.9 million triangles on Terra), sharing the kit of the surface (`SurfaceKit`), so its `HexRadius` must be that of the surface (else it logs an error and takes it). `SetPresented(false)` hides the actor (`SetActorHiddenInGame`), frees the pieces of its chunks, its prefetch and its auroras, clears its outline (`SetHoveredCell`), as `CellMarks` of a hidden layer kept the last one, and a hidden layer's `Tick` only finishes rebuilds and streams, so only the layer shown writes `MPC_HexPlanet`, the sun and its LOD; a hidden one streams pieces only for a trip that comes to it (`SetPiecePrefetch`). `hex.PlanetLayerCheck [Cell]` logs what of the hidden layer is in the scene (nothing must be), the sky lights (one), the cells outlined in `CellMarks` of each layer (`AHexPlanet::CountOutlinedCells`, an error when the hidden one keeps any), the sun, the exposure and the MPC, and with a cell its hover text on each layer and a check of picking underground. Feature flags of `UHexPlanetConfig` (Features) leave out what the underground has not: `bSea` (water cap and grid, sea and shore fields, floes, river mouths; `CreateEmptySeaField` gives the materials 1x1 cubemaps of no sea), `bClouds`, `bHaze`, `bAuroras`, `bRivers`, and `bFog` puts `M_HexUndergroundFog` in place of the haze (`CreateFog`); `bGenerateObjects` is on underground since the cave objects (`UNDERGROUND_MIGRATIONS`); object sets stand on the layers of `UHexPlanetObjectSet::PlanetLayers`, Surface by default. `DA_HexUnderground` is a copy of `DA_HexPlanet` that `create_planet_assets.py` makes once (`UNDERGROUND_VALUES`: the features off, `EdgeRule` HigherHolds, `DeepDepth` 10, `ShallowDepth` 5 and no `CoastWobble`, so the voids lie 35 below the passages (225 until 2026-11-10, when the developer raised them to a little below the floor; the values measured down from the passages followed as defaults of the class: `AbyssMist*`, `AbyssFade*`, `AbyssBrightness`, `CaveRune*`, `CaveMossWallDepth`, `MoteHeightBelow`, `LavaCliffReach`, `VoidFloorHeight` and `WallOuterDepth`), the rise of the floor (`FloorNoise` 24) and a dim sky light, `SkyLightColor` and `SkyLightIntensity`), so a later change of `DA_HexPlanet` does not reach it; the sun of each layer is `FHexLayerLighting` of `DA_HexPlanetCamera` (`GetLighting`, the surface from its old values, `UndergroundLighting` for the underground, applied by `AHexPlanetCameraPawn::ApplyLighting`), for the underground a faint, cool key light since it went dark on 2026-11-10 (below). Its files are in `<planet>/underground/` (`HexPlanetFile::GetDataFolder`), while `PlanetFolder` stays the identity of the planet; `SaveLevels` and `SaveAs` write every layer that has its data (`GetLoadedLayers`, `SaveLayerFiles`, its relief read with the data so a layer never shown keeps `relief.json`) and `entrances.json`, `HasUnsavedChanges` takes the edits of both layers and the entrances, and the overlay says "Entrances not saved" when only they changed (`HasUnsavedCells`). On Terra at entrance 112 the underground took 2.9 ms of GPU where the surface took 3.9.
- `underground/levels.txt` has the layout of the surface's with the letters V (void, level -2) and P (passage, level 0) (`HexPlanetFile::GetLevelLetters`; encoding writes a level without a letter as the nearest), and the pentagon clamp is for the surface alone; `underground/biomes.txt` holds the `UndergroundLetter` of the biomes (`AHexPlanet::FindBiomes`): F `DA_Biome_CaveFloor` (the tan earth with pebbles of `OchreScree`, `CAVE_FLOOR_LAYER` of the script, basalt cliffs), A `DA_Biome_Abyss`, and the voids of lava L `DA_Biome_Lava` and of dark lakes K `DA_Biome_CaveLake`, whose `UHexBiomeConfig::VoidStyle` (`EHexVoidStyle`: Mist, Lava, Lake) says what the floor of a void holds, from `create_biome_assets.py underground`, which creates or resets only the biomes with an underground letter, as a full run saves every surface biome again. `HexPlanetUnderground::GenerateBiomes` puts F on the passages and A on the voids, and of the voids within `poolRings` of a passage, where they can be seen, and further than `poolExitRings` from every exit (the candidates) makes `lavaShare` lava and then `lakeShare` lakes, each the highest of a noise of patches about `poolCells` across, logged with their patches and the middle of the largest (`FBiomeReport`; Terra 104 cells of lava in 21 patches and 208 of lakes in 33); the painter keeps the biome of a cell whose level did not change, and a void painted takes the biome most of its neighbors have. `entrances.json` at the root of a planet holds a sorted line per entrance, `{"cell": 1234, "network": "A"}`, networks A to H (`FHexEntrance`, `HexPlanetFile::MaxNetworks`); its exit is the same cell below. An entrance can be used only on plains or hills of the surface, never on a pentagon, over a passage (`HexPlanetUnderground::GetEntranceProblem`); one that cannot is kept in memory, marked red and not travelled through, and a save leaves it out (`GetSavedEntrances`) and says so on the screen and in the log (`AHexPlanet::ReportLeftOutEntrances`), so a new entrance must have its exit carved before the save. Since 2026-11-12 an entrance is built into a mountain and its exit into a wall of the underground, as the developer decided ("від тепер перехід можна ставити тільки на гору чи стіна в підземеллі", "вхід ставиться з гекса прохідного до гори чи стіни - тобто задаєм напрямок"): its record stays the passable cell before the mouth, the same cell on both layers, and the way its mouth opens (`Side`, `ExitSide`, by hand or chosen by the planet) says which neighbor it is built into, the one behind it, a mountain on the surface and a void underground. So besides the above an entrance can be used only with a mountain behind it (`EEntranceProblem::NoMountain`) and a void behind its exit (`NoWall`) into which no exit before it in the list is built (`SharedWall`: a mountain may hold several mouths, from the cells around it, a wall one); `AHexPlanet::GetEntranceProblem` adds these three, as it knows how each stands (`GetEntranceSeat`), while a save keeps such an entrance. One without them shows as a free rock in `ProblemColor`. The cell before the mouth is ground as any other: heroes walk over it and stand in its middle, and nothing happens there. A hero goes through by a click on the mouth, the mountain or the void it is built into (`AHexPlanetPlayerController::FindMouthUnderCursor`, which of several mouths of one mountain is nearest the cursor; `hex.Hero through <Cell>`), which points it to the same cell of the other layer, so its way is found as any way through an entrance: to the cell before the mouth and through, for `EntranceCost`, a point of movement again, so a hero with none left stays; a hero that stands before the mouth on the other side blocks it, as one cell holds one hero. The ground comes up to the mouth evenly, as the developer asked ("земля до переходів підходить плавно без крутого підтягувааня як зараз працює для гір"): the side between the cell of an entrance and its mountain is a low side (`HexPlanetSurface::FParams::LowSides`, `HexPlanetKit::GetSideKey`, from `AHexPlanet::FindEntranceSides`), which lies at the top of the lower of its two cells with both its corners, whatever the edge rule (`FindCornerHeights`, `BuildFrame`), so the plains stay flat up to it and the mountain carries the whole slope; a rebuild takes the sides of then and builds the cells of a side that came or went again (`StartRebuild`, `UpdateEntranceSides`), and the rock of the entrance stands `EmbedShift` (0.72, 0.82 at first) radii of a cell towards the mountain, so its mouth is a little before that side and the rest of it in the mountain: at 0.82 the glowing end of its mouth lay behind the side, where a mountain raised for it already rises, and the developer saw the mesh of the mountain in the arch in place of the glow. The key that sends a hero no longer takes one through, as it never stands in the passage itself. A hero that goes through walks into the mouth and out of it on the other side, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12, when it vanished on the cell before it ("герой має направитись до проходу а не зникати на клітинці перед так само на виході - має вийти (може погратись скейлом?)"): where the person sees it, the step through first glides it from the middle of its cell to the mouth (`AHexPlanet::GetEntranceMouth`: before the glowing end of an arch in a mountain, `EmbedMouthPlace`, 0.64 radii of a cell from the middle, or at the face of the wall around a niche) over `EntranceWalkTime` (0.55 s; 0.7 at first) of `DA_HexHeroes`, growing smaller down to `EntranceShrink` (0.75; 0.3 at first, which the developer found too small) of its size and dissolving in the glow, as they asked ("може вийде розчиняти при проході через світіння"): from `EntranceFadeStart` (0.1; 0.3 at first, after which the developer asked for the effect to show a little sooner) of that walk on it goes in grains of the world `HeroFadeGrain` across, those about to go glowing `HeroFadeGlow` in the color of the network (`FHexHero::PassFade`, `GlideFade`, `PassColor`, `AHexPlanet::GetEntranceColor`; `HeroFade` and `HeroFadeColor` of `M_HexPlanetHero`, `HERO_GRAIN_CODE` and `HERO_MASK_CODE` of `create_planet_assets.py`, so the material of every hero is masked now, whose cost is not measured), and it comes together again as it walks out on the other side (`FHexHero::bEnteringMouth`, `PassScale`, `GlideScaleFrom`, `GlideScaleTo`, in `UHexHeroes::StartNextStep`, the glide of the gates of castles, `PlaceHero`), then it goes through (`PassEntrance`) and waits small in the mouth on the other layer until the camera is there, `EntranceOutDelay` after the screen is no longer black, and walks out to the middle of the cell as it grows again; one out of sight goes through at once. While the tool of the entrances is chosen an arrow in the color of the network shows where a click would put one, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 ("малюй стрілку підказку куди стане і напрямок куди вихід буде"): on the cell before the mouth, from the side the mouth would be built at to the middle of that cell, the way the mouth opens, while the outline of the cursor is the cell that is or becomes the mountain (`UHexPlanetPainter::FindEntrancePlace`, `UpdateEntranceHint` from the controller every frame, drawn in the mesh of the tiles, `UpdatePreview`; its sizes are constants there). Turn and Remove take an entrance by its own cell or by the mountain or void its mouth is built into, of several the one nearest the cursor, and on a mountain or void with none built in the entrance on a cell beside it (`FindEntranceCellAt`), as the developer could neither turn nor take away one by its mountain. The palette puts an entrance on a mountain, as the developer asked the same day ("переходи тепер окремо не ставимо - тільки на горі - якщо я пробую поставити перехід на пустоту - це автоматично добудує стіну чи гору під неї"): the cell clicked is the mountain its mouth is built into, raised by the same step of Undo where it is plains or hills, and the entrance stands on the cell of plains or hills beside it that is nearest the cursor, its exit opening the same way, so its wall lies under the mountain (`UHexPlanetPainter::PaintEntrance`); the wall itself, a void under the mountain, is made at once in the same step, which keeps the cells of the underground it changed apart from those of its own layer (`FHexPaintStep::OldCaveLevels`, `NewCaveLevels` and their biomes, `UHexPlanetPainter::ApplyCave`), so Undo takes it back with the entrance, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 (until then it was made outside the steps of Undo) (`AHexPlanet::FitEntrances` for that cell, without its mountains, `bMountains` false, giving what it changed through `OutOldCaveLevels`: raised there too, the mountain left the tile of the stroke standing as a grey hex, as the stroke then changed nothing and nothing was built again to take the tile away). An entrance put where no passage lies under it cannot be used until one is carved, and shows as a free rock in `ProblemColor`. `AHexPlanet::FitEntrances` (`hex.PlanetFitEntrances`) gives every entrance what it is built into, for the planets saved before ("на старих картах збудуй нові проходи") and for entrances the generation placed, which get their mountains when the underground has its data: a neighbor of its cell becomes a mountain where none stands behind it, the one behind the way it was turned by hand, else the one most to the north that may (nothing stands on it, no entrance is before it, the land around it still reaches each other), and a neighbor of its exit a void, under the mountain where it can be; in the middle of a hall the void grows away from the exit, every passage behind it that it left one cell wide becoming void too, a wedge out to the edge of the hall, while the passages around still reach each other, and an exit for which no such wedge is found stays without a wall. Terra, Islet and Small were fitted and saved on 2026-11-12: Islet 5 of 5 entrances and Small its one can be used, Terra 31 of 32, as one found no cell to raise. The maze keeps a wall for each exit: one neighbor of its cell stays void whatever is carved (`FMaze::KeepWalls`, `Kept`: the one behind the way it was turned, else one by a hash of the cell), given up only where it would leave a passage one cell wide that nothing else can widen; the entrances the generation places are not put beside mountains, which an earlier try did at the cost of networks that could not be joined; `FitEntrances` raises a mountain behind each instead. The `underground` block of `planet.json` (`FHexUndergroundGeneration`) holds the generation, every field with a default, and `HexPlanetUnderground::Generate`, pure, deterministic and safe on any thread, places the entrances by farthest-point sampling on plains and hills (`entranceCount`, 0 for one per `cellsPerEntrance` cells) and groups nearby ones into networks (`networkCount`, `min`/`maxEntrancesPerNetwork`), carves a hall of `hallRings` around each exit (`hallEdgeShare` of its outer ring), joins each network by a Prim tree of cheapest ways whose cost follows the winding lines where a noise crosses zero (`wiggle`, `wiggleCells`, `wiggleSharpness`), kept `networkGap` cells from other networks (entering that zone at `gapPenalty` a cell only when there is no other way, never next to one), widens them to `passageWidth`, at least 2, so no passage has void on both neighbors K and K + 3 (`IsNeck`; for a pentagon two sides not next to each other), and adds `deadEnds` branches (`deadEndLength`, `deadEndWander`, `deadEndTurn`); `seedOffset` changes the maze and keeps the surface. `Check` gives the `FReport`: each network's pieces and cells, joined networks, exits in the void, necks, passages no exit reaches, and the network of each cell (`CellNetworks`), checked again when read after the levels of either layer changed (`GetUndergroundReport`). `Load` reads the files and generates and writes what is missing at once, as the surface does. `hex.PlanetUnderground <Folder|all> [maze|entrances|objects|biomes] [image]` does the same without a world, so from the editor's `-ExecCmds` ended by `QUIT_EDITOR`, generating the maze or also the entrances again, `biomes` generating `underground/biomes.txt` alone again (`FLoadOptions::bNewBiomes`, leaving `levels.txt` and `entrances.json` byte for byte), `objects` generating `underground/objects.json` again (which `maze` and `entrances` do too, as a new maze needs new objects, and which is written when missing, as a first show of the underground writes it; `maze` and `entrances` also delete `underground/walls.json`, the heights of the walls set by hand for the old maze), and `image` writes a map to `Saved/Underground/<Folder>.png`; `all` skips a folder without `levels.txt`, such as `New_planet` and `water_only_large`, which hold only `planet.json`, and logs it; a folder named explicitly is still generated. The `HexHero.Planet.Underground` tests check the files, the seed, the networks, the entrances, the necks, loading, the editing functions and the styles of the voids (`VoidStyles`).
- The underground holds its passages as flat ledges over the voids through the edge rule (`EHexEdgeRule` of `UHexPlanetConfig`, passed to the build in `HexPlanetSurface::FParams`): Shared, of the surface, lays a corner at the mean of its three tops and a side halfway between its two, while HigherHolds lays a corner at the highest of its three tops and a side at the higher of its two, so a passage stays flat to its sides and a void carries its whole cliff, `DeepDepth` deep, in its own cell; it reaches the corners, `BuildFrame`, every build of a cell, the coarse fan, the boulders and `BuildCellTriangles`, and the `Seams` and `Surface.Rebuild` tests check both rules. The floor of the passages rises by up to `FloorNoise` (24 underground, 0 on the surface, as the developer wanted a cave dug and trodden unevenly), a noise of the place `FloorNoiseScale` across with `FloorNoiseDetail` of one finer, which the cells that share a point see alike (`HexPlanetKit::GetFloorRise`, applied in `PlaceVertex` through `FPlacement::FloorRise`, all of it on the top of level 0 and fading out over `FloorNoiseFade` below it), so the unevenness runs on across the sides of the cells and is no bump in each, as the developer asked on 2026-11-10; with it the cells of level 0 and the pentagons are built as a lattice of 6 steps from their center to their sides (`AddLattice` of `HexPlanetSurface.cpp`, 216 triangles a hexagon where the flat piece of the kit has 24), as fine as the slopes of the kits, so their sides hold the points of their neighbors and no crack opens where it rises (the `Surface.Rebuild` test), the walls and the rims on their foot stand on it (`HexPlanetWalls::GetFloorRadius`), and picking reaches `FloorNoise` above the tops. It only rises, so the floor stays on the top of its level for the material. Picking on a layer without the sea tests the triangles of each cell along the ray (`FindHeldCellAlongRay`, `PickTriangles`, dropped with each build shown), as a cliff belongs to the void below it, and `FindGroundRadius` gives the ground of a cell in a direction. `M_HexUnderground` and its masked `M_HexUnderground_Cut` (`UNDERGROUND_CODE`) draw the biome layers without beach, sea, rivers, run-up, canopy or cloud shadows, the grid lines on passages only (`AbyssGridFade`) and the outlines on voids too; on the tops of the passages (within `CaveFloorTopDepth`) the floor takes `CaveFloorTint` and pools of tone (`CaveFloorPools`), its hollows a little darker and its swells lighter by `CaveFloorRiseShade` where the floor rises (`FloorNoise`, the height of a pixel above the top of the passages over it, which the foot of the walls takes too, and lumps too small for the lattice bend its normal by the slope of a noise `CaveFloorBumpScale` across, `CaveFloorBumps` strong, but not on the stone of a flagstone). The floor is not one ground nor one tone (`CAVE_FLOOR_CODE` of the script, which `M_HexUndergroundWall` puts on the floor at the foot of a wall too, so they meet without a seam, as the developer found it monotone on 2026-11-10): it turns warmer or cooler in regions (`CaveFloorHue`, `CaveFloorWarmColor`, `CaveFloorCoolColor`), `CaveFloorAltShare` of it in patches is packed earth, the layer `CaveFloorAltLayerName` (RedScree) times `CaveFloorAltColor`, the heights of both layers deciding their edge (`CaveFloorAltRagged`), and old flagstones lie here and there, as the developer asked for stone slabs covered with sand and cracked with age: a cellular pattern of the place `CaveSlabSize` across in patches over `CaveSlabShare` of the floor, each slab the layer `CaveSlabLayerName` (GrayRock) at a place of its own times `CaveSlabColor`, lighter or darker, dusted by the floor (`CaveSlabDust`), leaning its own way, with joints of dirt and worn edges along the floor to the nearest of the planes halfway to the points around it, some gone (`CaveSlabMissing`) and some cracked (`CaveSlabCracked`), under sand that drifts in a noise, gathers in the hollows of the floor and buries a patch towards its edge (`CaveSlabSand*`); a value noise keeps near its middle, so each noise of it is spread over about 0 to 1 by 2.6 around 0.5, where a share is about that share of the floor. `AHexPlanet::SetBiomeLayers` and `UpdateWalls` give both materials `CaveFloorAltLayer` and `CaveSlabLayer`, -1 when the layer is not found. At Small 830 on the machine it was made on at 1280x720 it cost about 0.1 ms of GPU (2.93 / 2.83 ms with and without it), mostly for the samples of the two more layers, small dark pools of water lie out of the cells with objects or of an exit (bit 6 of the alpha of the second biome pixel) and `CavePuddleRimGap` of the radius away from the rims and from the sides towards such a cell (bit K of green of the first biome pixel says that of neighbor K, written underground by `UpdateCanopy`, as green, the rock layer, is read by no material, and kept by `WriteBiomePixels`), so no side cuts one: towards them the water sinks by `CavePuddleRimDepth` with the square of how near it is, so a puddle there shrinks with a round edge (until 2026-11-10 a steep fall over 0.3 of the radius cut one along a straight line), gathering in the hollows of the floor (`CavePuddleHollow`) (`CavePuddle*`, a flat normal, a faint sheen that does not depend on the view, the wet earth showing at a soft shore and deepening to `CavePuddleColor` over `CavePuddleDepth`, a low `CavePuddleSpecular` and a roughness above the 0.4 to which Lumen traces reflections, so they read as dark water and not as holes or chrome, and no grid line and no pool of light on them; the paths of flagstones down the corridors, `CaveFlag*`, were taken out on 2026-11-10, as the developer found their texture odd, and the flagstones of `CAVE_FLOOR_CODE` came in their place the same day), green glowing moss grows along the rims and down the top `CaveMossWallDepth` of the cliffs (`CaveMoss*`, speckles that fade to `CaveMossFarGlow` as a pixel grows, so they never shimmer), and pools of light lie under what glows, beside lava and at the exits (`GlowPool*`, `CellCanopy`, which the underground fills with the glow of each cell in `AHexPlanet::UpdateCanopy`: `UHexPlanetObjects::GetCellGlow`, the `GlowPool` of a set by density, `LavaPool` of a lava void in `LavaLightColor`, or `ExitPoolStrength` on the cell of an exit that can be used and `ExitPoolRingShare` of it on the passages around, in `ExitPoolColor` `ExitPoolNetworkShare` of the way to the color of its network, blended `GlowPoolBlend` of the way to the mean of its glowing neighbors, and in a cell that does not glow the mean of the colors of its glowing neighbors by their strengths): each glowing cell lights the floor around its middle out to `GlowPoolReach` (1.55) of the cell radius, a smooth bump times its strength, and the light of neighboring glowing cells adds up to at most 1, nearly even over a patch at that reach, so a patch is one pool with a round edge (until 2026-11-10 each cell was lit evenly and fell off at its sides, which in the dark read as pale hexagons); a cell holds its strength in fifteenths in the alpha of `CellCanopy` and those of its neighbors in `CellGlowNear` (four bits each), so both cells of a side add up the same light there and two of different strengths meet without a step, and `GlowPoolNoise` of the cell radius moves the edge; the pool lights the floor by its albedo times `GlowPoolStrength` and `GlowPoolLight` of it whatever the albedo, so a grove turns cyan, a cavern teal and the passage beside lava orange (tuned again in the dark on 2026-11-10 to 0.4 and 0.08, where 0.8 and 0.2 drew pale plates); a few cliffs of voids below a passage carry carved runes (`CaveRune*`, `CAVE_RUNES_CODE`, a hexagon and two or three strokes of one of eight runes, by a hash of the cell and side, faded out from `CaveRuneFadeStart` world units a pixel); the rock darkens down the cliffs (`AbyssFadeStart`, `AbyssFadeEnd`, `AbyssDarkness`), and the floor of a void gives way (`AbyssFloorHide`, and its specular, so the uniform sky light lays no grey sheen on the dark) to an emissive teal grey mist of noise taken in `AbyssSlices` slices `AbyssSliceDepth` apart under it along the view (at most 1 / `AbyssViewFloor` of that), each drifting faster (`AbyssSliceDrift`), laid front to back, each as thick as its noise lies above `AbyssMistThreshold` (by `AbyssContrast`) times `AbyssSliceOpacity`, lit in `AbyssColor` times `AbyssBrightness` and keeping 2 to the power of -depth / `AbyssDepthHalf` of its light, over the nearly black `AbyssBottomColor`, so the deeper slices are darker and hidden by those above and a void reads as deep and nearly black (lum 8 to 12 of 255 on 2026-11-09, the floor 106; since the voids rose to 35 below the passages on 2026-11-10 the mist alone draws the depth of the abyss, under a floor the walls hide from the passages); far away, past `CoarseDistance` - `CoarseBand`, a void beside passages, which its coarse fan draws as a shallow cone, is shaded as deep as its cliff within `AbyssCliffBand` of its sides (`AbyssFarCliff`; `hex.PlanetCoarseDistance` reaches it too). The blue of the first biome pixel underground holds the style of a void in bits 6 and 7 and in bit K whether neighbor K is a void of another style (`AHexPlanet::WriteBiomePixels`, never the mountain bits there), by which a lava void glows in the cracks of a dark crust (`Lava*`: the low parts of a flowing noise below `LavaCrustShare` with a finer octave `LavaCrustDetail`, `LavaCrustDetailScale`, `LavaCrustDetailDrift`, red at the crust and brighter and yellower far from it, `LavaCoreShare`, `LavaCoreColor`, `LavaCoreBrightness`, at a `LavaBrightness` of 0.6 so the red does not clip, breathing, and fading to `LavaFarGlow` as a pixel grows from `LavaDetailFadeStart` to `LavaDetailFadeEnd` of a plate, so far away it glows as a pool and does not speckle; the noise is `FCaveNoise`, hashed in integers, as the float hash of `NOISE_CODE` jumps a little on some planes of its lattice, which the sharp edges of the cracks showed as short straight seams) and lights its cliffs by their rock in `LavaLightColor` (`LavaCliffLight`, `LavaCliffGlow`, up to `LavaCliffReach`, the rock darkened as down any cliff but for `LavaCliffDaylight` and tinted `LavaCliffTint` of that color, so they read lit orange and not pink), a lake is dark still water, lighter towards `LakeShoreColor` within `LakeShoreReach` of its shore, with a sheen that does not hang on a grazing view and a specular of its own (`Lake*`, about 20 of 255 where the void around it is 5), both lie within `VoidFloorHeight` of the floor and fade to the mist over `VoidStyleEdge` of a side towards a void of another style from a little inside it, moved by up to twice `VoidStyleEdgeNoise` (`VoidStyleEdgeNoiseScale`) and nothing of them on the side itself, so they do not end on the straight sides of their cells; and a faint lip lights the edges of the passages (`AbyssLip*`). `M_HexUndergroundFog`, a post process in the unused `HazeVolume`, darkens the scene with the length of the view and fills the chasms with mist below `FogMistTop` (`Fog*`, `FogMistMax` 0.15 since the abyss draws its depth itself), and thickens away from the hero (`FogFocus`, which `AHexPlanet::UpdateHeroLight` gives, clear within `FogFocusStart` and `FogFocusMax` from `FogFocusEnd` times the reach of the light of the hero). The markers of entrances (`AHexPlanet::UpdateMarkers`, `MarkerComponents`, on both layers whenever the entrances or levels change) are rocks with a cave mouth, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 ("має більше виглядати як гора з сталактітами і входом", with a few versions of the entrance and of the exit, modelled, textured and darker in the corners of their geometry; a cave mouth and a ring of standing stones until 2026-11-10, then a pit with a rim on the surface and stairs up a rock underground): a mound of rock that stands in the back of its cell with its mouth towards the middle, stalactites hanging in the mouth, stalagmites and fallen stones around it and crystals that glow in the color of the network beside it, and underground the same with stairs of worked stone up into a mouth out of which the day falls, under a shaft of daylight before it (`SM_PlanetExitShaft`, `ShaftPlace` radii of a cell from the middle, `ShaftColor` with `ShaftNetworkTint` of the color of the network). The versions are `EntranceMeshes` of the config of the surface (`SM_PlanetEntranceCrag`, a high crag, `Maw`, a low broad rock with teeth from above and below, `Gate`, with a doorway of two standing boulders and one laid across them with a crystal on it (straight beams of worked stone at first, which the developer asked away on every entrance, with their straight steps, now flat rough stones: "прибрати балки та замінити чимось природнішим або з валунів"), and, as they asked the same day for some that open to two or three sides, `Twin` and `Trident`) and `ExitMeshes` of that of the underground (`SM_PlanetExitCave`, `ExitGate`, `ExitTwin`), one for each entrance by a hash of its cell, a component of instances for each. The mouth, +X of a mesh, is turned to the open side of the cell (`AHexPlanet::GetEntranceSeat`: towards a neighbor a hero can stand on by its level, to the south where it can, as the camera of the game looks north unless it is turned, with a cell nobody stands on behind it if it can be), and the hero stands before it, `EntranceHeroPlace` (0.3) radii of a cell from the middle towards that side (`GetEntrancePlace`, which `UHexHeroes::GetStandDirection` gives; it stood beside the pit on the side it came from before); a hero may still come onto the cell from any side, so one that comes from behind walks through the rock. An entrance beside a mountain is built into it, and an exit beside a void into the wall along it, as the developer asked on 2026-11-12 ("вбудовувати вхід у підземелля у скелі", "вихід з підземелля у стіни підземелля"; `bEmbedEntrances` of the config of the layer, `AHexPlanet::GetEntranceSeat`): of the neighbors that are mountains, or voids, it takes the one whose far side is open and lies most to the south, so the mouth, which opens away from it, faces the camera, while the cell has a neighbor a hero can stand on; it is then of the rock layer of that cell, and the hero stands `EmbedHeroPlace` radii from the middle towards the mouth, in the middle by default. A free rock pushed against a mountain read as a dome put beside it, and against a wall its tone did not fit ("воно не зливається геометрією ... має зливатись, або більше ніж на половину купол має бути у горі", "ідеально мати такий самий стиль як і стін - зверху нависає край"), so the versions built in are shapes of their own. On the surface `EmbeddedEntranceMeshes` are arches in a spur of the mountain (`SM_PlanetEntranceSpur`, `SpurWide`, `spur` of the generator, a `Mound` with a `tail`, the rock behind its middle so many times longer, and a `tail_rise`, higher towards its back; the mouth of every rock is a shallow recess `DEPTH` (0.14) deep that follows its face and ends in a glowing cap, closed down to the ground (a mouth that opens at the ground has no wall under it, and without that edge the fan of the cap lacked its lowest triangle, through which the developer saw the mountain), as through a deeper one the ground of the mountain rising inside the hollow rock showed in place of the dark, which the developer asked to close on 2026-11-12, and the arch in a niche of a wall has a slab of dark rock behind and around it), as the developer asked after two tries that did not please, a hollow that lay on the slope and then stood nearly upright as a round drum ("вхід має бути у формі арки - не круга - тобто тропа з землі входить в арку - геомотрія далі має бути вписана в гору"): a path of flat stones runs from the ground into the arch (`add_path`), stalactites hang in it and its depth glows, and the rock stands `EmbedShift` (0.45) radii of a cell from the middle towards the mountain, so its tail runs into the mountain and rises with it. Its face leans back (`lean`) and the arch reaches high up it, so the camera, which looks down, sees into it, as the developer asked ("трохи привідкрий арку зверху ... не занадто щоб не виглядало як спуск у погріб"). The material draws the rock at the size and in the tint of its layer (`LayerParams`, which `SetBiomeLayers` gives it), and `MarkerRockLight` of `DA_HexPlanet` is 0.05, as the light of the color of the network on the rock (0.6 by default, still so underground) tinted it away from the mountain ("відрізняється по кольору від гори"). Underground the wall itself has a niche for the exit, as the developer chose when a buttress set before the wall, tried first, was still a block of its own ("геометрично суцільне"): `AHexPlanet::UpdateWalls` gives `HexPlanetWalls::Build` the exits built in, each its passage and its point on the line of the walls, the middle of a side or a corner of the cell (`FNiche`), and around the sample of the line along that cell nearest the point the inner face and its foot stand back into the wall by `WallNicheDepth` (48, at most most of the thickness of the wall) over `WallNicheWidth` (80) along the line, under an arch `WallNicheHeight` (135) high, its sides eased (`FSample::Niche`, `NicheTop`, applied in `GetLocal` to the rows of the inner face and the foot, which lie in its shade and lose their stalactites there), so the lip of the wall overhangs it as anywhere and the floor runs into it; only where the wall is at least `NicheMinHeight` of its height, and `HashChunk` takes it, so turning an exit builds again only the chunks it changes (`SetEntrances` builds the walls again). In the niche stand `EmbeddedExitMeshes` (`SM_PlanetExitNiche`, `ExitNicheCrystal`, the class `Doorway`): steps of flat rough stones up to the back of the niche, where an arch full of daylight is the way up (it stands 0.6 of a unit behind the origin of the mesh, before the back of the niche, which the wall has `WallNicheDepth` behind its face there, flat over the inner 0.8 of its width; at 1.05 the arch lay in the wall, which hid most of it, as the developer found on 2026-11-12), stalagmites beside it and stalactites over it, in one version with crystals; blocks of worked stone and a doorway of them, built first, looked square to the developer ("прибери брили квадратні на вході - має бути більш природнє"). It stands at the point of the niche (`FHexEntranceSeat::Anchor`), with the shaft of daylight `EmbedShaftPlace` radii from the middle of the cell. The developer could not tell it was an exit at first ("взагалі не видно геометрично що це вихід - не бачи діри, і це світіння стовп - також або не там - або лишнє"): over a niche the wall now has no overhang, stalactites or canopy, which hid it from above (`Open` of `GetLocal`), the arch glows in the color of the network too, and an exit in a wall has no shaft of daylight. A niche flush with the wall still did not show from every side ("вхід залився стінами - не видно - вхид має виступати в гекс ... стіни мають повернути так щоб стикуватись з ним"), so around it the wall stands out onto the passage: its inner face, its foot and the floor before it by `WallNicheBulge` (45), easing back to the line over `WallNicheShoulder` (40) beyond each side of the niche (`FSample::NicheOut`), and what stands in the niche stands as far out, so the exit stands out into its cell and the walls turn to meet it. Underground the rock of every exit is `MarkerWallTone` (0.55) of `CaveWallColor`, which `UpdateMarkers` gives the material as `MarkerRockTint`, so it is as light as the walls. The glow of the network fills the depth of every mouth of an entrance, so the way down shows from afar.
